**Scope.** All work in `avry-user-dashboard` + `vps-bridge` (dashboard repo pushed to `aivory-hub/main` at `bc459a3`; bridge mirror in the parent repo at `b89281a` + `b55c2eb`; everything deployed live and verified end-to-end through production). Three user-reported fronts.

**1. The 3 remaining deep-diagnostic PDF bugs from the 2026-08-25 ROI round.**
- *Garbled/overlapping glyphs* (regular-weight Manrope garbled, bold crisp, across improvement before/after boxes, executive-insight callouts, CTA panel, closing note, footers): exhaustively NOT reproducible — the exact failing document was rebuilt three ways (current code, the pre-fix `a902b59` deploy via a git worktree, and both with the user's REAL stored Bastion context+LLM result pulled from `dashboard.diagnostic_contexts` in prod) in a real headless Chromium through the real app code path, plus repeat downloads in one tab — all clean. Conclusion: environment-specific on the user's browser at generation time (stale chunk mix or a corrupt font fetch). Shipped defense-in-depth in `lib/pdfExport.ts`: TTF magic-byte+size validation before embed (corrupt fetch → clean Helvetica fallback instead of a corrupt embedded font), `?v=` cache-busting on font URLs, and jsPDF TTF **subsetting disabled** (`subset.encode` patched to embed the full original font — the subsetter was the one component that rewrites glyph tables/GIDs; +~310KB per PDF). `pdffonts` confirms Manrope×2+Doto embedded with `sub=no`; every previously-affected block renders clean. **User still owes one fresh-PDF re-download to confirm on their side.**
- *TOC page numbers "inconsistent"*: audited with the real data — every entry matches the actual landing page (checked both code versions). No code bug; no change.
- *Konteks Bisnis industry vs calculation mismatch*: the 2026-08-25 map fixed EN option strings, but **ID-locale answers** ('Jasa Profesional / Konsultasi' etc.) missed both `INDUSTRY_HOURLY_RATE_USD` and `INDUSTRY_BENCHMARKS` (EN keys only) and silently fell to the $30 default. Fixed with `INDUSTRY_KEY_ALIASES` + `normalizeIndustryKey()` used by both lookups; 5 new rate-parity tests (51/51 in the ROI suite). Note: the stored Bastion row currently holds `industry: 'Technology / Software'` (from a re-run) — if Bastion is really consulting, re-answer the industry question on the next diagnostic run.

**2. Workflow copilot latency — a bare "Halo selamat siang" took ~40s.**
- Root cause chain: every message, including greetings, paid a full bridge→OpenRouter round on the reasoning model (measured 6.6–27.8s for clarify; generate hit 60s timeouts — the recurring `[handleWorkflowCopilotDirect] error: timeout` log entries), plus retry chains.
- Fix A (dashboard, `bc459a3`): deterministic `matchSmallTalk()` fast-path — greetings/thanks/bye/capability questions answered locally with zero LLM calls in IDLE and CLARIFYING; deliberately conservative (whole message must be greeting/politeness tokens, so "halo, buatkan workflow invoice" still generates). 33 unit tests.
- Fix B (bridge, after the user rejected gemini and challenged the diagnosis — **and was right**): the slowness was never deepseek-v4-flash-0731 itself. Controlled A/B on identical prompts isolated it to **OpenRouter's DEFAULT provider routing** handing deepseek to slow endpoints (~12–25 tok/s decode: clarify 6.6–27.8s, generate 51–57s or 60s-timeout, persisting even with reasoning fully disabled). `provider: { sort: 'throughput' }` routes the same model at ~280 tok/s (Wafer/AtlasCloud). The state machine/routing/stepping overhead was measured at <1s total (0.7s direct vs 1.3s end-to-end) — not a factor. deepseek stays (user's preference), with `reasoning: { enabled: false }` for the mechanical clarify/generate/edit tasks; `workflow_semantic_review` deliberately keeps CONSOLE_MODEL with reasoning (its reviewer prompt degrades to lazy `'[]'` without it). An interim gemini-2.5-flash default was shipped and then replaced same-day by the deepseek+throughput fix.
- Live-verified through the production dashboard API: greeting 0.25–0.4s, clarify ~2.4s, generate ~3.5s with a valid 5-step workflow.

**3. Blueprint generation cold starts.** Same two-part treatment: frugal warm pings mirroring the existing CONSOLE_MODEL pattern (2-word prompt, `max_tokens: 1` — primary deepseek every 4 min matching the existing cadence, qwen failovers every 45 min; ~3 tokens/ping, well under $0.01/month combined), plus `provider: { sort: 'throughput' }` in `blueprintQueue.js`'s `callModel` (same slow-default-provider root cause as the copilot). Startup logs one `[warm-ping] armed` line; `vps-bridge` + `diag-worker` both restarted green. User confirmed visibly faster on the next real run.

**Rollbacks.** Dashboard: revert `bc459a3`. Bridge: `server.js.bak-pre-clarify-model-20260826`, `.bak-pre-workflow-model-20260826`, `.bak-pre-deepseek-throughput-20260826`, `.bak-pre-blueprint-warm-20260826`, and `lib/blueprintQueue.js.bak-pre-blueprint-warm-20260826` on the VPS.

## 2026-08-25 — Console approve/deny UI shipped; found the dashboard Approvals page has never actually worked

**Context.** Following up on discovering the AI Console's "YOUR AGENTS" picker was real (not a mockup — proven live the same day: it calls `/api/v1/telegram/agent-chat` → `route_console_message` → the same gateway path real Telegram traffic uses → confirmed via `vps-bridge` logs explicitly routing to Cerveau), the user asked for pending F-1 approvals to be resolvable inline in the Console instead of only from the dashboard's `/approvals` page.

**Built.** `route_console_message()`/`/api/v1/telegram/agent-chat` now return the full `pending_approval` object instead of discarding it. Frontend: `sendAgentMessage()` returns `{reply, pendingApproval}`; `useChat.ts` stores it on the assistant message and adds `resolveConsoleApproval()`, which calls the existing `/api/v1/agent-approvals/{id}/resolve` endpoint (the same one the dashboard page uses) and appends Cerveau's durable-resume reply as a new assistant turn on success. `ChatMessage.tsx` renders an inline Approve/Deny card (`ApprovalCard`), reusing a `describeTool()` helper moved into `lib/agentApprovals.ts` so the dashboard Approvals page and the Console share one implementation instead of two.

**Two real, previously-undiscovered bugs found while live-testing this (not simulated — a real pending approval created via a real Cerveau turn, at every step):**

1. **`CERVEAU_WEBHOOK_SECRET` was never passed into the `avry-backend` container.** `agent_approvals.py` (built earlier this session) reads it via `os.getenv`, but `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `avry-backend` service never listed it in `environment:` — every call failed closed with "Cerveau webhook secret not configured." Fixed by adding the passthrough to the compose file directly on the VPS (`.bak-pre-cerveau-webhook-secret-20260825` kept).
2. **`agent_approvals.py`'s `_CERVEAU_BASES` defaulted to `http://127.0.0.1:3100/3101`** — correct for the native systemd scripts (`approval-slack-notify.py`) but wrong for this route, which runs *inside* the `avry-backend` Docker container, where `127.0.0.1` is the container's own loopback, not the host's. Every list/resolve call silently failed on both bases (caught, logged as a warning, swallowed) — confirmed live: `docker exec avry-backend curl 127.0.0.1:3100` → unreachable, `curl host.docker.internal:3100` → 200. Fixed the default to `host.docker.internal`; the existing `CERVEAU_APPROVAL_BASE_1/_2` env override still works for any deployment where that name doesn't resolve.
3. **`httpx.Response` has no `.ok` attribute** (that's a `requests`-library idiom) — `_resolve_on()`'s `if not res.ok:` raised `AttributeError` on every single call, immediately *after* Cerveau had already processed the resolve successfully (confirmed via `avry-backend`'s own logs: `POST .../resolve "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"` followed one line later by the crash). The outer loop's generic `except Exception: continue` swallowed it and moved to the next candidate, which correctly 404'd (already resolved), and the route reported that as the final result. **Net effect: every real approve/deny through this endpoint has always succeeded server-side but always told the caller "Approval not found, not yours, or already resolved" — since this route was written, the dashboard's own `/approvals` page has never once shown a successful approval to a user**, even though the underlying action always went through. Fixed to `res.status_code >= 400`.

**Full live proof, both bugs fixed:** created a real pending approval (`tenant_odoo__chatter_post`) via a genuine Cerveau turn, resolved it through `/api/v1/agent-approvals/{id}/resolve` with no shortcuts — got back `{"success":true,"outcome":"approved",...}`, including Odoo's own second-layer preview→confirm approval cascading correctly (approving Cerveau's F-1 gate triggered the tool retry, which itself needed a second approval for Odoo's own write-confirm step — resolved that too). Independently verified the message landed in Odoo by querying `mail.message` directly (id 22, correct body). Visually verified the Console's Approve button and reply-append behavior in a real browser against a mocked-but-schema-accurate response (the local dev environment's `NEXT_PUBLIC_BACKEND_URL` points at an unstarted `localhost:8081`, unrelated to these fixes) — card renders, Approve transitions to "✓ Approved," the resume reply appends as a new message, exactly as designed.

## 2026-08-24 — Odoo Discuss approval notification shipped, plus an orphaned tenant registration found and fixed

**Context.** Per `docs/CERVEAU-CHANNEL-NATIVE-APPROVAL-PLAN.md`, extended the existing `approval-slack-notify.py` systemd timer (not a new process — same 3-minute cadence) with a second notification channel: when a pending F-1 `irreversible` approval belongs to a tenant with a `verified` custom Odoo MCP server registered (`product.tenant_custom_mcp_servers`, name contains "odoo"), the script now also posts a notice directly into that tenant's Odoo Discuss channel, alongside the existing Slack path. Tier 2 per the plan — notification + dashboard link, not a real interactive button; resolving a Discuss reply into a decision would mean parsing free-text intent, deliberately not attempted.

**Grounded in the live server, not its docs.** Before writing any parsing logic, probed the running `erpipe-org/mcp-odoo` bridge directly: `search_records`'s and `chatter_post`'s real payloads live in the MCP tool-call `content[0].text` block (JSON-encoded) — `structuredContent`'s shape is inconsistent between tools on this server (flat for `search_records`, nested a level for `chatter_post`), so the script deliberately parses the text block rather than trusting that field. Confirmed `chatter_post` is always a two-call preview → confirm dance (the first call, even with `confirm:true`, only returns a `{"mode":"preview","approval":{...token...}}`; the second call must echo that exact object back), and confirmed it is *not* gated by `ODOO_MCP_ENABLE_WRITES` (unlike the generic `preview_write`/`execute_approved_write` path, which returned a hard "write execution disabled" error in the same probe) — so Discuss notification works even for a tenant who never opted into general Odoo writes.

**Real bug found and fixed along the way.** The only existing Odoo custom-MCP-server row was registered under `user_id='aivory-internal'` — a synthetic id used during an earlier live-proof session, with no matching `product.agent_profiles` row. That made it invisible to every real polling/dashboard path keyed off `agent_profiles`, this notifier included — a "permanently registered" connection that no real code path could actually reach. Re-pointed the row (`id a4ac9962-...`, same verified state, same 41 tools) to the user's actual superadmin account (`user_d0985ab099ef142a` / `irfan.reichmann@aivory.id`, `agent_type=autonomous`, already `engine='cerveau'`).

**Full live proof.** Triggered a genuine Cerveau turn asking the agent to post to Odoo Discuss — correctly parked as a real F-1 `irreversible` pending approval (`tenant_odoo__chatter_post`) rather than executing. Ran the notifier: it posted a real notice into the "general" Discuss channel, independently confirmed by querying `mail.message` directly against the live Odoo instance (message id 19). Ran it again — no duplicate (idempotent). Denied the approval via the real resolve endpoint, ran the notifier once more — local state file correctly converged to empty. State tracking is now per-channel (`{approval_id}:slack` / `{approval_id}:discuss`) rather than per-approval, so one channel failing doesn't block or duplicate the other; a one-time upconvert treats pre-existing bare-id state entries as already-Slack-notified so the upgrade itself doesn't re-fire a duplicate Slack message.

**Deployed** to `/usr/local/bin/approval-slack-notify.py` (backup `.bak-pre-odoo-discuss-20260824`), `TELEGRAM_GATEWAY_TOKEN` added to `/etc/approval-slack-notify.env` (backup `.bak-pre-odoo-discuss-20260824`) so the script can call avry-backend's internal decrypted-server endpoint. Not tracked in any git repo — same standalone-VPS-script precedent as `composio-connection-sync.py`.

**Not done, per the plan's own sequencing**: Telegram/Discord (Tier 1, real interactive resolution) and Slack-native approval-awareness — deferred, not started.

## 2026-08-23 — Landlock fix: MCP tool install root was missing Execute permission

**Context.** Surfaced incidentally during Phase 6 verification of the toolkit expansion plan (below): journal logs showed `officecli`/`lightpanda`/`pdf-oxide` failing to exec with `Permission denied (os error 13)` on **every** `office_assistant`-agent-type turn, on **both** production instances. Confirmed pre-existing (unrelated to that day's config changes — file mtimes on the three binaries were weeks old, and the error reproduced identically on both instances despite one being freshly restarted and the other not yet touched) — it had simply never been exercised by real traffic recently enough for anyone to notice.

**Root cause, found by reading `crates/zeroclaw-runtime/src/security/landlock.rs`** (`~/Documents/AVRY-Cerveau`, no VPS access needed to diagnose): `apply_restrictions()`'s `extra_readable_root` rule — meant to grant the MCP tool's own operator-installed directory (`mcp_tool_install_root()`, e.g. `mcp-tools/officecli`) the same access as `/usr`/`/bin` — only ever granted `ReadFile | ReadDir`, never `Execute`, directly contradicting its own comment ("same trust tier as the /usr and /bin entries"). None of `officecli`'s, `lightpanda`'s, or `pdf-oxide`'s install roots fall under any of the ruleset's other granted paths (workspace, /tmp, /usr, /bin, /lib, /etc, /proc, /sys, /dev), so Execute was never available anywhere for the actual binary being `execve`'d — a latent bug since patch 0016 introduced this rule, not a regression from anything done this session.

**Fix.** One-line addition: `AccessFs::Execute | AccessFs::ReadFile | AccessFs::ReadDir` on the `extra_readable_root` rule (commit `3776ea21`, branch `fix-landlock-mcp-tool-execute`, merged to `cerveau-main`). Per standing discipline, never built/tested on `tencent-vps` — pushed, `cerveau-build.yml` triggered via `workflow_dispatch`, green. CI artifact downloaded, sha256-verified before and after transfer, `--version` confirmed `0.8.4`. Deployed with the usual atomic-binary-swap + staged-restart discipline: `:3100` → 90s window (health 200, `NRestarts=0`, **zero** `Permission denied` in logs vs. failing on every prior turn) → `-b` → joint 90s window, both green, `aivory.uk` 200. Live-verified with a real `/webhook` call under `office_assistant` on both instances post-deploy — clean exec, zero errors, confirmed via `journalctl` grep. Throwaway test tenants cleaned up.

## 2026-08-23 — Gmail/Google Calendar/Trello/Linear toolkits live, plus a real sync-poller bug found and fixed

**Context.** Two gaps identified against the (then) 5-toolkit ERP surface: `blueprintPlanner.ts` defaults the "Email"/"Calendar" categories to Gmail/Google Calendar, neither ever wired to Cerveau; ADR-007's "Product tracking" decision (Trello vs. Linear) was still open. Full plan + execution record: `docs/CERVEAU-TOOLKIT-EXPANSION-PLAN.md`. This entry is the handoff summary.

**1. A real production bug found first, before any new toolkit work.** Verifying that ERPNext's OAuth/API-key connect flow "really" reaches Cerveau (not a mockup) surfaced that `composio-connection-sync.py` — the systemd-timer poller that syncs Composio's connected-account state into `product.agent_toolkit_connections`, the table Cerveau's `ToolkitConnectionResolver` actually reads — only enumerated users with an `agent_type` of `finance_invoice_ops`/`customer_service`. Written before ERPNext/Slack/Asana were wired to `office_assistant`/`autonomous`, never updated: any tenant whose only profile was `office_assistant` or `autonomous` was silently invisible to the sync, so a genuine Composio connection for that tenant would never reach Cerveau's gate. Fixed same day: broadened to all 5 agent types (matching `agent_tool_scope.py`'s `TOGGLEABLE_TOOLKITS` exactly), deployed, confirmed live (`total=1` → `total=2` on the next sync run, picking up both real `autonomous` tenants that existed in prod).

**2. Provisioning.** Composio catalog queried live: Gmail 23 tools → curated 14, Google Calendar 28 → 13, Trello **150** (not 100 — first pull was page-limited) → 10, Linear 21 → 12. Real safety finding: Linear's `LINEAR_RUN_QUERY_OR_MUTATION` (arbitrary GraphQL wildcard) excluded outright, same class of decision as ERPNext's `CREATE_WEBHOOK` exclusion. Membership-trial rejections this round (Google Calendar `FREE_BUSY_QUERY`, Linear `GET_ALL_LINEAR_TEAMS`, four Trello slugs including card-update and member-assignment — genuinely unavailable, no accepted alternate). Four production MCP servers live: `aivory-gmail-mail`, `aivory-googlecalendar`, `aivory-trello-tasks`, `aivory-linear-tasks`; account verified at exactly 10 servers, no orphans.

**3. Tiering — draft-writes deliberately not auto-approved, mirroring ERPNext's stricter-than-HubSpot posture.** 11 hard-floor `irreversible` actions (Gmail send/delete/trash, Calendar delete, Trello delete-card, Linear delete-issue) plus 38 `reversible` (24 reads + 14 draft-writes); only the 24 reads went into `risk_profiles.agent_analyst_brain.auto_approve`. Satisfies the user's explicit "agent must confirm with the user first" instruction for Calendar (generalized to all four): default Prompt-per-call until a future config-only flip, same story as ERPNext's own documented path to an eventual autonomous mode.

**4. Config + deploy, both instances.** 4× `[[mcp.servers]]`, 4× `[mcp_bundles.*]`, `agent_type_mcp_bundles` additions (Gmail → `office_assistant`/`leads_qualifier`/`finance_invoice_ops`; Calendar/Trello/Linear → `office_assistant`; union into `autonomous`), `[tool_risk_tiers]` entries. Backups `.bak-pre-toolkit-expansion-20260823`, doctor identical before/after (78 ok, 11 warnings, 0 errors), staged restarts, both instances health 200/`NRestarts=0`, `aivory.uk` 200.

**5. Backend/frontend, committed *and* deployed.** `agent_tool_scope.py`/`CustomizeAgentModal.tsx` committed (`5af3de7`, `e5383b9`), pushed to `aivory-hub/main` (clean fast-forward, zero unrelated drift in either file this round). Then actually deployed — the gap explicitly checked before declaring done: VPS checkouts fast-forward pulled (zero tracked-file drift), `docker compose build` + `up -d` for both `avry-backend`/`avry-user-dashboard`, verified via container-level checks (`[OK] Agent tool scope routes registered` in the backend log, all 4 slugs present in the running container's file, "Google Calendar" string present in a real served JS chunk) — same bar the 2026-08-22 ERPNext deploy used.

**6. Verification — negative-path protocol passed, with a real methodology lesson.** Fail-closed ✅. Full-chain proof needed a fresh tenant id on the second attempt — the first reused the fail-closed test's tenant id and the model's response looked like a repeat refusal; a fresh tenant plus explicitly asking the model to relay the tool's raw error **verbatim, don't paraphrase** got Composio's real API error back cleanly: `"No connected account found for user ID toolkit-verify-tmp-002 for toolkit gmail"` (and the same pattern for Trello) — proving the full chain, not just config correctness. Isolation ✅ (`customer_service`, not assigned any of the 4, sees zero tools despite live connection rows). Cleanup verified zero-count; Composio account re-confirmed at 10 servers.

**Incidental finding from this round**: see the Landlock entry directly above — found via this verification's own control test, fixed same day.

**Current status.** All of the above live and verified on both instances. Open items: (a) happy path awaits a real tenant connecting one of the four; (b) a `CERVEAU-TOOLKIT-EXPANSION-PLAN.md` follow-up (n8n orchestration for genuine multi-step cross-toolkit scenarios — lead capture, support escalation, task handoff, invoice approval — scoped to avoid the no-benefit 1:1-wrapper trap) is queued next.

## 2026-08-22 — ERPNext toolkit live (Frappe × Cerveau), learning loop enabled behind patch 0039

**Context.** User asked whether Frappe/ERPNext could add ERP capability to Cerveau (not replace it). Outcome: yes, via the proven ADR-007 Option A pattern — Composio-hosted MCP toolkit + connection gating + risk tiers. Full planning + execution record lives in `docs/CERVEAU-ERP-INTEGRATION-PLAN.md` (approved before execution); this entry is the handoff summary.

**1. Provisioning.** Composio `ERPNEXT` catalog queried live: 52 tools → curated **26** (12 reads / 6 draft writes / 8 ledger+business-doc actions); `CREATE_WEBHOOK` excluded entirely (persistence vector), duplicate CRUD dropped. All 26 accepted by the server-creation endpoint first try (no HubSpot-style catalog quirk). Auth: custom `API_KEY` auth config `ac_ILn9zmSA5cqN` — tenants supply their own Frappe URL + key/secret (Frappe User Settings → API Access), zero credentials on Aivory's side. Production MCP server: `aivory-erpnext-erp` (`…/v3/mcp/356767f6-…`); account holds exactly 6 servers.

**2. Tiering — decided by user, stricter than HubSpot precedent.** Reads auto-approved; **every ERP write including drafts is `irreversible`**, so on the webhook path each write creates a durable F-1 pending-approval row instead of executing ("no moment where an agent nyelonong"). The runtime semantics make this land naturally (verified in source: Irreversible + non-interactive → `Pending`, un-overridable). Future "autonomous" mode = flipping the 6 draft-write slugs to reversible+auto_approve, an explicit human config action.

**3. Config + deploy.** Both `:3100`/`-b`: one `[[mcp.servers]]` (`composio-erpnext-erp`, `requires_composio_toolkit = "erpnext"`), `[mcp_bundles.erp-erpnext]`, bundle assigned to `finance_invoice_ops`/`office_assistant`/`autonomous`; 26 slugs tiered; reads into analyst-brain auto_approve. Backups `.bak-pre-erpnext-20260822`, TOML-validated locally+VPS, doctor 78 ok / 0 errors, staged restarts — final state all green (health 200×2, NRestarts=0, HAProxy UP, site 200).

**4. Verification — negative-path protocol passed all four proofs.** Fail-closed gate ✅ ("I don't have access to an ERPNext or Frappe integration"); full-chain proof via synthetic `ACTIVE` gate row ✅ (model made two real tool calls, Composio answered *"No connected account found for user ID erp-verify-tmp-001"* with the correct entity_id — only a genuine tenant connection is missing, disclosed not fabricated); isolation ✅ (`customer_service` sees zero ERP tools despite the ACTIVE row); cleanup verified zero-count. Lesson recorded: negative tests must name the integration — generic asks correctly route to native tools.

**5. Repo alignment (the 08-18 open item closed).** `cerveau-main` reset onto the CI-green v0.8.4 series (+ docs commit updating the base note; old history preserved at tag `archive/cerveau-main-v0.8.3`). During the same pass: **patch 0039** — upstream's `[skills.skill_creation]`/`[skills.skill_improvement]` write to the host workspace that tenant turns also load, so both blocks are now guarded with `current_tenant().is_none()` (learning = host/internal-turn-only until per-tenant skill stores exist). CI green, binary checksummed and staged-deployed (one corrupt-scp segfault caught by the pre-deploy `--version` gate, fixed via rsync re-transfer). Learning loop then enabled in config: `skill_creation` + reflection ON; `skill_improvement` was discovered already-on from an earlier round and is only now actually safe. Guard proof: a multi-tool tenant turn produced zero auto-created skills.

**6. Dashboard/backend shipped too.** Backend `agent_tool_scope.py` allowlist + modal label committed (`2866145`, `770d107`); then the Connections-tab gap was closed — OAuth-only filter meant API_KEY toolkits had no connect path, so `POST /api/integrations/apikey/connect` (server-side Composio account creation, auth-config ids never client-supplied) plus an inline Base URL/Key/Secret form were added (`20bc324` rebased onto the user's rewritten dashboard history) and **both containers rebuilt and deployed** (backend healthy, `/dashboard/agents` 200 public, erpnext present in container code and JS bundles).

**Incidents this round, both caught by gates:** corrupt scp → segfault at the version check (pre-deploy); VPS backend merge conflicts during deploy resolved by discovering the entire drift was already committed upstream (verified feature-by-feature before taking HEAD wholesale; stash kept as archive).

**Current status.** Everything above live and verified. Open items: (a) happy path awaits a real tenant connecting Frappe via the Connections tab; (b) monitor `/api/cost` for reflection/review-fork spend; (c) autonomous-mode flip is a deliberate future config change; (d) Stage-2 tenant-facing learning blocked on per-tenant skill stores; (e) Composio API key rotation worth bundling into the next maintenance window with the JWT secret.

## 2026-08-18 — Cerveau upgraded v0.8.3 → v0.8.4, live in production → v0.8.4 in production — 50-commit rebase onto upstream, a security-critical merge bug caught by the compiler and fixed, a self-inflicted production outage from running the test suite on the live VPS, recovery via GitHub Actions CI instead of local build, staged deploy, and two systemd boot-hygiene fixes. See the entry directly below for the full account, most recent first; the 2026-08-17 Stripe cleanup and everything before it is unchanged, further down).

## 2026-08-18 — Cerveau upgraded v0.8.3 → v0.8.4, live in production

**Context.** User asked to update/rebase Cerveau to upstream `zeroclaw-labs/zeroclaw` v0.8.4, explicitly "safely, and without errors." This is Cerveau's first version bump since the fork — the whole discipline below (isolated worktree, CI-verified before touching production, staged rollout with stability windows) is now the template for every future upstream bump, not a one-off.

**0. Found the local checkout was stale before touching anything.** The VPS's `AVRY-Cerveau` git checkout (`cerveau-main` branch) was only 1 commit ahead of the `v0.8.3` tag — just the CI-bootstrap patch. The real 38-patch series (tenant isolation, MCP wiring, F-1 approvals, memory lifecycle, everything this whole status doc describes) lived on `aivory-hub/cerveau-main`, 49 commits ahead, never fetched into this particular working copy. Fetched it; confirmed the deployed production binary (built 2026-08-15) actually corresponds to that 50-commit history, not the stale local one.

**1. Pre-flight risk research before rebasing anything.** `v0.8.3..v0.8.4` upstream: 263 commits, 974 files, +105,894/−41,132 lines — explicitly not a small bump. Release notes flagged two breaking changes; both checked against the real production configs rather than assumed safe: (a) "webhook channels without a configured `secret` now fail to start" — both instances' `[channels.webhook.cerveau]` already had `enabled = false` *and* a `secret` set regardless, so moot; (b) "ClawHub skill sources (`clawhub:<slug>`) removed" — grepped both live configs, zero usage, also moot. Flagged the real risk areas from the release notes as places to expect conflicts: SOP admission/approval, memory/embeddings, and Landlock sandboxing — all three did in fact produce the round's hardest merges (see below).

**2. The rebase.** Done in an isolated `git worktree` (`cerveau-work/rebase-v084`, new branch), never touching the production checkout, so a bad outcome would cost nothing. `git rebase --onto v0.8.4 v0.8.3` replayed the 50-commit Aivory patch series onto the new base. Most conflicts were mechanical and additive (two patches adding new struct fields at the same textual position — kept both; test insertions landing next to an existing test — kept both, reconciled one differing comment line); a few needed real engineering judgment:
  - **Landlock (patches 0014/0016/0018), the hardest merge of the round.** Upstream v0.8.4 independently rewrote the exact same bug Cerveau's patch 0014 had already fixed in production — "Landlock restrictions were being applied to the daemon process instead of the spawned child" — but via a different mechanism: upstream uses a `pre_exec` closure (restrict inside the forked child, before `exec()`); Cerveau uses a self-re-exec pattern (rewrite the `Command` to invoke this same binary's hidden `internal-landlock-exec` subcommand, which restricts *itself* — a fresh, ordinary process — then `execve`s into the real target). Surfaced this explicitly to the user rather than picking silently: **kept Cerveau's already-proven re-exec mechanism** (lower risk than adopting a brand-new mechanism mid-rebase) **but adopted upstream's substantially richer ruleset table** (added required-vs-optional path handling, more system paths) underneath it — a genuine splice, not a pick-a-side resolution. Patches 0016 (grant the MCP tool's own npm install root) and 0018 (grant `/proc`, `/sys`, broader `/dev` — CoreCLR/.NET startup requirements discovered via live `strace`) then had to be re-folded into the new merged ruleset table rather than their original diffs, since both were originally written against the pre-merge simpler structure.
  - **`skills/mod.rs`**: patch 0011 had extracted a shared `apply_skill_bundle` helper out of an inline loop specifically to reuse it for a new tenant-agent-type bundle path; upstream v0.8.4 never had that extraction. Verified via `git show v0.8.4:...` that upstream's version of this function was byte-for-byte the same *pre-patch-0011* shape (confirming the "conflict" was purely rebase-mechanics noise, not real upstream work to reconcile) before taking Cerveau's side wholesale.

**3. `cargo check`/`cargo test` caught 4 real bugs no diff-reading would have.** All four only surface because the compiler forces every struct-literal field to be accounted for:
  - **Security-critical: `ApprovalManager::derive_for_risk_profile`.** Upstream v0.8.4 added this constructor (used for delegated execution — an SOP step naming a different agent) before Cerveau's `risk_tiers`/`idem_ledger`/`pending_store` fields existed, so it compiled by leaving them `None`. Left as-is, this would have **silently disabled tiered approval for every delegated execution** — an `Irreversible`-tier tool degrading to `Reversible`, F-2 idempotency protection vanishing, no durable pending-approval record written, turning any agent-delegating SOP step into a bypass for the whole approval mechanism. Fixed by carrying all three over from `self`, same as `non_interactive` already does on this constructor — deployment-level infrastructure, not per-agent policy that should reset.
  - Two unclosed-delimiter bugs, both self-inflicted during merge ("keep both sides" resolutions where the closing brace after the conflict belonged to *both* sides, so keeping both bodies silently dropped the actual closer): `struct StdioState` in `mcp_transport.rs`, `fn main()` in `main.rs`. Both invisible in the diff, both caught immediately by `cargo check`.
  - `GatewayChatOutcome`'s `input_tokens`/`output_tokens`/`cost_usd` went from "used" to "dead code" — turned out to be upstream *correctly* removing this crate's own duplicate `LlmResponse`/`AgentEnd` emission from the webhook handler (a real upstream bug fix: one webhook prompt was getting two unrelated turn IDs; `process_message`'s runtime turn guard is now the sole emitter). Not a regression — cost is still persisted to `/api/cost`/`costs.jsonl` independently. Narrowed the destructure, kept the fields with `#[allow(dead_code)]` and a comment rather than deleting patch 0021's plumbing outright (a product call on whether to surface per-turn cost on the webhook response, not a rebase decision to make unilaterally).
  - Two test-fixture gaps in the opposite direction from the ApprovalManager bug (upstream's new `AppState.mdns_peer_registry` field missing from 4 Cerveau test fixtures; an upstream `McpServerConfig` test fixture missing 5 Cerveau-added fields) — fixed with an explicit default for the former, `..McpServerConfig::default()` spread for the latter so it survives whichever side adds the next field.

**4. Self-inflicted production outage, mid-verification.** Ran a full `cargo test` across five crates directly on `tencent-vps` — the same box serving all live traffic — reasoning (wrongly) that since `cargo check` had run there safely, `cargo test` would too. It didn't: parallel test binaries + threads + tests that spawn real child processes exhausted the box's memory. `nice -n 15` only deprioritizes CPU, it does nothing for memory pressure. SSH itself stopped answering at the banner-exchange stage (before auth, so every key/user failed identically — not a credentials problem), `aivory.uk` and the dashboards went to `000`, direct-to-origin curl also `000`. No SSH access meant no way to kill the runaway processes from this session; user had to force-restart the instance from the Tencent Cloud console. Production was down for the restart window. Separately, ~3 hours were lost to a self-authored monitoring bug: a wait-loop used `pgrep -f cargo` to detect whether the build was still running, which matched its own command line (which contains the word "cargo") and hung forever reporting "still running" on a job that had actually already finished. **Both are now recorded as standing rules, not just this-session lessons** — see `[[feedback-avoid-over-engineering-expensive-tests]]`, updated same-day: never run a full test suite on `tencent-vps` (the curated CI test list is the necessary set; if it's green, that's sufficient), and prefer GitHub Actions for anything test-suite-shaped rather than the production box.

**5. Recovery revealed a second gap: the bug fixes from step 3 had never actually been committed.** They were applied as uncommitted edits directly to the VPS worktree, verified against that (already-patched) working tree, and never `git commit`'d — so the branch already pushed to GitHub at that point still had the original unclosed-brace/missing-field bugs. This is *why* the first CI run (see below) failed at the very first step with an unrelated-looking `Cargo.lock`/`--locked` error: a fresh CI clone reads the actual commits, not the VPS's silently-patched working tree. Committed properly as 3 explicit commits with full rationale in the messages (merge-mistake fixes; the security-critical approval fix, isolated on its own with the full blast-radius explanation; test-fixture + dead-code + lockfile hygiene) and re-pushed.

**6. Switched the actual release build to GitHub Actions CI instead of building on the VPS, specifically because of #4.** Pushed the rebased history to a new branch (`cerveau-main-v0.8.4`, not `cerveau-main` — deliberately not fast-forwarding the "real" branch until proven end-to-end), triggered `cerveau-build.yml` via `workflow_dispatch`. First run failed fast on the missing-commits gap from #5 (caught early, cheaply, on a runner — not on production, which was the whole point of moving off the VPS). Second run, with the complete history: **green, 29m24s** — every test gate (tenant isolation, memory lifecycle, Postgres embedding/recall, capability graph, webhook rate-limit dimension, ADR-005 shared-limiter/admission-queue) plus the release binary build and publish, all on an isolated runner untouched by anything happening on `tencent-vps`. (A local VPS release-build attempt was also tried first, under a purpose-built memory-floor + health-check watchdog script — it correctly self-aborted once when available memory crossed the 1.5 GB floor, with zero production impact, proving the watchdog works, but the box still isn't comfortable running a fat-LTO release build alongside live traffic; CI is now the standing answer for this class of task, not a one-off workaround.)

**7. Deployed to production with the same atomic-swap-plus-staged-restart discipline as every other change in this project.** Downloaded the CI-published release artifact + its `.sha256`, checksum-verified, extracted, confirmed `--version` reports `0.8.4` — all in a staging directory, nothing touching the live binary yet. Backed up the running `v0.8.3` binary (`zeroclaw-cerveau.bak-pre-v084-20260818`). Both `:3100`/`:3101` systemd units share one binary path, but since Linux keeps an already-running process bound to its old inode after the file underneath it is replaced, swapping the binary on disk didn't touch either currently-running process — confirmed both still 200 immediately after the swap. Restarted `:3100` alone, confirmed `0.8.4`, ran a 90-second stability window (`NRestarts=0`, health 200 throughout) plus a live functional check (a deliberately-401'd probe still wrote to the shared Redis rate-limit key from the new binary), then restarted `:3101`, another 90-second window on both together, and a final full check: both instances `0.8.4`, HAProxy's `cerveau-a`/`cerveau-b` both `UP`, the Redis-backed limiter's counter provably shared across both post-upgrade processes (incremented by a probe to the *other* instance), `aivory.uk` 200.

**8. Two systemd boot-hygiene gaps found and closed while verifying post-deploy state, unrelated to the version bump itself but surfaced by the reboot in #4.** (a) `zeroclaw-cerveau-b.service` was `disabled` for boot while `zeroclaw-cerveau.service` (`:3100`) was `enabled` — confirmed live: after the forced reboot in #4, `:3100` came back on its own and `:3101` silently didn't, caught only because this was actively being checked, not because anything alerted. Enabled it to match. (b) No systemd ordering between either Cerveau unit and the `redis` container (docker-compose, `restart: unless-stopped`, no native systemd unit of its own) — a real boot-time race where Cerveau could start before Redis is accepting connections and permanently fall back to the in-process rate limiter for that process's whole lifetime (the documented ADR-005 fail-open behavior, but landing on the *wrong* side of it by pure timing). Closed with a drop-in on both units (`After=docker.service` + an `ExecStartPre` that polls `/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/6379` for up to 30s) — tested both branches directly: Redis-already-up resolves in 6ms (the common case pays almost nothing), Redis-genuinely-absent still `exit 0`s after the full 30s wait, preserving the existing fail-open contract rather than changing it. Also fixed both units' `Description=` strings, which still said "v0.8.3 fork." None of this required restarting the already-verified-stable running instances — boot-ordering and `ExecStartPre` changes only take effect on the *next* start, so they were applied via `daemon-reload` alone and will silently do the right thing on the next real reboot instead of needing a human to notice and intervene, which is the exact gap this closes.

**Current status.** Production fully on `v0.8.4`, both instances enabled for boot, Redis race closed, verified end-to-end (version, health, LB, shared-limiter behavior, live site). `cerveau-main-v0.8.4` is pushed and CI-green but **not yet fast-forwarded into `cerveau-main`** — worth doing once this has run a few days without incident, so the branch state matches what's actually deployed. Backup binary retained for instant rollback. No further action required unless a regression surfaces.

## 2026-08-17 — Stripe MCP bundle cleanup closed out

**Context.** The 08-16 entry's ADR-007 section flagged a stale default in `blueprintPlanner.ts:169` (`payment|finance|billing` → `Stripe`) left over from before Stripe was retired from Composio wiring in the 2026-08-08 "agnostic tools" pivot (`agent_tool_scope.py`'s `TOGGLEABLE_TOOLKITS` no longer lists it), and separately an open question on whether the dashboard's Stripe MCP bundle for `finance_invoice_ops` was now dead weight worth unwiring. User asked to clean both up.

**Investigated before touching anything, per the usual discipline.** Checked the actual live production Cerveau configs first rather than assuming the ADR's framing was still accurate: `/home/ubuntu/.zeroclaw-cerveau/config.toml` (`:3100`) and `/home/ubuntu/.zeroclaw-cerveau-b/config.toml` (`:3101`), both last modified 2026-08-15, already had **zero** Stripe references anywhere — no `[[mcp.servers]]` entry, no `mcp_bundles`, no `auto_approve`/`tool_risk_tiers` lines. Production had already been cleaned before this was ever flagged as open; the ADR-007 note was stale, not the config. The only place Stripe wiring still existed was `/home/ubuntu/.zeroclaw-cerveau-dryrun/config.toml`, dated 2026-08-09 — confirmed via `systemctl list-unit-files` that this directory has no corresponding systemd service at all (not `zeroclaw-cerveau-dryrun.service` or anything else), i.e. it was never live-serving, just a leftover manual-testing config from an earlier round.

**Fixed anyway, for hygiene, since it's cheap and the file is confusing to leave stale.** Removed the full `composio-stripe-invoicing` `[[mcp.servers]]` block (incl. its `[mcp.servers.headers]` sub-table), the `[mcp_bundles.finance-invoice-ops-stripe]` block, `"finance-invoice-ops-stripe"` from both `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.finance_invoice_ops]` and `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.autonomous]` bundle arrays, the 8 `composio-stripe-invoicing__STRIPE_*` entries from `auto_approve`, and the 8 matching entries split across `[tool_risk_tiers]`'s `irreversible`/`reversible` arrays. Edited a local copy, validated with `python3 -c "import tomllib; tomllib.load(...)"` before and after, backed up the live file on the VPS as `config.toml.bak-pre-stripe-cleanup-20260816` first, then atomically swapped in the cleaned version (`.new` + `mv`). No restart needed or possible — not a running service. Confirmed `grep -ic stripe` returns `0` on the live file post-swap.

**`blueprintPlanner.ts:169` fixed and shipped.** Removed the `payment|finance|billing → Stripe` entry from `DEFAULT_INTEGRATION_BY_CATEGORY` with a comment explaining why no replacement default was added (no Composio-connectable payment platform exists today) — the category now correctly falls through to `needsClarification`, same as any other category with no real default, matching the precedent already set for "Product tracking" in the 08-16 ADR-007 entry. Verified `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (no new errors) and `vitest` on the full `blueprintPlanner` suite: 132/135 passing, the same 3 failures present before the change (confirmed via `git stash`/re-run — pre-existing, unrelated `graphSemantics.test.ts` failures, not caused by this fix). No test referenced Stripe directly, so nothing needed updating on that side. Committed (`ec7fb13`) and pushed directly to `aivory-hub/main` — local `main` was exactly in sync with `aivory-hub/main` (0 ahead/0 behind) at push time, so no worktree workaround was needed this round.

**Current status: fully closed, nothing left open.** Production Cerveau was already clean; the dryrun config and the frontend default are now clean too. `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s Stripe-adjacent entries (if any) and `types/blueprint.ts`/`types/diagnostic.ts`'s Stripe mentions in sample/placeholder content were left untouched — those are illustrative copy, not real tool wiring, and out of scope for this cleanup.
