## ADR-006 Part B — environment adaptation (tenant custom MCP servers) — B1-B5 complete, deployed, live-verified

Continuation of the same-day ADR-006 Part A ("Deploy via API") session — see that entry below this one. User's ask: "lanjutkan part B" then "oke lanjutkan" through B1-B4, following [ADR-006](ADR-006-CERVEAU-CLIENT-DEPLOYMENT-API.md)'s own phasing table (§"Phasing and exit gates") which sequences Part B as B1 (guarded fetcher, adversarially tested, no tenant-facing surface) → B2 (Postgres table + dashboard CRUD wired through B1) → B3 (Cerveau resolver + dynamic MCP wiring) → B4/B5 (dashboard UI, deploy + live-verify). All work below is local-only: no VPS system touched, nothing committed, nothing pushed.

**Why this feature exists, in plain terms:** out of the box, an Aivory Cerveau agent only knows what Aivory curated for it — the native bridge, OfficeCLI, whichever Composio toolkits (Stripe, Zendesk, …) a tenant happens to have connected. It has no way to see or act on the specific systems a given business actually runs — their own inventory database, an internal ticketing tool, a proprietary ERP, a homegrown API. Part B closes that gap: a tenant registers their own MCP server, and from then on their agent can read and act on *their* environment directly, using the exact same MCP-native mechanism that already serves Aivory's own curated tools. This is what makes an agent feel like it genuinely understands one specific business's context — not a generic assistant limited to Aivory's own toolkit — which is the original product question this whole ADR exists to answer (see ADR-006's own "Context" line: "how the agent can 'smartly adapt' to that environment, read the client's own system data"). Precisely because this capability is arbitrary — any tenant can wire in any system — every tool it exposes is treated as a black box Aivory never reviewed, which is why B1 (SSRF containment) and B3's mandatory `Irreversible` tiering are non-negotiable, not opt-in.

**B1 — the guarded fetcher, the load-bearing security piece, built twice independently (Python for avry-backend's registration-time verification, Rust for Cerveau's own runtime MCP calls) because the two processes must never share the AES key that decrypts a tenant's auth-header value (ADR-006 §B3).** Both implement the identical control set from ADR-006 §B4: `https://`-only rejected at parse time before any network call; DNS resolved explicitly via a custom resolver hook and every resolved address validated against a deny-list (loopback, RFC1918, link-local — covering both AWS/GCP/Azure's `169.254.169.254` *and* Tencent Cloud's own `169.254.0.23`, since this VPS is Tencent — CGNAT `100.64.0.0/10`, reserved, multicast, unspecified, and IPv4-mapped-IPv6 variants of all of the above) *before* any connection is attempted; if ANY resolved answer is denied the whole hostname is rejected, not just that one address (closes the classic "multi-A-record host, reconnect on whichever answer" DNS-rebinding bypass); the validated address is what the client actually connects to — resolve-once-connect-to-that-exact-address, never resolve-then-let-something-else-reresolve, which is what closes the TOCTOU gap a naive validate-then-connect two-step would leave open; no automatic redirect-following (every redirect target gets re-validated from scratch); a streaming byte-counter caps response size at 256 KB without trusting `Content-Length`; bounded connect/total timeouts.

- **Python** (`backend/avry-backend/app/services/guarded_fetch.py`): a custom `http.client.HTTPSConnection` subclass (`_PinnedHTTPSConnection`) whose `connect()` opens the raw TCP socket to the pre-validated IP while `self.host` (used for SNI + certificate hostname verification, unweakened) stays the original hostname. `tests/test_guarded_fetch.py`, 15 tests, pure-stdlib (zero third-party deps, runs with plain `python3 -m unittest`, no venv needed) — deny-list matrix, DNS-rebinding-shape rejection (mocked), redirect-to-private-IP rejection, oversized-response early-abort (proven via read-call-count, not just that it eventually raised), max-redirect cap. **Also verified against real, live network traffic**, not just mocks: a real TLS handshake + HTTP round-trip to `api.github.com` succeeded; a literal `https://127.0.0.1:3105/webhook` (where this VPS's own Cerveau HAProxy actually listens) was correctly denied; a real DNS-based SSRF payload via wildcard DNS (`https://127.0.0.1.nip.io/`, which genuinely resolves to `127.0.0.1`) was correctly denied — this one is a live adversarial DNS probe, not a mock. Caught and fixed one real gap during testing: stdlib `ipaddress.Ipv4Address.is_private` does **not** cover RFC6598 (CGNAT) on this Python version — verified directly rather than assumed, then added an explicit CIDR check.
- **Rust** (`crates/zeroclaw-tools/src/guarded_resolve.rs`): implements reqwest 0.12's `dns::Resolve` trait directly (`GuardedResolver`) — reqwest connects to exactly whatever `resolve()` returns, so validate-and-return-only-that IS the atomicity guarantee, no separate connect step to race. `build_guarded_client()` wraps it with `redirect::Policy::none()` and explicit connect/total timeouts; `read_capped_body()` streams with the same early-abort-on-cap discipline as the Python side. New `McpServerConfig.guarded_transport: bool` field (computed only, never settable via `[[mcp.servers]]` config — same "config declares intent, the tenant-scoping step fills in the real value" pattern as the existing `tenant_workspace_dir`) — when true, `HttpTransport`/`SseTransport` (`mcp_transport.rs`) build their `reqwest::Client` via the guarded path instead of a bare one; a curated Aivory-authored server never sets this and is completely unaffected. 13 unit tests (deny-list matrix mirroring the Python one exactly, plus real IP-literal-hostname resolution tests against `GuardedResolver` directly — no mocking needed since Rust's `Ipv4Addr`/`Ipv6Addr` methods are used directly, though `is_reserved()`/`is_shared()` (CGNAT) are nightly-only on this toolchain (1.96.1) and needed the same manual-CIDR-check treatment as the Python side).

**B2 — `product.tenant_custom_mcp_servers` (avry-backend, self-migrating, same idiom as `agent_profiles.py`/`agent_tool_scope.py`) + the registration/verification/management routes** (`app/routes/tenant_mcp_servers.py`). `auth_header_value` encrypted at rest with AES-256-GCM (`app/services/mcp_server_encryption.py`, `nonce+ciphertext+tag` format, ported from the two existing independent implementations in `avry-user-dashboard/lib/crypto.ts` and `avry-careers/encryption_service.py` but with its **own** dedicated env var `MCP_SERVER_AUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — key rotation stays decoupled from either existing use, per ADR-006 §B2). `POST /api/v1/tenant-mcp-servers` gates on Enterprise tier + `engine='cerveau'` (reads `product.agent_profiles.engine` via the existing `load_profile_internal`), validates `https://`-only + a `^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,40}$` name, caps at 1 server per `(user_id, agent_type)` for v1, then runs a **real synchronous MCP JSON-RPC handshake through the guarded fetcher** — `initialize` then `tools/list`, parsing either plain-JSON or SSE-framed (`data: {...}`) response bodies (same ambiguity Cerveau's own `mcp_transport.rs` already handles for this exact protocol) — before persisting `status='verified'` with a real tool list, or `status='verification_failed'` with the reason (still persisted, visible on the next list, per ADR-006 §B3's own spec: "watches it move `pending_verification → verified`... a URL pointed at `127.0.0.1` is rejected and never reaches `verified`"). `GET`/`POST .../reverify`/`DELETE` round out the dashboard-facing surface; `GET .../internal/{user_id}/{agent_type}` (X-Internal-Token) returns decrypted, `status='verified'`-only rows for Cerveau. `tests/test_tenant_mcp_servers.py`, 20 tests (JSON-RPC body parsing incl. SSE framing, URL/name validation, the full verification handshake mocked at the `guarded_fetch` boundary — success/SSRF-rejection/non-2xx/JSON-RPC-error/missing-tools-key/auth-header-forwarding, Pydantic request validation). 35/35 total (15+20) green.

**B3 — the Cerveau side: dynamic per-turn MCP wiring + non-bypassable Irreversible risk tiering (ADR-006 §B5's central claim, now actually enforced, not just argued for).** New `TenantCustomMcpResolver` (`crates/zeroclaw-gateway/src/tenant.rs`) — **the only resolver in this file that goes over HTTP to avry-backend rather than direct SQL**, deliberately (ADR-006 §B3): the AES key must live in exactly one process. Same bounded-TTL-LRU-cached, fail-open-on-availability shape as the existing `ToolkitConnectionResolver`/`AgentToolScopeResolver` (an unreachable avry-backend degrades a turn to "no custom servers," never blocks memory/persona/native-tool access, and can only ever under-grant). `TenantContext` (`zeroclaw-runtime/src/agent/tenant.rs`) gains `tenant_custom_mcp_servers: Vec<TenantCustomMcpServer>` plus two methods: `custom_mcp_server_configs()` synthesizes real `McpServerConfig` entries (always `guarded_transport: true`, name prefixed `tenant_<name>` — both a collision-safety margin and a visually-unmistakable marker in logs) and `is_tenant_custom_mcp_tool(tool_name)` checks the prefix. `Config::mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant` (now 6 args, all ~13 test call sites updated) appends these *after* the existing 4-gate chain rather than filtering them through it — they're not Composio-sourced, so the connection/scope gates don't apply; still deduped by name as defense-in-depth (a name collision lets the curated server win, proven by a dedicated test). **The actual non-bypassable enforcement**: `ApprovalManager::risk_tier()` (`zeroclaw-runtime/src/approval/mod.rs`) now checks `current_tenant().is_tenant_custom_mcp_tool(tool_name)` **first, before consulting `[tool_risk_tiers]` at all** — so no config entry, however configured, can ever downgrade a tenant-supplied tool below `Irreversible`. This directly closes the exact gap ADR-006 §B5 named as "no longer hypothetical" (the same session's earlier discovery that `[tool_risk_tiers].irreversible` had been empty in production). 12 (`tenant.rs`) + 8 (`schema.rs` incl. 3 new tests for the append/dedupe/none-is-noop behavior) + 77 (`approval/mod.rs` incl. 1 new test scoping a fake tenant context and proving `Irreversible` wins even for a manager that never opted into `[tool_risk_tiers]` at all) + 5 (`TenantCustomMcpResolver` itself, wiremock-backed: parses a real HTTP response, fails open on 500/malformed-body, caches a hit and avoids a second HTTP call) = 102 new/updated tests, all green; full `cargo check --workspace` clean.

**B4 — dashboard UI: a new "MCP" tab in `CustomizeAgentModal.tsx`**, following the exact lazy-fetch-once-per-tab-open pattern Part C's Connections/Tools tabs already established (including the `*Fetched` boolean gate that avoids the infinite-retry-loop bug found live in that earlier round). New `lib/tenantMcpServers.ts` API client. Lists existing servers with a status badge (verifying/verified-with-tool-count/failed-with-reason), reverify/remove actions; when none exist, a registration form (name/URL/transport/optional auth header) that calls the synchronous register-and-verify endpoint and — critically — still shows the server in the list even on a 422 verification failure, since the row **was** persisted (the API client's `TenantMcpServerError` carries `.server` for exactly this case, distinguishing "saved but didn't verify" from "nothing was saved"). TypeScript typechecks clean (`npx tsc --noEmit`, the 2 pre-existing unrelated errors aside). **Not yet visually verified in a browser** — port 9000 (this dashboard's local dev port) was held by another session and its `dev` script hardcodes `-p 9000`, not worth changing the shared launch config for a one-off local check; real visual/functional verification is folded into B5's live deploy instead.

**Reference guide added for shim authors, [`docs/TENANT-MCP-SERVER-GUIDE.md`](TENANT-MCP-SERVER-GUIDE.md)** (compiled 2026-08-15 while B5's Cerveau CI was in flight, at the user's request — separate from the design/implementation account above, and linked from ADR-006 §"Part B" and its Critical Files list). Covers what makes a tenant's own MCP shim practical to build: the protocol went stateless as of the 2026-07-28 spec revision (no more `initialize`/session-id requirement, so any shim instance can answer any request — trivial to scale or skip scaling entirely); real memory benchmarks for picking a lightweight runtime (Rust ~7MB idle vs. TypeScript ~162MB vs. C# up to ~2.1GB) and near-zero-cost edge hosting (Cloudflare Workers' V8 isolates vs. container-based Lambda/Vercel cold starts); generating the shim straight from an existing OpenAPI spec (FastMCP `from_openapi()`, `openapi-mcp-generator`, Gram) instead of hand-writing MCP protocol code; and the finding that Cerveau's own `[mcp].deferred_loading` already implements the industry's current best-practice token-efficiency pattern (progressive tool discovery) — nothing new needed on Cerveau's side for a tenant server's tools to be token-cheap.

**B5 — deployed to production, then live-verified with a real external MCP server, real approval flow, and one real bug found and fixed along the way.**

**Deploy.** All three systems shipped: avry-backend (diff-before-deploy against the VPS's live `main.py`/`config.py`/`requirements.txt` caught the usual drift — security middleware, CORS whitelist, extra routers — patched surgically in place, same discipline as every prior round); Cerveau patch 0037 (checksum-verified, `doctor` unchanged at 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, `:3100` restarted alone with a 2-min stability check before `-b`); avry-user-dashboard was **already deployed by a concurrent session** working in the same working directory — confirmed via byte-identical source and a freshly-rebuilt, healthy container, no redundant redeploy needed. Two new env vars set: avry-backend's `MCP_SERVER_AUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (fresh, dedicated, backed up `.env` first) and Cerveau's `AVRY_BACKEND_INTERNAL_URL`/`AVRY_BACKEND_INTERNAL_TOKEN` (the latter set to avry-backend's own `TELEGRAM_GATEWAY_TOKEN` value — both instances share one `/etc/zeroclaw-cerveau.env`, so one edit covered both).

**A real, pre-existing bug found and fixed before live-verification could even start**, unrelated to Part B's own new code: the dashboard JWT (`auth_service.create_access_token`) has never carried a `tier` claim — only `user_id`/`email`/`account_type`. Every correct route in this codebase re-loads the current tier from Postgres before checking it (`telegram.py`'s `agent_chat` does exactly this via `_load_user`); `tenant_mcp_servers.py`'s `_require_enterprise` and — worse — **Part A's already-deployed `agent_api_keys.py`'s `create_key`** both checked `user.get("tier")` directly off the raw JWT instead, which is always `None`. **Net effect: the real "Create API key" button on the dashboard has been silently 403ing every genuine non-superadmin user since Part A shipped**, entirely masked because this session's own Part A verification bypassed the JWT route and inserted the test key directly via SQL. Fixed both call sites to re-load the record via `load_user_record` first, redeployed, and confirmed via the actual JWT-authenticated flow below that Enterprise gating now genuinely works.

**Live-verified against 4 of ADR-006's 5 named exit gates, all through the real dashboard-facing API — not mocks, not direct SQL:**
- **(a) registration → verified, real tool list**: registered a real MCP server (a tiny throwaway JSON-RPC echo endpoint added to avry-backend itself at `/api/v1/b5-test-mcp-echo` — chosen after a real external option, a Cloudflare Workers `*.workers.dev` deployment, turned out to be blocked by Cloudflare's own bot-challenge for datacenter-origin traffic, which would block *any* automated MCP client from a cloud VPS, not just this one — a genuine environmental finding, not a flaw in the guarded fetcher). `POST /api/v1/tenant-mcp-servers` → `201`, `status: "verified"`, real tool list `[{"name":"get_current_time",...}]`.
- **(b) SSRF rejection**: registered `https://127.0.0.1:3105/webhook` — **the exact address where this VPS's own Cerveau HAProxy listens** — got `422`, `"reason":"'127.0.0.1' resolves to a disallowed address"`, `status: "verification_failed"`, never `verified`.
- **(c) engine gate**: an agent type with no `engine='cerveau'` row got `403` ("Custom MCP servers require this agent to be running on Aivory Cerveau") before any network call.
- **(d) end-to-end turn → real `Pending` row → real approval → real execution**: asked the test tenant's `customer_service` agent to call the registered server's tool — Cerveau correctly returned `pending_approval: {risk_tier: "irreversible", tool_name: "tenant_b5verifyorders__get_current_time"}` (a mundane "get the time" tool, hard-gated purely because it came from a tenant custom server — direct proof the non-bypassable tiering from B3 is live). **First approval attempt failed** — see the real bug below. After the fix, re-ran the whole sequence: approved via the tenant-scoped `/webhook/approvals/{id}/resolve` route, got back a real executed result, `"2026-08-15T12:07:51.233870+00:00"`, pulled from the real external server.

**A second real bug, found by (d) itself failing on the first attempt — this one in Part B's own new code, not pre-existing.** `execute_approved_tool` (`zeroclaw-gateway/src/api_approvals.rs`) — the out-of-band executor used by *both* the loopback admin resolve route and the tenant-scoped webhook resume path — only ever looked up the tool's server in `config.mcp.servers`, the static `[[mcp.servers]]` list. A tenant custom server is never written there; it's synthesized fresh per-turn from `TenantCustomMcpResolver` (patch 0037). So the approval was correctly *recorded* ("outcome": "approved") but the tool never actually ran — `"no [[mcp.servers]] entry named 'tenant_b5verifyorders' in current config"`. **Patch 0038**: extracted the `McpServerConfig`-synthesis logic into a free function (`tenant_custom_mcp_server_configs`, still used by `TenantContext::custom_mcp_server_configs` too) callable without a live `TenantContext`; `execute_approved_tool` now falls back to re-resolving the tenant's servers directly from the pending-approval row's own authenticated `principal`/`agent_type` (never agent/LLM output) on a static-config miss. 3 new unit tests for the fallback's edge cases. CI green, deployed via the same checksum-verify → backup → atomic-swap → `doctor` → staged-restart recipe, both instances stable. Re-ran the full (d) sequence after deploying the fix — passed cleanly, real timestamp returned.

**(e) — the one exit gate not fully live-proven, stated honestly rather than stretched.** ADR-006's own exit-gate wording: "re-pointing a *previously verified* server's DNS at a private IP and re-running a live call proves the runtime path — not just registration-time — refuses it." This needs a DNS record under genuine, immediate control (something to flip mid-test). This session had none: the test server lived on Aivory's own fixed-DNS domain (can't safely repoint production DNS for a test); a temporary, no-login Cloudflare Workers account doesn't come with an editable zone; and this VPS has no Cloudflare API token anywhere (only a firewall-allowlist script, confirmed by grep). **What *is* proven, at the mechanism level**: `GuardedResolver::resolve()` (Rust, runtime path) calls `resolve_and_validate()` fresh on every single invocation with no caching layer anywhere in the code — confirmed by direct source reading, not inference — so there is structurally no way for a runtime call to reuse a stale, pre-rebind DNS answer from registration time. This is the same mechanism already unit-tested (13 Rust tests) and, on the Python registration-time side, already **live-tested against a real DNS-based rebind** earlier this same session (`https://127.0.0.1.nip.io/`, correctly denied). The specific gap is a live production reproduction of the full verified-then-rebind-then-runtime-call sequence — flagged as genuinely open, not assumed safe by extension.

**Cleanup, all confirmed:** `product.tenant_custom_mcp_servers` (2 rows — the verified server, the SSRF-test row), `product.agent_profiles` (2 rows), `identity.user_tiers`/`identity.users` (1 row each), `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` (1 agent + 4 memory rows) all deleted for the test tenant. The throwaway `/api/v1/b5-test-mcp-echo` route removed from avry-backend entirely and redeployed clean (confirmed `404` afterward). Two resolved `pending_approvals` rows were **deliberately left** — they live in a shared, live-write SQLite WAL file (`control_plane.db`) alongside real production approval history; touching that file directly for two harmless, already-resolved audit rows wasn't worth the risk. Final health check: both Cerveau instances stable (`NRestarts=0`), avry-backend/dashboard both `200` publicly, production `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` counts back to baseline.

**What's still open:**
1. Exit gate (e)'s full live reproduction — needs a domain with DNS the team can flip on demand (short TTL), not available this round.
2. Part A's two still-open questions (ADR-006 §"Open decisions for the user", items 2 and 4) — worth revisiting now that Part B has a second real precedent for the tenant-scoped resolve path.
3. The now-fixed JWT-tier bug (this entry, above) affected `agent_api_keys.py`'s `create_key` since Part A shipped — worth a quick audit for any *other* JWT-gated route in the codebase making the same `user.get("tier")`-off-the-raw-JWT mistake; none found in this session's own new code, but the pattern wasn't grepped exhaustively across the whole route tree.

---

**Phase 4, Parts B + C of F-1-for-approvals — designed, built, deployed, live-verified end to end (patches 0035, 0036 on Cerveau; new routes/callback-handling on avry-backend; a new route on vps-bridge; new tabs on the dashboard).** Continuation of the same-day patch 0033/0034 session. User's ask: "lanjut part B/C/D."

**Part A gap found first: the wire contract Part B needs never actually shipped.** Patches 0028–0032 (F-1-for-approvals' resume mechanism) were live, but `/webhook`'s response has always been `{"response": ..., "model": ...}` — no structured way for a channel front-end to know a turn hit a `Pending` approval; the only place a `pending_id` ever surfaced was inside the model's own free-text reply. **Patch 0035** closes this: a new task-local (`LAST_PENDING_APPROVAL`, `agent/tenant.rs`) — unlike `TENANT_CONTEXT`/`TURN_ORIGIN_CONTEXT` (set once at scope entry), this one is `Mutex`-wrapped and written to mid-turn by `approval_gate::gate_tool_approval`, the only place a `Pending` row is ever created. `handle_webhook` scopes a fresh cell per turn, takes it after, and adds an additive `"pending_approval": {id, tool_name, risk_tier} | null` field to the response. 8 new unit tests (record/take/overwrite/no-op-when-unscoped semantics); full `zeroclaw-gateway --lib` (377 tests) and workspace check clean.

**Part B — real Telegram inline Approve/Deny buttons (Telegram only this round, per the original plan; Slack deferred).** `backend/vps-bridge/telegram-agent.js` gains `createApprovalDecisionHandler` (new `POST /telegram/approval-decision`: credit-gates the continuation turn under a distinct ledger reason, `approval_resume`, added to avry-backend's `VALID_REASONS`; then calls Cerveau's tenant-scoped `POST /webhook/approvals/{id}/resolve`) and relays the new `pending_approval` field through `/telegram/message`'s own response. `backend/avry-backend/app/services/telegram_service.py` gains real `callback_query` handling (was previously dropped outright): `_handle_callback` answers the callback immediately, parses `appr:{pending_id}:approve|deny` (validated against a strict `pa_[A-Za-z0-9-]{1,64}` shape before ever touching it), calls the bridge, sends the reply, and strips the inline keyboard only once the row has genuinely left `pending` (a credit-exhaustion or transient-error reply leaves the buttons tappable so the user can retry). `_route_to_agent` now returns `{reply, pending_approval}` instead of a bare string; `_handle_message` builds a real `reply_markup` from it. All 5 production Telegram bots re-registered live with `allowed_updates` widened from `["message"]` to `["message","callback_query"]` (previously Telegram was silently filtering button taps before they ever reached the webhook — confirmed via `getWebhookInfo` before writing any code, per the plan's own instruction).

**Part C — per-agent external-toolkit scope toggle (dashboard Tools tab).** New `product.agent_tool_scope` table + `avry-backend` routes (`GET`/`PUT /api/v1/agent-profiles/{agent_type}/tools`, self-migrating, default-enabled — a row is only ever a denylist). **Patch 0036** wires it into Cerveau: `apply_toolkit_scope_gate` (`zeroclaw-config/schema.rs`) is the fourth per-server tenant-scoping step in `mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant` (now 5 args) — and deliberately the *only* fail-**open** gate in that module (every other one, including patch 0024's connection gate, is fail-closed); reuses `requires_composio_toolkit` rather than adding a new field, so native-bridge/OfficeCLI/Lightpanda/pdf-oxide stay automatically exempt with no exclusion list to maintain. New `AgentToolScopeResolver` (`zeroclaw-gateway/tenant.rs`), same bounded-TTL-LRU-over-Postgres shape as `ToolkitConnectionResolver` but keyed by the composite tenant id (`user_id.agent_type`, not `platform_user_id` alone) since the toggle is per-agent-type. `TenantContext` gains `disabled_toolkits: Vec<String>`, resolved in both places a tenant turn gets built (`handle_webhook`'s live path and `api_tenant_approvals`'s resume-continuation path, so a resumed turn respects the same toggle a fresh one would). 12 new tests (5 direct gate unit tests incl. an explicit fail-open-on-unresolved-list case, 1 end-to-end `mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant` proof, 2 `TenantContext` pass-through tests, 4 existing call sites updated for the new parameter) — all green, plus the full `zeroclaw-config`/`zeroclaw-gateway` suites (1126 + 379 tests).

**Dashboard: `CustomizeAgentModal` restructured into Identity | Connections | Tools tabs**, lazy-loading each tab's data only once opened. **Real bug caught live in the browser, not assumed fixed from reading the code**: the first cut gated each tab's fetch on "is the data still null," which is indistinguishable from "never fetched" after a *failed* fetch — a genuine 401 (this preview session has no real auth) rendered as a "Loading…" spinner that never resolved, looping the fetch forever. Fixed by tracking fetch-attempted state (`connectionsFetched`/`toolsFetched`) independently of the fetched data itself; re-verified live in the browser afterward — both tabs now correctly settle into a real error state.

**A real, pre-existing security bug found and fixed before wiring the Connections tab, unrelated to this session's own changes.** `lib/integrations/resolveUserId.ts` — used by both `/api/integrations/oauth` and `/api/integrations/oauth/connect` — trusted unverified client-supplied `x-tenant-id`/`x-user-id` headers and fell back to a literal `'default'` identity, meaning the existing `if (!userId) return 401` branches in those routes were dead code (the function never actually returned falsy): any unauthenticated or spoofed request could already reach another tenant's Composio connections. The proper replacement, `resolveIntegrationUser` (`lib/integration-auth.ts`, real JWT/session verification + paid-tier gating), already existed in the codebase from a prior session but was never actually wired into the routes. Migrated both routes to it, deleted the insecure file entirely. Found and closed one more gap in the same pass: the OAuth revoke path resolved `userId` but never checked it against the `connectedAccountId` being deleted — any authenticated user could revoke *any* user's connection by knowing or guessing its id. Fixed with an ownership check reusing the file's own existing `connectedAccounts.list({userIds})` call. **A narrower, separate nuance flagged for dedicated review, not fixed here**: `resolveIntegrationUser`'s identity source is the cross-subdomain `aivory_user` cookie (`document.cookie`-readable, unsigned) — a pre-existing, dashboard-wide trust model question (this exact dashboard also has a proper `JWT_SECRET`-verified path, `lib/serverAuth.ts`, used by other routes), not something introduced by this fix.

**Deploy discipline, all four systems, each checked for drift before touching anything — this mattered.** Diffing local against the VPS's live source before any copy caught two real divergences that would have been destructive if skipped: `avry-backend`'s `main.py` (VPS has security middleware — `PrototypePollutionGuard`/`LoginRateLimiter`/`ErrorSanitizer`, a CORS origin whitelist, and `telegram`/`slack`/`admin_users`/`entitlements`/`assessment_leads` routers — none of which exist in the local monorepo copy) and `credits.py` (VPS has admin balance-adjustment routes local doesn't). Both patched surgically in place (new router registration inserted at the right spot, `VALID_REASONS` line edited) rather than overwritten wholesale; `telegram_service.py`, `server.js`, `telegram-agent.js`, and the dashboard's changed files were confirmed to be pure additions over the VPS's current content before deploying wholesale. Cerveau patches 0035/0036 deployed with the by-now-standard recipe (checksum → backup → atomic swap → `doctor` → restart `:3100` alone with a functional check → only then `-b` → 2-min stability on each); `avry-backend`/dashboard rebuilt via `docker compose build` + `--force-recreate`; `vps-bridge` via `pm2 restart`.

**A second, genuine standing production security gap found as a side effect of testing patch 0036, unrelated to this session's own new code.** Patch 0036's toggle-off test passed cleanly on `:3100` but silently did nothing on `-b` — traced to `-b`'s `config.toml` missing `requires_composio_toolkit = "zendesk"` entirely on its Zendesk `[[mcp.servers]]` entry. This is patch 0024's own connection-gating field (customer_service tenants must have a real, connected Zendesk account before the tool is granted) — never backported to `-b` when that patch shipped 2026-08-09. **Net effect: since patch 0024, `-b` has been unconditionally granting Zendesk to every `customer_service` tenant regardless of connection status**, the exact cross-tenant over-grant patch 0024 existed to close — live on roughly half of real `customer_service` traffic via HAProxy round-robin this whole time. Fixed (the one line, on `-b`, backed up first) and re-verified with a fresh tenant showing the correct denial. **Broader drift surfaced in the same diff, deliberately not fixed this pass** (feature-parity gaps, not security): `-b` is missing `parallel_tools = true` and `[capability_graph] enabled = true` (both from the 2026-08-09 patch 0026 config update), `aivory-native-leads-qualifier__enrich_lead_contact` (parked per the Lead Enrichment on-hold decision), and still has `[browser] enabled = true` where `:3100` correctly disabled the broken native backend. A full fleet resync remains queued, separate work.

**Full live proof, patch 0036 + the new `[tool_risk_tiers]` classification below, both instances**: a fresh tenant with a real `product.agent_toolkit_connections` row and a real `product.agent_tool_scope` disabled row genuinely lost Zendesk tool visibility while its always-on native-bridge tools stayed unaffected — proven on `:3100` first, then (after the drift fix above) independently reproduced on `-b`.

**`[tool_risk_tiers].irreversible` was empty in production — the entire `Pending`-approval safety mechanism had no live trigger.** The only actions ever classified `Irreversible` were Stripe's (`STRIPE_FINALIZE_INVOICE` etc.), and Stripe was deliberately retired in the 2026-08-08 "agnostic tools" pivot (see `cerveau-tools-going-agnostic` memory) — its removal silently emptied the tier along with it, something no one had re-checked since. Surfaced while trying to live-verify Part B (nothing could ever create a `Pending` row to approve). **User's explicit call, asked directly rather than assumed**: make a real, permanent classification, not a temporary test-only flip. `composio-zendesk-support__ZENDESK_REPLY_ZENDESK_TICKET` — a real message delivered to a real customer on a real external platform, genuinely hard to unsend, the closest real analog to Stripe's original irreversible set and the exact tool the plan's own Part D design already named — moved from `reversible` to `irreversible` on both instances (and dropped from `auto_approve`, where its presence was harmless but misleading given `Irreversible` is a hard floor `auto_approve` can't override regardless). Backed up first, `doctor` clean, both restarted one at a time with 2-min stability each.

**Then the full Part B mechanism proved itself for real, against a real external system, independently verified — not the model's claim alone.** Using the same long-lived shared Composio test entity prior sessions established (`pg-test-e24bae69…`, real connected Zendesk workspace, ticket `#2`): asked the agent to reply to the real ticket — correctly blocked, `pending_approval: {id: "pa_49d04aee-…", risk_tier: "irreversible", tool_name: "composio-zendesk-support__ZENDESK_REPLY_ZENDESK_TICKET"}` came back structurally on the response, exactly patch 0035's contract. Called the new `POST /telegram/approval-decision` directly (simulating a real button tap) with `decision: "approve"` — got back `{"outcome":"approved","reply_text": "..."}`, a coherent continuation reply. **Independently re-verified via a second, separate query** (not trusting the first response): asked the agent to fetch ticket #2's comment thread fresh — the real Zendesk comment was there, exact text match, correct timestamp. This is patches 0028–0032 (resume), 0035 (structured field), and 0036 (the tool that made the safety net non-empty) all composing correctly together, through the brand-new tenant-scoped route, against a genuine external system. The one thing this can't prove standalone is Telegram's own delivery of a real human button tap — but `getWebhookInfo` already confirmed (Part B.0, before any code was written) that all 5 production bots now accept `callback_query` updates, and `_handle_callback`'s logic is a thin, already-tested parse-and-forward to this exact same resolve path.

**Security note, same session:** two more incidental secret exposures during investigation, same low-urgency class as prior ones this project has tracked (internal service keys, not customer credentials) — the OpenRouter API key (while testing the embeddings `dimensions` param earlier this session) and a Composio auth header value (while reading `-b`'s Zendesk server config block to diagnose the drift). Neither printed a value that grants access beyond what this VPS's own services already have; both worth folding into the next real credential-rotation pass.

**Patch 0033 deploy → real regression → rollback → patch 0034 (the actual fix) → clean redeploy, all same session.** Patch 0033 (CI-green per the entry below) was deployed to `:3100`/`:3101` via the standard recipe — binary swap only, `doctor` unchanged, both instances stable 2+ min post-restart. The functional check that followed (not just the stability check) is what caught it: a real `/webhook` `memory_store` call failed, and the model's own reply said so plainly ("the memory store is returning a database error"). Root-caused directly against Postgres logs, not guessed: `ERROR: expected 768 dimensions, not 1536` — `cerveau.memories.embedding` is `vector(768)` per ADR-004's deliberate storage-budget decision, but `embedding_provider="openrouter"`/`embedding_model="text-embedding-3-small"` (flipped on in an earlier session) produces 1536-dim vectors natively. This mismatch had existed since that earlier config change; patch 0033 was simply the first code ever to call the embedder on a real `store` path, so it was the first thing to hit it. Checked the blast radius before doing anything else: exactly 3 error-log lines, all one Postgres connection, all within an 11-second window matching a single webhook call's retry behavior — no other tenant, real or synthetic, touched the embedder during the ~10 minutes 0033 was live. **Rolled back both instances to the pre-0033 binary immediately** (same `.bak-pre-0033`/atomic-swap mechanism as every deploy), re-verified `memory_store` worked again (embedding NULL, as expected pre-fix), cleaned up both test tenants.

Investigated which fix was actually correct before writing any code, not just picking one: read `crates/zeroclaw-memory/src/embeddings.rs` on GitHub directly. `OpenAiEmbedding` already threads a `dims` field through its constructor and `.dimensions()` getter, and ADR-004 already documents the intended design in so many words ("Model: `text-embedding-3-small` with `dimensions=768`, Matryoshka-native reduced dims") — but `embed()`'s actual HTTP request body was only ever `{"model": ..., "input": ...}`. **The `dimensions` field was never placed on the wire at all** — a real implementation bug, not a design question to relitigate. Confirmed live against the real OpenRouter endpoint before writing the fix (not trusting docs): the identical request without `dimensions` returns a 1536-length vector; the same request with `"dimensions": 768` returns exactly 768 — OpenRouter genuinely honors OpenAI's Matryoshka truncation param as ADR-004 assumed it would. This also settled the alternative (migrate the column to `vector(1536)` instead): objectively worse, since it would silently abandon ADR-004's deliberate 4× storage-budget win without fixing the actual bug, for a column that has never had a single real row of embedded data to migrate anyway.

**Patch 0034** (commit `55ce0527`, pushed **directly** to `cerveau-main` — no VPS-bundle-relay needed, `gh auth` already carries `repo` scope from this Mac): extracted `embed()`'s body construction into `build_embed_body()`, which sets `"dimensions"` whenever `dims > 0` (0 stays omitted rather than sent as a literal `0`, which OpenAI-compatible APIs reject outright). Added 3 unit tests asserting the actual JSON shape sent (positive-dims case, dims=0 omission, multi-text input) — no network needed, a real regression guard for this exact bug class. `cargo test -p zeroclaw-memory` (23/23 in `embeddings::`) and `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` (both default and `--features memory-postgres`) clean before pushing. CI green in 24m39s.

**Deployed with an extra caution this time, given the incident:** checked `-b`'s `embedding_provider`/`embedding_model`/`embedding_dimensions`/`vector_enabled` against `:3100`'s line-for-line before touching anything (no drift, unlike the earlier documented `-b` config-drift incidents) — restarted **`:3100` alone first**, ran the full functional gate (real `memory_store` webhook call → direct Postgres query confirming `embedding IS NOT NULL` and `vector_dims(embedding) = 768`, not just "no error") before touching `-b` at all, then additionally proved **semantic recall**, not just successful storage: a second webhook call with a paraphrased query ("What language do I enjoy coding in?", never the original wording "favorite programming language") correctly answered "Rust." — proof the embedding is actually usable for similarity search, the entire point of patch 0033/0034, not just schema-valid. Only after `:3100` passed both checks and held stable 2+ minutes was `-b` restarted and independently re-verified the same way (own test tenant, own `memory_store` call, `has_embedding=t`/`dims=768` confirmed directly in Postgres). Both instances stable throughout (0 unexpected restarts, health 200, HAProxy round-robin confirmed healthy across both via `:3105`). All test tenant rows cleaned up from both runs; production `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` counts confirmed real (15/40, no test-pattern rows remaining).

**Security note, same session:** while grepping `:3100`'s `config.toml` for the OpenRouter API key to test the `dimensions` parameter live, the key's actual value was printed into a tool-output stream once (an unquoted grep match, not a deliberate echo). Low-stakes — an internal service key shared across all `[providers.models.openrouter.*]` aliases, not a customer credential — but the same class of incidental exposure this project has hit before (webhook secret, N8N_API_KEY, a live Slack bot token); every subsequent step in this session sourced it server-side into a shell variable without printing it. Worth rotating on the next pass through the §8 credential-rotation list, same as those others.

**Phase 4 exit gate closed: durable in-channel approval resume (F-1-for-approvals), patches 0028-0032, all deployed.** Phase 4's own docs flagged one explicit gap: an approved `Pending`-tier tool call executed out-of-band and never resumed the original conversation — `pending_approvals.rs`'s own module doc said so outright. Planned via `/plan` (`~/.claude/plans/functional-splashing-perlis.md`) after confirming two real architectural corrections against source rather than assumption: (1) Cerveau's webhook-driven turns have no literal saved transcript — `process_message` rebuilds `history` from scratch every call, so "resuming" means synthesizing a fresh continuation prompt (mirroring patch 0025's `continuation_drive.rs` shape), not replaying messages; (2) Telegram's `callback_query` (button taps) terminate in `avry-backend`, not `vps-bridge` — confirmed at `telegram_service.py:319-323`, which today explicitly drops them, so real inline-button wiring is separate follow-on work (Part B of the plan), not done this session. **What shipped:** `pending_approvals.rs` gained `tenant_id`/`agent_type`/`session_id`/`origin_message`/`delivered_at` columns and `insert_with_context()`; a new `TurnOriginContext` task-local (`agent/tenant.rs`) threads session/origin data alongside the existing `TenantContext` with zero signature changes to callers; a new `crates/zeroclaw-gateway/src/api_tenant_approvals.rs` holds `resolve_and_continue_tenant_approval` (reuses `execute_approved_tool` verbatim on approve, synthesizes a continuation prompt, re-resolves a fresh `TenantContext`, runs it through `process_message`) and the new tenant-scoped route `POST /webhook/approvals/{id}/resolve` (same `X-Webhook-Secret` contract as `/webhook`, plus a tenant-ownership check — 404, not 403, on mismatch); an outbound redelivery client (`reqwest`, new `gateway.approval_redelivery_url/secret` config) and a 5-minute reaper sweep (`sweep_undelivered_approvals`, 2-minute grace period, uses `tool_idem`'s new read-only `status()` lookup so the reaper can check "did this already run?" without speculatively claiming it) cover the restart-mid-flow edge case. Each patch independently CI-green and deployed to both `:3100`/`:3101` via the standard checksum-verified-download → backup → atomic swap → `doctor` → 2-min-stability recipe. Parts B (real Telegram inline buttons), C (dashboard per-agent tool-scope UI), and D (the full live-proof chain) remain genuinely open — see the plan file for the exact remaining scope.

**Real bug found and fixed: every one of the 5 Aivory agent types was leaking vanilla zeroclaw's "Aivory" self-identity, even with a dashboard-set identity.** Reported by the user: the `autonomous` agent introduced itself as "Aivory" and talked about AI-readiness assessments regardless of the identity configured in the dashboard. Root-caused through 3 layers, not assumed: the TOML `system_prompt` field looked fine, but each per-alias workspace's `SOUL.md`/`IDENTITY.md` files (a **separate, more dominant** identity-injection mechanism than `system_prompt` — not previously understood as such) still carried zeroclaw's own vanilla "you are Aivory, an AI readiness platform" self-description, verbatim, because the whole `~/.zeroclaw/` config directory had been copied wholesale at bootstrap rather than authored per-tenant. Confirmed via a checksum comparison against a fresh vanilla zeroclaw install. Fixed by rewriting `SOUL.md`/`IDENTITY.md` (except `workflow_brain`'s, which is internal-only and never customer-facing) across both `:3100` and `-b`, removing the vanilla branding while preserving disclosure/security rules — architecture question resolved explicitly with the user first: **kept the single-host-alias design** (all 5 agent types still served by one alias, `analyst_brain`, per the original patch 0011 decision), differentiating identity via per-tenant config rather than splitting into 5 aliases. Verified with a rigorous 2-persona distinctive-marker test (unguessable made-up business names/personas) before/after clearing poisoned memory, confirming the persona is now genuinely tenant-controlled, not hardcoded.

**Tool-wiring audit, same session — real drift found and fixed, not just the identity leak.** Per the user's follow-on ask ("bersihkan semua agent yang dicopy mentah mentah ... lalu check tools dan skills"), audited each of the 5 agent types' actual tool/skill bundles against what the project's own build history says they should have. Found and fixed: **retired Stripe wiring removed** (superseded by the 2026-08-08 "agnostic tools" direction — OfficeCLI + self-hosted n8n native bridge — Stripe/external-account toolkits were already deprioritized, this pass actually deleted the dead config rather than leaving it inert); the **`autonomous` bundle was missing tools entirely** on production (OfficeCLI, meeting-transcribe-adjacent tooling, and the PDF generator toolkit the user specifically flagged mid-task — `coba check, seharusnya ada officeCli ... dan ada juga tools PDF generator` — were confirmed present after the fix, not just claimed). **`-b`'s fleet drift was far worse than the surface-level diff suggested:** `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.autonomous]` and `[agent_type_skill_bundles.autonomous]` were **entirely absent** from `-b`'s config (not just missing one bundle name), meaning `autonomous` traffic landing on `-b` via HAProxy round-robin got zero tools; `lightpanda`/`pdf-oxide` binaries were never installed on `-b` at all. Full resync performed: binaries copied, all missing config sections added, verified against `:3100`'s config section-by-section rather than trusting the original narrower assumption of what was missing. (The Rust-based meeting-transcribe tool the user separately asked to be sourced — `oke carikan tools meeting transcribe yang rust tapi bagus` — was **not** researched this session; still open.)

**pgvector activated in production — a long-deferred item finally closed, then correctly implemented.** Swapped `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s Postgres image to `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` (drop-in replacement for `postgres:16`), handled the resulting collation-version mismatch (`ALTER DATABASE ... REFRESH COLLATION VERSION` + `REINDEX DATABASE`), and flipped `vector_enabled=true`/`embedding_provider="openrouter"` in Cerveau's config. **This surfaced a real, much bigger gap than "just flip the flag":** the `embedding` column existed (DDL-only, from `try_enable_pgvector`) but `store_with_agent`/`recall`/`recall_for_agents` in `crates/zeroclaw-memory/src/postgres.rs` never referenced any `EmbeddingProvider` or pgvector's `<=>` operator at all — confirmed via direct `cerveau.memories.embedding IS NULL` checks, verbose-logging with zero embed-related log lines, and a source-code grep turning up only the DDL mention. Escalated into **patch 0033**, a full embedding-wiring fix: `PostgresMemory` gained a real `embedder: Option<Arc<dyn EmbeddingProvider>>` plus `vector_weight`/`keyword_weight`; `store_with_agent` now computes and writes a real embedding; `recall`/`recall_for_agents` compute a query embedding and blend `ts_rank_cd` keyword scoring with pgvector cosine similarity (`1.0 - (m.embedding <=> $n::vector)`), with the keyword WHERE clause deliberately widened so a zero-keyword-overlap but semantically-close row isn't filtered out before scoring ever sees it — the exact case hybrid search exists for. 4 new integration tests (`pg_embedding_recall.rs`) using a deterministic fake embedder (no LLM cost) cover: real semantic recall, embedding column actually populated on store, no-embedder keyword-only regression, and failing-embedder graceful degradation.

**Two real CI failures on patch 0033, both root-caused and fixed for real — not silently marked done.** (1) **TIMESTAMPTZ parameter-type-inference bug:** switching `since`/`until` from variable-arity SQL (parameter omitted when `None`) to an always-bound `$n::TIMESTAMPTZ IS NULL OR ...` shape made Postgres infer `$n` as `timestamptz` directly from the sole cast context, which `rust-postgres`'s `Option<String>::ToSql` doesn't support — fixed with a nested `$n::TEXT::TIMESTAMPTZ` cast (infers as `text` first, matching `Option<String>` exactly), applied across both `recall` and `recall_for_agents`. (2) **A genuine race condition, found via deep diagnosis after the fix above still failed CI** — the harder, more important bug of the two. Rather than assume a second flake, reproduced the exact failing query three independent ways (raw `psql` PREPARE/EXECUTE, a minimal standalone Rust binary pinned to the same crate versions, and the real test suite itself) against the VPS's live pgvector Postgres — all passed cleanly, repeatedly (7 consecutive clean runs), which meant the bug wasn't in the SQL or the Rust binding logic at all. Root cause, found by reproducing CI's *exact* starting condition (a database where the `vector` extension has never been created — the VPS's own `aivory` DB had it installed earlier this session, which is why the first repro attempts didn't catch it): CI runs `pg_embedding_recall.rs`'s 4 `#[tokio::test]` functions in parallel, each constructing a `PostgresMemory` with `pgvector_enabled=true` against the same fresh container. `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` is not safe against a genuine first-time-creation race between concurrent sessions, and `try_enable_pgvector`'s `DO $$ ... EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS ... END $$` block silently swallowed the resulting "type vector does not exist" error from the `embedding` column's `ALTER TABLE`, so `pgvector_ready` ended up `true` even though the column was never created — violating the exact invariant the field's own doc comment promises. Reproduced deterministically on a throwaway database (2 of 3 attempts failed with CI's exact signature); fixed with a retry-on-conflict for `CREATE EXTENSION` plus a post-hoc `information_schema.columns` verification that makes `pgvector_ready` an honest signal instead of "the DDL request didn't throw"; verified the fix closes the race (5/5 clean runs against the same fresh-extension database), then confirmed CI green on the real commit (`57d74e80`). Full local suite (398 tests) and the full Postgres integration suite (`pg_lifecycle`, `pg_vector_init_thread`, `pg_v3_migration_schema_scope`, `pg_embedding_recall`) all pass. **Not yet deployed to the VPS** (`:3100`/`:3101` still run the pre-0033 binary) — the natural next step, not done this session per the user's explicit stop instruction once CI went green.

**Previous update, 2026-08-10, later (real Slack reconnect completed via the dashboard — closes the "waiting for reconnect" loop from the entry below — plus a real production-identity mixup found and fixed, and a Slack deploy-link UX improvement shipped).**

**What happened:** the user created a brand-new Slack app ("Demo App", `A0BPW8K38CQ`) intending to replace the one already wired, and spent this session's early half configuring it (manifest with OAuth redirect/event-subscription URLs, Public Distribution activated, App Home Messages Tab enabled, App icon/descriptions filled). `avry-backend`'s `.env` (`SLACK_CLIENT_ID`/`SLACK_CLIENT_SECRET`/`SLACK_SIGNING_SECRET`) was updated to what the user copied from Slack's Basic Information page, `avry-backend` force-recreated (plain `docker compose up -d` is **not** enough — Compose does not track `env_file` content for change-detection, only `--force-recreate` actually applies an `.env` edit to a running container; recorded as a new gotcha, worth adding to §7 proper next time this file is touched). The user then reconnected the real Slack workspace **"Aivory AI" (`T0BFF7YK49K`)** — the exact workspace unbound in the entry below — through the actual dashboard `Deploy → Slack` flow, and it connected successfully (`avry-backend`'s `data/slack_installations/T0BFF7YK49K.json` updated, `status: active`, bound to `user_d0985ab099ef142a`/`autonomous`).

**Real finding: the credentials actually in `.env` belong to the OLD app, not the new one.** A Slack admin page the user checked (`App Name / Workspace / App ID / Distribution Status` table) showed workspace "Aivory AI" has app **"Aivory Agent" (`A0BF382BKB9`)** installed and Publicly Distributed — not "Demo App" (`A0BPW8K38CQ`) at all. Most likely cause: the Client ID/Secret/Signing Secret the user copy-pasted into `.env` this session came from the wrong browser tab at some point (multiple app-settings tabs were open across the session, and an earlier mix-up onto `A0BF382BKB9` was already caught once — see the entry below — evidently not the only time it happened). This actually explains why OAuth worked cleanly despite all the new-app setup: `A0BF382BKB9` was **already** fully configured and Publicly Distributed from a prior session, so the connection succeeded regardless of which app's manifest/distribution work happened this session. **Net effect: `avry-backend`'s live Slack integration runs on `A0BF382BKB9` ("Aivory Agent"), the pre-existing app — not the newly-created "Demo App".** All the manifest/distribution/App Home work done on `A0BPW8K38CQ` this session is orphaned (harmless, just unused) unless a future session deliberately migrates to it.

**Dashboard UX shipped, deployed to production:** the "connected" state in the Slack deploy modal (`frontend/avry-user-dashboard/app/agents/page.tsx`) previously showed a static checkmark with no way to actually reach the agent from the dashboard. Added a **"Open chat in Slack"** link using Slack's `app_redirect` deep link (`https://slack.com/app_redirect?app=<APP_ID>&team=<TEAM_ID>`, new `buildSlackOpenUrl()` in `lib/slackDeploy.ts`) — considered a QR code first (mirroring Telegram's `react-qr-code` flow) but the user chose plain-link-only, no scanning, since the OAuth-install step already requires a real browser regardless. Also made the **existing per-deployment badge** on each agent card (`DeploymentRow`, already showed connected Slack/Telegram deployments with a disconnect button) clickable for Slack deployments specifically, so a workspace that was connected in an *earlier* session (not just the one just completed) can also reach its chat directly — the modal's "connected" view only ever rendered right after a fresh connect completed, so without this the link was otherwise unreachable for pre-existing connections. New build-time env var `NEXT_PUBLIC_SLACK_APP_ID` (not a secret — Slack App IDs are public, visible in every install/redirect URL) threaded through `Dockerfile` (`ARG`/`ENV`, was missing entirely, added alongside the existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` set) and `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `avry-user-dashboard` build args (first attempt accidentally also added it to the unrelated `avry-website` service via a `sed` match on a shared `NEXT_PUBLIC_ROADMAP_URL` line — caught and removed before building). **Real production source location correction:** the dashboard's actual deployed source is `/home/ubuntu/AVRY-V2-Main/frontend/avry-user-dashboard` (built via `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `avry-user-dashboard` service) — a *separate*, seemingly-unused standalone clone also exists at `/home/ubuntu/avry-user-dashboard` with its own `docker-compose.yml`; diffed both against local before patching, both were byte-identical pre-change, but only the `AVRY-V2-Main` copy is what's actually live (confirmed via `docker inspect`'s `com.docker.compose.project.config_files` label) — worth remembering next time a dashboard patch is needed, don't assume the standalone-named directory is the real one. Deployed 3 times this session (QR attempt → simplified to link+badge → App ID corrected to `A0BF382BKB9`), each via manual backup+swap+`docker compose build`+`--force-recreate` (not git-tracked — same patch-in-place discipline as every other VPS source edit in this project), verified healthy (`HTTP 200` on `/dashboard/agents`) and the correct App ID string confirmed present in the built JS bundle after each swap, not just assumed from the source diff.

**Not independently re-verified this session:** whether this reconnect actually routed through Cerveau (the `pm2 logs vps-bridge | grep "routing to cerveau"` check the entry below prescribes) — the reconnect succeeded and the agent responded in Slack DMs (confirmed by the user after the App Home Messages Tab fix), but the specific Cerveau-vs-legacy routing log line was not checked this time. Should still be true per the routing flag's own logic (`product.agent_profiles.engine` stayed `'cerveau'` for this `user_id`+`agent_type`, unaffected by which Slack app was used for the OAuth), but flagging as inferred, not re-confirmed, per this project's own "don't trust the unverified claim" discipline.

**Security note, same session:** while inspecting `avry-backend`'s `data/slack_installations/T0BFF7YK49K.json` to confirm the reconnect, a Python-filter command intended to redact `bot_token` from the output failed silently (the file isn't newline-delimited JSON, so the per-line `json.loads` in the filter threw, and a `||` fallback `cat` printed the raw file — including the live `xoxb-...` bot token — into a tool-output stream). Flagged to the user immediately when it happened. Low-stakes relative to the credentials in §8 (an internal/test workspace, not a paying customer's), but same class of incidental exposure as the webhook-secret slip already tracked there — rotate by disconnecting and reconnecting this Slack deployment (reinstall generates a fresh bot token) on the same pass as the other pending rotations.

**Previous update, 2026-08-10 (§8 credential-rotation status closed out; Stripe toolkit direction confirmed retired). User reported, in chat, three facts: Stripe is no longer used, the `avry-postgres` password has been rotated, and the Composio API key has been rotated. Verified read-only rather than taking the report at face value, since a coordinated-rotation miss (Postgres password updated on one side but not another) would silently break production: `zeroclaw-cerveau`/`zeroclaw-cerveau-b` both `active`, `doctor` clean (78 ok/0 errors) with zero auth/password lines in the last 2h of `journalctl`; `docker exec avry-postgres psql -U aivory -d aivory -c "select 1;"` succeeded; `avry-backend` container `Up 8 hours (healthy)`. This confirms every consumer that touches the DB password is already coordinated on the new value, not just one side. §8 updated to close out the Postgres-password and Composio-key rotation items and to record Stripe as retired rather than merely deprioritized. **One follow-up flagged, not yet actioned:** if `:3100`'s config still carries the `composio-stripe-invoicing` MCP entry for `finance_invoice_ops`, it's now dead weight (the Composio connection behind it may no longer be valid post-rotation either) — confirm with the user whether to unwire it or leave it inert.

**Previous update, 2026-08-09, later still (real Telegram + Slack bindings unbound so the dashboard's own reconnect flow can be used to verify Phase 6.1 routing end-to-end).** User's ask, after the `office_assistant` parity work below: clean up the existing real-tenant bindings so a fresh QR-scan/OAuth-install from the actual user dashboard can be used to check whether traffic is really hitting Cerveau or still the legacy engine.

All 4 real bindings that existed were removed — 3 Telegram (`sammyjason39`→`AivoryCSBot`/`customer_service`, `sammyjason39`→`AivoryAgentBot`/`autonomous`, `Lark33X`→`AivoryAgentBot`/`autonomous`) and 1 Slack (workspace "Aivory AI", `T0BFF7YK49K`, `autonomous` — same `user_d0985ab099ef142a` as the Lark33X Telegram binding). Each is just a JSON file in `avry-backend`'s file-backed store (`data/telegram_bindings/*.json`, `data/slack_installations/*.json` — confirmed by reading `telegram_service.py`'s `_handle_stop`/`slack_service.py`'s `delete_installation`, which do exactly this, nothing more, e.g. no Slack-side token revocation). All 4 backed up first to `/home/ubuntu/telegram_bindings_backup_20260809/` on the VPS before deletion, restorable if needed.

**Deliberately left untouched: `product.agent_profiles.engine` for both accounts (`user_9e552e5f601c49b8`, `user_d0985ab099ef142a`) stays `'cerveau'`.** The routing flag is keyed by `user_id`+`agent_type`, independent of the binding/chat itself — so a fresh reconnect through the dashboard (new QR scan or Slack OAuth install) will route to Cerveau on the very first message without needing to be re-flagged, which is what makes this a real verification rather than a reset-to-default. Verify with `ssh tencent-vps 'pm2 logs vps-bridge --lines 50 --nostream | grep "routing to cerveau"'` after reconnecting — a matching line confirms Cerveau; its absence (with a normal reply) means the legacy `telegram-agent.js`/OpenRouter path handled it instead.
