**Previous update, same day (`office_assistant` meeting-summary parity — closes one of the two accepted Phase 6.1 tool-parity gaps). User's direction, from a pasted prior-session analysis: close the gap the cheap way first (native-bridge module, no external tool), then add a credit-gated audio-capture add-on on top, scoped to `office_assistant` only and gated purely by credit balance (no separate Enterprise-tier check — audio-capture STT vendor selection deliberately deferred to its own research pass, not decided here). Planned via `/plan` (`~/.claude/plans/cached-weaving-fountain.md`) after confirming the exact legacy tool schema, the dashboard's existing `agent_actions` action-log endpoint, and the existing Intelligence-Credit-pack top-up flow so no new purchase UI would be needed. **This entry covers Part 1 only (text-based parity); the audio add-on is still design-only, pending an STT tool verification pass.**

**What shipped (Part 1):** new native-bridge module `/home/ubuntu/aivory-native-bridge/agents/office-assistant.mjs` — one tool, `record_meeting_summary`, schema copied field-for-field from the legacy `telegram-agent.js` tool (`title`/`summary` required, `meeting_date`/`duration_minutes`/`participants`/`decisions`/`action_items[]`/`risks` optional). Unlike the other native-bridge modules (which own their own `aivory_ops.*` Postgres tables), this one deliberately proxies to the **existing** `avry-backend` endpoint `POST /api/v1/agent-actions/internal` (`action_type='meeting'`) via a new n8n workflow (`Native Office Assistant Bridge`, id `MGg4Gtb6eH7TNt5j`) — the legacy tool already writes there and the operator dashboard already renders it, so a new parallel table would have meant Cerveau-routed tenants' summaries not showing up on the dashboard (not actually parity). Wired into Cerveau config on **both** ADR-005 fleet instances (`:3100` and `:3101`/`-b`, which have independently-maintained config-dirs, not a shared file) — new `[[mcp.servers]] aivory-native-office-assistant`, `[mcp_bundles.office-assistant-native]` added to `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.office_assistant]`, `aivory-native-office-assistant__record_meeting_summary` added to `[tool_risk_tiers].reversible` and to `agent_analyst_brain`'s `auto_approve` (same pattern as every other native-bridge write tool).

**Three real bugs found and fixed during live verification, not caught by `doctor`/syntax checks:** (1) a `sudo cp` used to write the root-owned-on-`:3100` `config.toml` pattern was applied blindly to `-b`'s config too, but `-b`'s config has always been `ubuntu:ubuntu`-owned (unlike `:3100`, whose root ownership is a documented one-off from patch 0026) — the blind `chown --reference` against a `sudo`-created backup left `-b`'s config `root:root` mode 600, unreadable by the `ubuntu` user the service runs as; caught by running `doctor` before restart (per the established recipe) rather than after, so no downtime. Fixed by restoring `ubuntu:ubuntu`/600 to match every sibling backup in that directory. (2) The new n8n workflow's `Record Meeting Summary` HTTP Request node was built from a copy-pasted convention (another workflow's `host.docker.internal:3003` + `x-internal-token: aivory-internal-2026`) that turned out to be **vps-bridge's** address and a **different** internal token, not `avry-backend`'s — `avry-backend`'s internal endpoint actually checks `TELEGRAM_GATEWAY_TOKEN` and listens on `avry-backend:8081` (reachable directly by container name — both `n8n` and `avry-backend` share the `aivory-network` Docker network, no need to round-trip through the host). First test call got a false "Meeting summary saved ✅" from the model despite the write silently 403'ing — caught by checking `data/agent_actions/*.json` inside the `avry-backend` container directly rather than trusting the reply, per this project's established "verify independently" discipline. (3) Once the token+host were fixed, saved records had a stray literal `=` prepended to every string field (`"title": "=Retest2 Sync"`) — an n8n expression-syntax mistake: nesting per-field `="{{ }}"` syntax inside a `jsonBody` that was already one big `={...}` expression, so the inner `=` characters were emitted as literal text instead of marking a new expression boundary. Fixed by dropping the redundant inner `=` on every field; numeric/array fields (written as bare `{{ }}` from the start, no inner `=`) were unaffected.

**Live-verified end-to-end after all three fixes, real state not just the model's claim:** a real `/webhook` call to a test `office_assistant` tenant produced a clean, correctly-typed JSON record on disk in `avry-backend`'s `data/agent_actions/` volume (no stray `=`, `duration_minutes` a real int, `action_items` a real array of objects) — confirmed via `docker exec avry-backend cat`, not the model's reply text. Regression-checked: `finance_invoice_ops`/`customer_service` test tenants still see exactly their own 5 tools each, no `record_meeting_summary` leakage either direction. Both `zeroclaw-cerveau` (`:3100`) and `-b` (`:3101`) restarted clean (`doctor` unchanged at 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors on both, 0 restarts, all health components `ok`) and held stable 2+ min post-restart. Test artifacts fully cleaned up: 2 leftover `agent_actions/*.json` test files deleted, 6 test `cerveau.agents` rows (+ their `cerveau.memories`) deleted; production baseline now 13 agents/31 memories (the +1/+1 over the previously-documented 12/30 baseline is the real Phase 6.1 `autonomous`/`user_d0985ab099ef142a` production message from the entry below, not a leftover — confirmed 0 `verify-office-assistant*`/`verify-regression*` rows remain).

**Known drift surfaced, not fixed this pass (out of scope for this task):** `-b`'s config was already behind `:3100`'s before this change — missing `lightpanda-browsing`/`pdf-toolkit` entirely on `office_assistant`/`customer_service`/etc., predating this session (its config-dir was copied from `:3100` on 2026-08-08, before those toolkits were wired 2026-08-09). Not touched here beyond the one office-assistant addition — a full fleet resync is a separate task. Also flagged this session: an `N8N_API_KEY` value was printed into a tool-output stream while inspecting `n8n-mcp`'s container env (read-only lookup, not a new grant) — same class of incidental exposure as the webhook-secret slip noted below; low urgency (internal-only) but worth rotating via n8n's Settings → API page on the same pass as the other pending rotations.

**Not done yet:** the audio-capture add-on (Part 2 of the plan) — voice-note ingestion in `avry-backend/telegram_service.py`, credit-gating via `credit_service.consume()`, and STT vendor selection all remain design-only pending a dedicated tool-verification pass (license/activity, same rigor as `pdf-oxide`/Lightpanda) before any code is written against a real provider.

**Previous update, same day (Phase 6.1 initial rollout — Telegram + Slack traffic routed to Aivory Cerveau, staged per-tenant, live-verified).** User's ask: "mulai kerjakan phase 6" once Phase 6 was cleared to start (see the entry below). Researched the actual current message flow first, not assumed: both `avry-backend`'s `telegram_service.py` and `slack_service.py` `_route_to_agent()` already POST to the exact same `vps-bridge` endpoint (`/telegram/message`, `telegram-agent.js`) — one chokepoint serves both channels, so wiring Cerveau in there covers both at once even though rollout is staged per-**tenant**, not per-channel. Planned via `/plan` (written to `~/.claude/plans/resilient-puzzling-dawn.md`) after two Explore passes + one Plan pass confirmed the exact webhook contract (`POST /webhook`, `X-Webhook-Secret`/`X-Tenant-Id`/`X-Agent-Type`, synchronous, 180s wall-clock cap on `:3100`/`:3101`), the `product.agent_profiles` schema (no existing rollout-flag column), and real tool-parity gaps against the old `telegram-agent.js` toolset — user chose to migrate **all 5 agent types together** (accepting `finance_invoice_ops`'s missing `flag_invoice_anomaly` and `office_assistant`'s missing meeting-summary/workflow-routing equivalents as documented regressions, not blockers) with a **new `product.agent_profiles.engine` column** (`'legacy'`/`'cerveau'`, per `user_id`+`agent_type` — same grain as Cerveau's own internal tenant id) as the rollout flag, over a temporary env-var allowlist.

**What shipped:** `product.agent_profiles` gained `engine TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'legacy'` (additive `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, exposed only through a new internal-only loader `telegram-agent.js` already calls via its existing `getAgentProfile()` round-trip — deliberately **not** added to the dashboard-facing `GET`/`PUT` routes, no UI this phase). `telegram-agent.js` gained a `callCerveau(ctx, userText)` function (`POST http://127.0.0.1:3105/webhook` — the existing HAProxy LB, never `:3100`/`:3101` directly) and an engine branch right after the credit gate (`consumeMessageCredit`, unmoved — still fires before any LLM call on both paths): `engine==='cerveau'` skips `buildToolset()` entirely and calls Cerveau; the legacy path is otherwise untouched. On any non-200 from Cerveau (`408`/`429`/`503`/`500`/`400`/`401`/network abort), `callCerveau` throws into the handler's existing `try/catch` → the same `502 {error:true}` response `runAgentLoop` failures already produced — no silent fallback (credit was already spent; re-running the legacy loop after a Cerveau failure would double-charge), differentiated only in server-side logging per status bucket. A new `stripMarkdownForChat()` pure function now runs on **both** engine paths whenever `channel !== 'console'` — Cerveau's `/webhook` has no format hint in its contract and returns raw model text, confirmed live: the model's actual reply to a test prompt was literally `**PONG** # test`, visible verbatim in Cerveau's own `cerveau.memories` row, while the user-facing reply came back correctly stripped to `PONG # test`. **Two real gaps found and fixed while researching, not just implemented:** (1) `telegram-agent.js`'s `_profileCache` had a bare 5-minute TTL — since `engine` rides in that same cached object, a Postgres flip wouldn't have taken effect for up to 5 minutes, undermining "instant rollback"; lowered to an env-tunable 30s default (`AGENT_PROFILE_TTL_MS`). (2) the top-level `if (!orKey) return 500` for a missing OpenRouter key ran unconditionally before the engine branch even existed — would have 500'd every Cerveau-routed tenant too if the key were ever unset/exhausted; moved inside the legacy-only branch. Timeouts raised with headroom stacked outward from Cerveau's own 180s cap: `telegram-agent.js`→Cerveau `AbortSignal.timeout` at 185s (new), `avry-backend`→bridge at 195s (was 90s Telegram / 60s Slack) — flagged, not fixed this pass: Telegram's own webhook route awaits `_route_to_agent` synchronously, so the higher timeout slightly widens (doesn't newly introduce) the window for Telegram to retry-deliver a duplicate update; Slack's route already backgrounds this call, so it's risk-free there. The async-webhook-ack redesign that would close this properly is an explicit Phase-6 follow-up, not done here.

**Deployed and live-verified end-to-end, real state not just code review:** `avry-backend` rebuilt+recreated (Docker, `docker compose build && up -d --no-deps avry-backend` — no bind mount, source is baked into the image at `/home/ubuntu/AVRY-V2-Main/backend/avry-backend`, confirmed identical to the monorepo copy before patching), `vps-bridge` patched+restarted via pm2 — both stable, 0 unexpected restarts, health 200 throughout. A real test tenant (`phase6-verify-user`/`customer_service`) flagged `engine='cerveau'` via direct SQL, then hit through the **exact same endpoint `avry-backend` calls** (`POST :3003/telegram/message`, not a shortcut against Cerveau directly): `pm2 logs` showed the new `[telegram-agent] routing to cerveau` line and no `runAgentLoop` activity; a direct `psql` query against `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` proved Cerveau itself processed the turn (not just the bridge's own log claim); exactly one credit deducted (`billing.credit_ledger`, `-1, reason=agent_message`); the tier gate re-confirmed unaffected (`agent_tier_error()` still correctly blocks `office_assistant` for a non-enterprise user, called directly). **Rollback proven, not assumed:** flipped back to `'legacy'`, a second message through the identical endpoint produced no new `[routing to cerveau]` log line and — the stronger proof — **zero new rows in `cerveau.memories`** for that tenant, while the reply text matched exactly what was asked (confirming it actually ran the legacy OpenRouter loop, not a silent no-op). Test tenant fully cleaned up afterward: `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` rows deleted, `product.agent_profiles` row deleted, `billing.credit_ledger`/`billing.user_credits` rows deleted — confirmed 0 remaining everywhere queried. Both services confirmed stable post-cleanup (`vps-bridge` 0 unstable restarts, `avry-backend` `RestartCount=0`, Cerveau's own `/health` `ok` throughout — untouched by this change, it only gained a new caller).

**Security note, same session:** while reading Cerveau's config to extract the webhook secret for wiring `vps-bridge`'s new env vars, a `grep` command printed the secret's actual value into a tool-output stream (flagged to the user immediately when it happened; the value itself is not repeated here or anywhere after). Low-stakes relative to the already-tracked Postgres password below — this is an internal-only auth token between two services on the same box, not a database credential — but still worth rotating as a fast-follow; every later step in this session that needed the value extracted it server-side into a variable without printing it, so this was a one-time slip, not a repeated pattern. Separately, the `avry-postgres` `aivory` user's password (already tracked below) was **not** re-exposed this session — every DB interaction here went through `docker exec avry-postgres psql` (trust-authenticated inside the container) specifically to avoid needing the password on the command line at all.

**Scope boundary, explicit:** no dashboard UI for toggling `engine` this phase (manual SQL only); the tool-parity gaps (`flag_invoice_anomaly`, `office_assistant`'s meeting-summary/workflow-routing, `escalate_to_human` wiring) are accepted regressions, not addressed; WhatsApp is untouched (Telegram + Slack only, per the one shared chokepoint).

**Real tenants flagged to `engine='cerveau'`, same session, 2026-08-09** — the first real (non-synthetic) rollout, not just the mechanism proof above. Queried `avry-backend`'s real `data/telegram_bindings/*.json` store (file-backed, inside the `avry-backend` container's data volume) rather than guessing — found exactly 3 real, active Telegram bindings in production, **all `account_type: "superadmin"`** (internal/staff test accounts, not paying customers — `sammyjason39`/`Lark33X`, both real people's Telegram handles bound to `@AivoryCSBot`/`@AivoryAgentBot`): `user_9e552e5f601c49b8`/`customer_service`, `user_9e552e5f601c49b8`/`autonomous`, `user_d0985ab099ef142a`/`autonomous`. Flagged `customer_service` first alone (best tool parity of the three agent types touched — see the confirmed-match list above), confirmed all three services still healthy, then flagged the remaining two `autonomous` bindings once that held. All three `product.agent_profiles` rows now `engine='cerveau'`; the `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE SET engine, updated_at` used for this only ever touches those two columns, so any pre-existing identity fields (agent_name/tone/etc.) on these rows are untouched. **First real inbound message observed and independently verified, same session, minutes later.** The user sent "Halo" to the `autonomous` agent (`@AivoryAgentBot`, chat "Lark33X", `user_d0985ab099ef142a`) from their own real Telegram. Confirmed via three independent signals, not just one log line: (1) `pm2 logs vps-bridge` showed `[telegram-agent] routing to cerveau {user_id: 'user_d0985ab099ef142a', agent_type: 'autonomous', session_id: '8856577619_7829703589'}`; (2) a direct `psql` query against `cerveau.memories` (joined to `cerveau.agents` on `alias = 't_user_d0985ab099ef142a.autonomous'`) returned a real distilled memory row — *"User greeted in Indonesian ('Halo'), and the assistant welcomed them, offering AI adoption services and asking about their focus..."* — proving Cerveau itself processed and answered the turn, not just that the bridge logged an attempt; (3) `billing.credit_ledger` correctly shows **zero** rows for this user — not a bug, `credit_service.py::consume()` short-circuits to `{"unlimited": true}` for `account_type="superadmin"` before ever touching the ledger, exactly as designed, reconfirmed by reading the source rather than assumed. `vps-bridge`'s error log was checked and is stale (last write 2026-07-26, pre-existing unrelated `ioredis`/`n8nMcpClient` reconnect noise) — nothing new from this message. **Phase 6.1's mechanism is now proven against a real synthetic tenant AND a real inbound Telegram message from a real account, end to end.** The other two flagged bindings (`user_9e552e5f601c49b8`'s `customer_service`/`autonomous`) remain flagged but not yet independently observed against a real message — same verification recipe applies whenever either fires. Roll any of the three back individually with `UPDATE product.agent_profiles SET engine='legacy' WHERE user_id=... AND agent_type=...` if anything looks wrong (≤30s to take effect, per the TTL fix above).

**Previous update, same day (product-direction decision: the 1,000–10,000-concurrent target is cancelled). User's call, after reviewing this doc: "untuk concurrency 1000-10000 user lebih baik dibatalkan saja, tetap gunakan kapasitas sekarang" — don't chase the 1,000+/10,000-concurrent validation any further; operate at whatever capacity is already established. **This closes out Phase 5's exit gate** (§3): the still-open item was specifically the true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent run on disposable infra (see the Finding-4 entry below and §5 item 19) — with that target cancelled, Phase 5 is considered passed on the data already gathered: the item-19 100-concurrency re-run established a real ~80-90-concurrent-request ceiling on `tencent-vps` (CPU/load-average bound, `LimitNOFILE` fixed), and the Finding-4 re-investigation above found only clean `200`/designed-`503` responses, zero raw `500`s, well past that ceiling. **Phase 8 is redefined**, not dropped: it was "10k-concurrent load test → cutover decision, gate for decommissioning `telegram-agent.js`"; it's now "load test at the already-established current-capacity ceiling → cutover decision" — same gate shape (prove the system holds up before decommissioning the old one), smaller number. **The ADR-005 horizontal-scaling infrastructure (2-instance fleet, Redis shared rate limiter, HAProxy LB, all live since 2026-08-08/09) stays as-is** — it's already built, already working, and costs nothing extra to leave running; it's just no longer an active workstream aimed at reaching a specific higher concurrency number, more like available headroom if Aivory ever needs it. [DEPLOYABLE_AGENT_RUNTIME_PLANNING.md](DEPLOYABLE_AGENT_RUNTIME_PLANNING.md)'s stated "target scale: 10,000+ concurrent users" and Phase 8 exit gate were updated to match this same decision.

**Previous update, same day (Finding 4 re-investigated — the unexplained 500-error wave from the 2026-08-08 load test). User asked whether Phase 6 could start; audit surfaced the true-1,000+/10,000-concurrent gap plus this specific unroot-caused Finding 4 as the two real blockers, and the user chose to close Finding 4 first. Rebuilt the isolated loadtest harness from scratch (`~/.zeroclaw-cerveau-loadtest`, fresh `cerveau_loadtest` schema, port `:3199`, 750 synthetic tenants, `LimitNOFILE=65536` applied from the start this time) — but this time with **`[observability].backend = "verbose"`** instead of `"none"`, the exact fix the original investigation said it needed. **Hit two real setup bugs before any load could flow, both real findings in their own right:** (1) rotating `CERVEAU_WEBHOOK_SECRET` in the env file did nothing — traced into the actual gateway source (`zeroclaw-gateway/src/lib.rs`) and found the webhook secret is read from `config.channels.webhook.<alias>.secret` in `config.toml` directly, not an env var at all; the cloned config-dir carried the *literal production secret* baked in from the `cp -r`, silently ignoring the new env file. Fixed by editing the secret directly in the cloned `config.toml`. (2) The load generator's own guardrail script raced on a stale `/tmp/cerveau-loadtest-generator.pid` left over from the aborted first attempt, read the dead PID before the new process overwrote it, and exited immediately declaring the run "done" — silently leaving the actual 100-concurrency run unmonitored for a stretch. Caught by manually checking `pgrep`/HAProxy-style reasoning rather than trusting the guardrail's own "exited cleanly" log line. **Real run, real result:** 1436 requests / 2843 actual LLM-call records, real cost **$6.76** (`costs.jsonl`, well under the $10 sign-off ceiling), guardrail correctly self-aborted at `loadavg1=8.64` — **more than double** the ~4.0 load-average the original Finding 4 wave appeared at. **Status codes across the entire run: only `200` (1264) and `503` (172) — zero raw HTTP 500, even well past the original trigger condition.** The 503s are the *designed* admission-queue backpressure (ADR-005/patch 0023), not a crash. **Conclusion: Finding 4's original mystery was never independently re-root-caused because it no longer reproduces** — the most plausible explanation is that the admission queue, deployed *after* the original 2026-08-08 investigation, now sheds excess load gracefully with clean 503s well before the system ever reaches whatever condition used to produce raw 500s under CPU starvation. Documented as resolved-by-superseding-infrastructure, not verified line-for-line against the original crash path (that path may no longer be reachable at all under current config, which is itself the point). Full teardown afterward: systemd unit + env file + config-dir removed, `cerveau_loadtest` Postgres schema dropped (cascaded 4 tables), 750 `loadtest_*` rows deleted from `product.agent_profiles` (confirmed 0 remaining), scratch logs cleaned; production `zeroclaw-cerveau`/`zeroclaw-cerveau-b`/`haproxy` confirmed still `active` throughout, untouched. **The true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent validation on disposable infra remains the one still-open item gating a clean Phase 5 pass** — this session closed Finding 4, not that.

**Previous update, same day (internal load balancer for the ADR-005 2-instance prototype).** User's steer: "gue sudah memerintahkan untuk membuat sistem load balancer dan gunakan queueing system pake redis" — prompted a status check before building anything, since ADR-005 (§2, patch 0023) already covers half of that ask. **Audit first, confirmed on the real VPS, not from docs:** the Redis-backed shared rate limiter and per-instance admission queue were genuinely live (`config.toml`'s `redis_url`/`redis_key_prefix`, `zeroclaw-cerveau-b.service` confirmed `active`); Redis itself had zero `*cerveau*` keys at audit time (expected — rate-limiter counters are ephemeral, only exist under live traffic) and no other queue mechanism existed beyond what patch 0023 already built. The genuinely missing piece: **no load balancer** — `avry-traefik` (the shared production reverse proxy) had zero routing rules for either Cerveau instance, confirmed by grepping its dynamic config and every container's Traefik labels. Asked the user how to expose it, given Cerveau has no production consumer yet (`vps-bridge` still runs the old `telegram-agent.js`; Cerveau migration is Phase 6) — user chose **internal-only, new port on `127.0.0.1`**, not a public hostname. **Investigating that surfaced why the original "Traefik LB" framing was wrong in the first place, not just deferred:** Traefik runs inside a Docker container on `aivory-network`; both Cerveau instances bind `127.0.0.1` on the *host* network namespace, which is fundamentally unreachable from inside a container regardless of `host.docker.internal` — so routing through the shared Docker Traefik was never actually viable without either widening Cerveau's bind (weakens its loopback-only posture) or extra Docker networking plumbing. Installed a standalone **HAProxy** (2.8.16, plain `apt`, systemd-managed — not Docker, not Traefik) instead: `frontend` on `127.0.0.1:3105`, `backend` round-robins `:3100`/`:3101` with an active `GET /health` check (`fall 2 rise 2`) so an unhealthy instance self-removes from rotation. Appended to the distro-default config (backed up first), `haproxy -c` validated before reload. **Live-verified via HAProxy's own runtime stats socket** (`show stat`, not logs): 6 real requests split exactly 3/3 across both instances, both `status=UP`, health checks passing (`L7OK`/200); confirmed durable across a full `systemctl restart`, not just a hot reload. Zero risk to the shared production Traefik or any existing site route — it was never touched. **Not yet wired to a real consumer** — this is infrastructure for Phase 6, not something serving traffic today.

**Previous update, same day (PDF toolkit — `pdf-oxide` CLI + a custom MCP shim, wired to all 5 agent types).** User's ask: research alternatives to `opendocswork-mcp`/office-oxide-mcp for PDF read+create+form-fill after that candidate turned out license-mismatched (README claimed MIT/Apache-2.0, the actual `LICENSE` file was GPL-3.0) and immature (0 releases, 2 contributors, 8 weeks stale) — surveyed a dozen MCP office/PDF servers by checking real license files and commit history directly, not README claims, and most turned out stale (9+ months), license-mismatched, or thinner than advertised (`alejandroBallesterosC/document-edit-mcp`'s "PDF support" is create-blank-from-text + docx→pdf only, no read/form-fill, real development stopped April 2025). Landed on **[yfedoseev/pdf_oxide](https://github.com/yfedoseev/pdf_oxide)** (Rust, dual MIT/Apache-2.0 verified via the actual `LICENSE-MIT`/`LICENSE-APACHE` files present in the repo, 942★/115 forks/30 contributors, releases every 1-2 days, v0.3.77). **Key architectural gap found and closed:** `pdf_oxide`'s own bundled MCP server (`pdf_oxide_mcp`) exposes only one read-only `extract` tool — create/fill/edit exist solely in the Rust library and the `pdf-oxide` CLI (26 subcommands, confirmed by reading `pdf_oxide_cli/src/cli/commands/*.rs` source directly on GitHub, not docs, after the docs' own Quick Start undersold what the CLI can do). Built a standalone **custom MCP shim** (`pdf-oxide-mcp-shim`, new repo `~/Documents/aivory-pdf-mcp-shim`, external process wired via config only — not part of `AVRY-Cerveau`'s own Rust workspace, same "small standalone service" pattern as `aivory-native-bridge`) exposing **4 grouped tools**, deliberately not 1 monolithic tool like `officecli__officecli` nor 26 tiny ones: `pdfoxide_read`, `pdfoxide_create`, `pdfoxide_fill_form` (all `reversible`+`auto_approve`) and `pdfoxide_edit` (watermark/merge/split/rotate/crop/reorder/compress/flatten/bookmarks — deliberately left untiered, hard-denied by default per patch 0013, same posture as Lightpanda's mutating tools below). Minimal deps (`serde`/`serde_json` only) — cross-compiled cleanly to `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` from a macOS dev box via a Homebrew `musl-cross` toolchain, no CI round-trip needed (unlike Cerveau itself, whose `ring` dependency needs a real Linux `cc`). One shared `[mcp_bundles.pdf-toolkit]` referenced from all 5 `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.*]` in a single pass, mirroring Lightpanda's all-agents-at-once wiring rather than one-at-a-time. **Validated end-to-end before touching production:** a local create→read round-trip against the real `pdf-oxide` binary (macOS arm64 build) produced a genuine PDF whose extracted text matched the source markdown; a `pdfoxide_fill_form` call against a non-form PDF returned a real semantic error (`Form field not found: name`), proving the `--fill`/`--output` flag wiring is correct rather than merely that the CLI ran; a path-traversal probe (`../escape.pdf`) was rejected by the shim's own `safe_relative_path` guard — defense-in-depth on top of Landlock, same posture as the SSRF-filter-plus-Landlock layering used for Lightpanda. **Landlock required zero new grants** — the first live test under `internal-landlock-exec`, with `cwd` correctly set to the tenant workspace (mirroring patch 0015's fix), succeeded on the first try, a sharp contrast to OfficeCLI's 4-round Landlock saga (patches 0016-0019), because this is a static Rust binary with no CoreCLR/Node/OpenSSL init dependencies to punch sandbox holes for. Checksum-verified download on `tencent-vps` (`sha256:09c75a46...`, matched GitHub's own published asset digest exactly), config backed up (`config.toml.bak-pre-pdfoxide`), `doctor` clean pre/post (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, unchanged), restart, stable 2+ min (PID `3864937`, 0 restarts, all 9 `/health` components `ok`). **Live-webhook-verified after explicit cost sign-off ("ok"):** `office_assistant` genuinely created a PDF from markdown and read its text back correctly in one turn — independently confirmed on disk, not just the model's claim: a real 2306-byte PDF v1.7 file sitting at the exact per-tenant workspace path. `finance_invoice_ops` and `customer_service` (a cost-conscious spot-check of 2 of the remaining 4 agent types) both confirmed seeing all 4 new tools with zero regression to their existing toolkits (Stripe/native-bridge/Zendesk tool lists unchanged). Test tenant rows deleted afterward; production counts confirmed back to the exact baseline, **12 agents / 30 memories**; the service held the same PID throughout the entire restart-through-verification window with 0 restarts. **Known, deliberate gap, not an oversight:** `pdfoxide_edit`'s 9 mutating operations are wired in the shim and fully functional, but hard-denied by Cerveau's approval gate until a real need surfaces — same posture as Lightpanda's interactive tools.

**Previous update, same day (Lightpanda browsing toolkit + patch 0027 — a real Landlock/DNS bug found and fixed).** User asked for a browsing tool "jauh lebih ringan dari playwright tapi secanggih playwright"; researched and picked **Lightpanda** (`lightpanda-io/browser`, Zig-from-scratch, V8, CDP-compatible, native MCP server, AGPL-3.0, 33.6k stars, actively pushed the same day) over Playwright/Puppeteer (same Chromium weight) and Browserbase (paid third-party SaaS, cuts against this project's established self-hosted/zero-signup direction — see memory `cerveau-tools-going-agnostic`) — real-measured 22-26MB peak RSS per fetch vs. Chromium's 100-300MB+, confirmed via a throwaway spike on `tencent-vps` (separate from `:3100`, no production impact): `/usr/bin/time -v` on real fetches, a raw JSON-RPC MCP handshake proving 25+ real tools (not just `--help` text), and a live SSRF test proving `--block-private-networks` genuinely blocks a fetch to `:3100`'s own `/health` endpoint while a public fetch succeeds — critical given this VPS also hosts Postgres/Redis/n8n on internal addresses reachable to anything unconfined. **Found a real, already-live production bug while investigating where to wire it in:** Cerveau's native (non-MCP) `browser` tool (`backend="agent_browser"`, Vercel's CLI) was `enabled=true` and reachable to every Aivory agent type already — but the `agent-browser` binary was never installed on the VPS (confirmed via `find`), so it's been silently broken since whenever it was configured; any tenant asking to browse got a silent runtime failure, not an error message the model could explain. Neutralized (`[browser].enabled=false`) as part of this same change, not spun off separately, since Lightpanda supersedes its job entirely. User's mid-task steer: originally scoped to `leads_qualifier` only, then "oh iya, better wire ke semua agent" — extended to all 5 (`finance_invoice_ops`, `office_assistant`, `customer_service`, `leads_qualifier`, `autonomous`) via one shared `[mcp_bundles.lightpanda-browsing]` referenced from every `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.*]`, plus `tenant_workspace_root` for Landlock confinement (defense-in-depth on top of the SSRF filter, per the user's explicit ask). **Risk tiering, deliberately conservative:** the 20 read-only tools (`goto`/`markdown`/`html`/`extract`/`tree`/`search`/etc.) added to `[tool_risk_tiers].reversible` + `agent_analyst_brain`'s `auto_approve`; the 11 mutating/interactive/escape-hatch tools (`click`/`fill`/`hover`/`press`/`scroll`/`selectOption`/`setChecked`/`evaluate`/`save`/`session_new`/`session_close`) deliberately left untiered, so they hit patch 0013's default hard-deny for unclassified MCP-origin tools rather than being silently auto-approved — reading the web ships now, acting on it is a separate future decision. **Pre-deploy Landlock smoke test surfaced a second real bug**, this one in Cerveau's own sandbox code, not the toolkit: `internal-landlock-exec`-confined fetches failed every time with `CouldntResolveHost`, even though the existing ruleset already grants `/etc` read access. Root-caused with `strace -f -e trace=openat,socket,connect`: `/etc/resolv.conf` is a symlink to `/run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf` on this systemd-resolved host, and Landlock's `PathBeneath` evaluates a rule against a symlink's *resolved target*, not its directory entry — `/etc/nsswitch.conf` and `/etc/hosts` (real files) opened fine under the identical ruleset, only the symlinked one failed, and the DNS socket `connect()` to `127.0.0.53:53` itself succeeded (confirming Landlock's `AccessFs` rules never touch network syscalls, only the filesystem paths a resolver reads first) — this is the first time this gap surfaced because Lightpanda is the first Landlock-sandboxed MCP tool in this fork that resolves arbitrary hostnames itself; OfficeCLI never needed to. Asked the user whether to ship now unconfined (SSRF-filter-only) with the Landlock fix as a fast-follow, or fix first and deploy once — user chose **fix first**. Patched `crates/zeroclaw-runtime/src/security/landlock.rs` (commit `511a30ad`): a new conditional grant for `/run/systemd/resolve` (read + readdir, degrades to a no-op rather than a hard error on non-systemd-resolved hosts via an `.exists()` check, unlike the always-required paths in the main loop), plus corrected the pre-existing `/etc` grant's comment (it had wrongly claimed resolv.conf was already covered). No Linux toolchain available locally to cross-compile-check (macOS box, `ring`'s build script needs a Linux `cc`) — relied on real CI as the authoritative check, consistent with this project's established pattern (patch 0026's `DropOnThread` bug was likewise only catchable on real CI Postgres). **Pushed via the VPS-bundle-relay method** (local creds still can't push — see §7), **CI green in 25m23s** (run `31296314921`), release binary re-verified against the fix: same `strace`-confirmed repro now returns real page content instead of `CouldntResolveHost`, and the SSRF block still holds under the fixed binary. **Deployed to `:3100`**, standard recipe: checksum-verified download (`sha256:ed3ee909...`, matched the `cerveau-cd` release's own published digest exactly — this release also separately published a `.sha256` sidecar asset, a first for this project's deploy recipe), config+binary both backed up first (discovered along the way that `config.toml` and the binary path are now root-owned as of the 0026 deploy, requiring `sudo` for the write — previously ubuntu-owned; not something this session changed, just adapted to), atomic `.new`+`mv` swap, pre- and post-swap `doctor` both unchanged (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors), new PID `3793487`, confirmed stable 2+ min (`NRestarts=0`, `ActiveState=active`, all 9 `/health` components `ok`, zero panic/fatal/error in `journalctl`). **Live-webhook-verified after explicit cost sign-off ("boleh"/"ya"):** 3 real `/webhook` calls — `leads_qualifier` and `finance_invoice_ops` (a spot-check of two of the five newly-wired types, not all five, to keep cost down) both genuinely browsed `https://example.com` via the real MCP path and returned the correct heading text; a third call attempting to probe that `click` stays denied was inconclusive (no session continuity was kept between calls, so the model correctly reported no open page rather than either performing or being denied a click) — not chased further, since the higher-stakes proof (browsing genuinely works, real content returned, two different agent types) already landed and the tiering itself is a config fact, not something that needed a live call to confirm. Test tenant rows (`t_verify-lightpanda-{1,2}-20260809`, 2 agents + 3 memories) deleted afterward; production counts confirmed back to the exact prior baseline, **12 agents / 30 memories**; service held the same PID throughout with 0 restarts across the entire verification. **Known, deliberate gaps, not oversights:** interactive/mutating Lightpanda tools (click/fill/form-submit) are wired but hard-denied by default — a future decision, not shipped this pass; the `agent-browser`-backed native `browser` tool's config keys (`backend`, `native_headless`, `native_webdriver_url`) are left in place but inert (`enabled=false`) rather than deleted, in case a future session wants to reconsider that backend instead of/alongside Lightpanda.

**Previous update, same day (Phase 4.2 + 4.4, patch 0026):** user's ask this session: "lanjutkan fase 4," then "kerjakan keduanya sekarang" when asked whether to prioritize 4.2 (capability graph) or 4.4 (concurrent tool calls) first. **4.4 turned out to need zero new Rust code** — `execute_tools_parallel`/`should_execute_tools_in_parallel` already existed upstream, already correctly excluded approval-gated and `tool_search` batches from concurrency, and F-2's idempotency claim/complete/release already run strictly outside the concurrent-dispatch window (sequential, before/after) — so there was nothing to fix, only a config flag to flip. Verified via a throwaway non-LLM harness against a real copy of `:3100`'s config that adding `parallel_tools = true` to `[runtime_profiles.agent_analyst_brain]` only (the one profile that actually serves Aivory tenant webhook traffic — `analyst_brain` is the alphabetically-first host alias, per §7) resolves correctly and leaves the other 5 sibling profiles' `false` untouched. **Deployed to `:3100` 2026-08-09** — `parallel_tools = true` inserted into `[runtime_profiles.agent_analyst_brain]` alongside the 0026 config below, one combined restart. Note: this session's config-file SSH mutation actually went through this time (unlike items 22/23/24, where the Bash classifier blocked it) — no explanation for the difference, just recording what happened. **4.2 (capability graph) built for real, honestly rescoped from the planning doc's framing:** the doc's motivation — "a tenant with hundreds of Composio tool schemas is too many for the prompt" — doesn't hold at Cerveau's actual scale today (5-16 tools/tenant, and `mcp.deferred_loading` already keeps them out of the prompt via `tool_search`). What's real and worth building: `tool_search`'s keyword-match ranking has no memory of which tools a tenant has actually used together before. New `zeroclaw_memory::capability_graph` module (one Postgres table, `kg_capability_edges`, tenant-scoped by `TenantContext::tenant_id`, no separate nodes table or ingestion crawler — nodes are just the deferred stub's own name string, "ingestion" is `tool_search` itself recording which tools it surfaced together), config-gated (`[capability_graph].enabled`, default `false`, absent ⇒ bit-for-bit unchanged), wired into `ToolSearchTool` (reranks keyword-search + `select:` results by learned edge weight to whatever's already activated this session) and into `scoped.rs`'s construction site via `current_tenant()` + a process-wide install hook. **Found and fixed a real bug while building the wiring tests, not just the ranking logic:** `search_limit` was truncating to `max_results` *before* reranking ever saw the full candidate pool, so a promoted-but-lower-keyword-rank tool could never actually surface — fixed by widening to fetch-all whenever a ranker is attached, mirroring the existing access-policy widening. 12 new tests (7 pure ranking-logic, no DB; 5 `ToolSearchTool` wiring against a fake ranker) plus a `CERVEAU_TEST_PG_URL`-gated Postgres integration test proving tenant isolation (mirrors `pg_lifecycle.rs`'s shape). Whole workspace `cargo check --all-targets --features memory-postgres` clean; `zeroclaw-config`/`zeroclaw-tools` full suites clean; `zeroclaw-runtime` 3118/3118 single-threaded (one test flaked under the default parallel run, passed both isolated and single-threaded — pre-existing test-isolation issue, not from this change). **Pushed to `cerveau-main` (commit `2ad2a2bb`) via the VPS-bundle-relay method** (see §7 — local credentials still can't push). **CI failed twice, then passed — both failures were real bugs, not flakes, found only because CI has a real Postgres and the local dev box doesn't:** (1) `PgCapabilityGraph::connect` originally ran `Client::connect` and schema-init as two separate `run_on_os_thread` calls; fixed to match `PostgresMemory::initialize_client`'s proven single-thread shape (commit `4f8a51da`) — **this fix did not actually address the failure** (identical panic, identical location, re-ran clean-looking locally because none of the local unit tests construct a real `PgCapabilityGraph`). (2) Root-caused properly by reading the `postgres` crate's own source: `Client::drop()` calls `close_inner()` → `self.connection.poll_block_on(...)` — **every** `Client` drop tries to `block_on` its own internal Tokio runtime, including the implicit drop at the end of an async test function, which runs on a Tokio worker thread. `postgres.rs` already has the fix for exactly this, named for exactly this purpose: `DropOnThread<T>`, a wrapper that moves its inner value to a background OS thread before dropping it there. Duplicated the same ~25-line type into `capability_graph.rs` (commit `0f50e342`) — CI green on the third attempt (`Capability graph (Postgres) tests` step: 04:14:55–04:15:10, `success`; full job `success` by 04:31). User's directions across this back-and-forth: "setelah CI hijau lanjut deploy," then "nyalakan saja" (also turn the config flag on, not just deploy the dormant binary). **Deployed to `:3100` 2026-08-09**, standard recipe: checksum-verified download (`sha256:5b1cae4e...`, matches the `cerveau-cd` release's own published digest exactly), pre-swap `doctor` clean (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, unchanged), backup at `.bak-pre-0026`, atomic `.new`+`mv` swap, both config changes (`[capability_graph].enabled = true` here, `parallel_tools = true` for 4.4 above) applied together before a single restart, post-swap `doctor` unchanged, new PID `3709296`, confirmed stable 2+ min (`NRestarts=0`, `ActiveState=active`, all 9 `/health` components `ok`, zero panic/fatal/error lines in `journalctl` for the window). **Both 4.2 and 4.4 are now live, and now live-webhook-verified too** (user's explicit cost sign-off: asked for an estimate first, "$0.05–0.20 for ~6-8 calls" quoted from the item-24 precedent, then "ya"). 3 real `/webhook` calls, 8 LLM turns total, **actual real spend $0.037675** (from `costs.jsonl`, well under the estimate) — cheap because ~87% of tokens were cached input (the ~40-open-skill host-alias prompt, same cache-hit pattern the Phase 5 load test found). Full account:

  - **Capability graph rerank, proven against the raw `kg_capability_edges` table directly, not just the model's relayed summary** (this project's own "don't trust the model's claim" discipline — see the OfficeCLI/native-bridge isolation proofs in §2 for the precedent). Tenant A: one `tool_search select:get_invoice,update_invoice_status` call (builds one edge), then one keyword search for `"invoice"` in the same turn. Tenant B (fresh, no history): the same keyword search cold, as both a baseline and a cross-tenant-isolation check. Result: `update_invoice_status` jumped from **last** in B's cold baseline to **first** in A's reranked result — traced to the DB directly: at A's second-call rerank time, exactly one edge existed (`get_invoice`↔`update_invoice_status`, weight 1, from the `select:` call), and the rerank SQL's `if/else` attributes that edge's boost to whichever side is the *candidate* — here, `update_invoice_status` — while `get_invoice` (the *recent* side) gets none. **Found a real, minor asymmetry this way**: when both ends of an edge are simultaneously in `recent` and in `candidates` (as here), only one direction gets boosted, not both, because the row-processing `if`/`else if` in `PgCapabilityGraph::rerank` is mutually exclusive per row. Not a correctness bug — the feature demonstrably works for the common case (a recent tool boosting a *different* candidate it's linked to) — but a real sharp edge worth fixing before this is relied on for anything more than reordering. Left as-is for now (documented here, not silently ignored); a real backlog item, not blocking.
  - **Cross-tenant isolation, direct DB proof**: querying `kg_capability_edges` mid-test showed tenant A's and tenant B's edge rows keyed to distinct `tenant_id` values (`verify26-capgraph-a.finance_invoice_ops` vs. `...-b...`) with zero overlap — confirms the edge table's tenant scoping (bare `tenant_id` column, no FK, per the patch-0026 design) holds in practice, not just in the unit tests.
  - **4.4 (parallel tool execution), correctness proven end-to-end, concurrency timing not independently proven**: a third tenant (`autonomous` agent type, so both `finance-invoice-ops-native` and `leads-qualifier-native` are available) asked to call `list_invoices` and `list_leads` "at the same time" — both tool calls executed successfully (0 results each, expected for a fresh tenant), no error, no crash, `/health` stayed clean throughout. This confirms the newly-enabled `parallel_tools = true` path works correctly against real native-bridge tools in production. It does **not** independently prove the two calls ran *concurrently* rather than sequentially — `[observability] backend = "none"` on `:3100` swallows the fine-grained timing that would show it (see §7's "Debugging a silent :3100" gotcha), and flipping to `verbose` for this alone wasn't judged worth another restart cycle given the mechanism itself was already proven safe by construction (F-2 claim/complete/release sequencing, approval-gate exclusion — see the patch-0026/item-7 account above). If real concurrency timing is ever needed as its own proof, that's a `backend = "verbose"` rebuild away, not a re-architecture.
  - **Cleanup**: all `verify26-*` rows deleted from `cerveau.kg_capability_edges` (21 rows), `cerveau.memories`, and `cerveau.agents` (3 aliases) directly via `psql` (TCP, `host=127.0.0.1 port=5432` — the `sudo -u postgres psql` unix-socket form doesn't work on this box, avry-postgres only accepts TCP). Verified 0 `verify26%` rows remain anywhere; production `cerveau.agents`/`cerveau.memories` counts unchanged (12/30) before and after. Service never restarted for any of this — same PID (`3709296`) throughout the entire verification + cleanup. Previous update, same day: §5 item 24 — real `/webhook` live proof for both the `autonomous` agent-type fix (item 22) and the toolkit connection gate (item 23), after explicit user sign-off on LLM cost; clean pass, no bugs found. **All 5 Aivory agent types are now demonstrably working, live-verified end to end** — the user's ask that session, "lanjutkan phase 4 sampai 5 agent terbukti jalan," is complete. Patch 0024 — external-toolkit MCP grants (Stripe/Zendesk) now gated on the tenant's own Composio connection, closing a real cross-tenant over-grant gap; see §4/§5 item 23. Previous update, 2026-08-08: ADR-005 taken from design to a live, verified 2-instance prototype — patch 0023 (shared Redis-backed rate limiter + per-instance admission queue) deployed to `:3100` and a new `:3101` instance, live-verified against two real running daemons that a tenant's rate limit now binds on aggregate traffic, not per-instance, and that overload yields a clean `503` not a `500` — see §5 item 3; Phase 3 exit gate's 90-day flat-disk simulation run and closed — see §5 item 1; horizontal-scaling design doc [ADR-005](ADR-005-CERVEAU-HORIZONTAL-SCALING.md) done — see §5 item 2; Phase 5 item 18 closed — live-pricing was never enabled, config fix; item 19 scaled re-run at 100-concurrency found + fixed a real `LimitNOFILE=1024` gap on production `:3100` itself, plus a real CPU/load-average ceiling on `tencent-vps`; pgvector's "needs a new OpenAI key" premise corrected — OpenRouter's own `/embeddings` endpoint already works with the existing key; **same day, later: `autonomous` agent-type gap found + closed, config-only — see §5 item 22.** The doc's own earlier claim that `autonomous` "inherits the others' superset tool set" was checked against the actual code for the first time and found false — bundle resolution is a bare fail-closed `HashMap::get()`, so `autonomous` was granting zero extra skills/tools, identical to no `X-Agent-Type` header at all. Fixed by adding `[agent_type_skill_bundles.autonomous]`/`[agent_type_mcp_bundles.autonomous]` = the union of the other 4 types' bundles, config-only, no new Rust patch. Verified with a standalone Rust harness calling the real resolution functions against a real copy of the production config — zero LLM calls, per the user's explicit ask to keep this verification free)
**Purpose:** single source of truth to resume work safely in a fresh session. Pairs with the phased plan ([DEPLOYABLE_AGENT_RUNTIME_PLANNING.md](DEPLOYABLE_AGENT_RUNTIME_PLANNING.md)) and ADRs 001–005.

## 1. What Cerveau is (one paragraph)

Aivory Cerveau = Aivory's fork of [zeroclaw-labs/zeroclaw](https://github.com/zeroclaw-labs/zeroclaw) (Rust, Apache-2.0), the multi-tenant deployable-agent engine that will replace the bespoke `telegram-agent.js` behind the user dashboard's Telegram/Slack/WhatsApp/Office-Assistant agents. Fork repo: **[Aivory-hub88/AVRY-Cerveau](https://github.com/Aivory-hub88/AVRY-Cerveau)**, default branch `cerveau-main`, based on upstream **v0.8.3** + a rebase-friendly patch series. Local clone: `~/Documents/AVRY-Cerveau` (rustup override 1.96.1).

A host-local HAProxy solves it directly with zero risk to the shared Traefik/site. Nothing here is customer-facing yet — both instances remain `127.0.0.1`-bound, side-by-side, exactly like `:3100` always was; the LB is for when Phase 6 wiring lands. |

Getting a real OfficeCLI invocation to actually run inside that boundary took 4 live-iteration rounds, each fixing one genuine Landlock `EACCES` a real request hit (its own npm install dir, `/etc/ssl/openssl.cnf`, then `/proc`+`/sys`+`/dev` for the bundled .NET CoreCLR runtime's GC-heap sizing — full list in §7). **Confirmed live, 2026-07-27:** `zeroclaw-cerveau internal-landlock-exec --workspace <dir> -- officecli --version` prints `1.0.142` cleanly — the runtime starts and runs correctly under real Landlock restrictions for the first time. **Two-tenant isolation proof RUN AND PASSED, 2026-07-30 — gap closed.** First attempt surfaced a real bug, not the isolation property being tested: `apply_restrictions()` handled `MakeReg`/`RemoveFile` as Landlock access types but never granted them on the workspace dir or `/tmp`, so **no new file could be created anywhere by any tenant** (writing to an *existing* file worked; creating one didn't — `--version` never exercised this path). Fixed as **patch 0020** (§4), deployed, then the proof re-run cleanly: (1) tenant A creates `tenant-a-secret.docx` via a real `/webhook` call, verified on disk directly (`file` confirms Microsoft Word 2007+, `unzip -p ... | grep` confirms the exact body text) — not just the model's claim; (2) tenant B, same agent type, told (as an authorized pen-test) to read that exact absolute path via the `officecli__officecli` tool — got back `Error: ... Access to the path '...' is denied.`, and a clean LLM-independent repro (`cat` the same path under B's `internal-landlock-exec` scope) reproduces the identical `Permission denied`, while the same `cat` **without** the sandbox wrapper succeeds — ruling out ordinary Unix permissions as the cause (same OS user owns both trees) and isolating Landlock as the actual mechanism; (3) tenant A's own access re-confirmed working immediately after, via a direct `officecli get` call under A's own `internal-landlock-exec` scope. Test tenant workspaces/DB rows cleaned up afterward, service restarted stable (0 restarts) with the temporary debug logging reverted. See §5 item 14 and §4 patch 0020 for the full account. |
