Real Composio MCP server `e3a09368-90d6-4856-b200-5cef983e3081` created via the REST API directly (`@composio/client` SDK wasn't installed on the VPS; docs pages 404'd for non-browser fetches, so — same as OfficeCLI's docs before — ground truth came from calling the endpoint and reading the actual response, not the docs), curated to 5 actions verified live against the real tools list: `ZENDESK_LIST_ZENDESK_TICKETS`, `ZENDESK_GET_ZENDESK_TICKET_BY_ID`, `ZENDESK_REPLY_ZENDESK_TICKET`, `ZENDESK_CREATE_ZENDESK_TICKET`, `ZENDESK_GET_ABOUT_ME` — `ZENDESK_DELETE_ZENDESK_TICKET` and the organization-management actions deliberately left uncurated. `[[mcp.servers]]` entry `composio-zendesk-support` (`transport = "http"`, same `tenant_entity_query_param = "user_id"` mechanism as Stripe) granted only to `customer_service` via `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.customer_service]`. **Found a real gap along the way:** all 3 read-only actions initially got auto-denied on the webhook path despite being untagged in `[tool_risk_tiers]` — turns out unclassified *MCP-origin* tools (any `server__tool` name) are never `Safe` by default regardless of tiering, only non-MCP tools are (`Config::tool_risk_tier`'s doc comment: "a newly-wired toolkit is supervised until someone explicitly reclassifies it"); Stripe/OfficeCLI never hit this because every action they exposed was already a mutating one explicitly tiered. Fixed by adding all 5 curated actions to both `reversible` and `auto_approve` (previously only the 2 writes were). **Live-verified, same rigor as Stripe/OfficeCLI:** visibility — `customer_service` sees exactly its 5 tools; `finance_invoice_ops` and `office_assistant` (regression-checked) see none of them and still see their own tools correctly. Execution — asked `customer_service` (as the connected entity) to list open tickets; got back the real live ticket (`#2`, "SAMPLE generated by AI: IC balance not updating after workflow build") verbatim, matching Zendesk's own UI. **Operational gotcha hit during setup, worth remembering:** the Composio "Connect Account" subdomain field must be the bare subdomain (`aivoryid`), not the full `aivoryid.zendesk.com` — entering the full domain produces a doubled-domain OAuth URL (`aivoryid.zendesk.com/.zendesk.com/oauth/...`) that DNS-fails; left 3 stale `EXPIRED` connected-account rows in Composio from that (harmless, just clutter — the real one is `ca_1FEXAuwRrk3c`). |
| **Native tools bridge (no external account needed)** | **Built and live-verified end-to-end, 2026-07-30/31, all 3 currently-toolkit-eligible agent types.** Motivation: every toolkit above (Stripe/OfficeCLI/Zendesk) requires the *tenant* to already own an external account — real friction (the Zendesk OAuth setup alone burned 30+ minutes this session). Explored self-hosting Chatwoot/Twenty CRM/Dolibarr as a zero-signup alternative; ruled out (no VPS headroom for three more heavy stacks, Twenty has no workspace-creation API, Dolibarr's Multi-Company isolation is a paid add-on). Landed on: **`aivory-native-bridge`**, a small standalone Node/TypeScript service (`/home/ubuntu/aivory-native-bridge`, systemd unit `aivory-native-bridge.service`, ~30MB RSS, listens on `127.0.0.1:4100`) that speaks real MCP protocol (official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, `StreamableHTTPServerTransport`) and proxies to plain n8n **Webhook** workflows backed by a new Postgres schema, `aivory_ops` (`tickets`/`ticket_comments`, `leads`, `invoices`/`invoice_lines` — all `tenant_id`-scoped, same discipline as `cerveau.memories`). Wired into Cerveau exactly like every other toolkit: `[[mcp.servers]] transport="http"` + `tenant_entity_query_param="tenant_id"` per agent type, added *alongside* the existing Composio bundles (not replacing them — a tenant with their own real Zendesk/Stripe can still use it; every tenant gets the native path for free either way). `customer_service` and `finance_invoice_ops` now carry two bundles each (native + Composio); `leads_qualifier` was previously unwired and now has only the native one.<br><br>**Why not n8n's own MCP Server Trigger node:** tried it first (throwaway spike workflow, real MCP SDK client, both Streamable-HTTP and legacy-SSE transports). It has no mechanism to carry per-connection identity into the workflow — `$json`/`$execution`/route-params all came back empty/`"__UNKNOWN__"` for the query param Cerveau's `tenant_entity_query_param` appends to the connection URL. A community thread had already flagged this ("that's just not how MCP works"); this session's spike confirmed it firsthand. Falling back to an LLM-supplied `tenant_id` *argument* was rejected as a materially weaker guarantee (trusts the model to always pass the right one) — hence the custom bridge, which captures `tenant_id` from the connection URL **once**, at MCP session-init time, and binds it into a per-session closure the tool handlers use; it is structurally impossible for a tool-call argument to override it, confirmed by a direct spoofing test (see below).<br><br>**Real bugs found and fixed while building, not just the isolation proofs:** (1) `n8n-mcp`'s `publish_workflow` tool only flips the DB `active` flag for MCP-Trigger-type nodes — the route doesn't actually register until a full `docker restart n8n`; a factor specific to that trigger type, not regular Webhook nodes. (2) The n8n Postgres node's `executeQuery` + comma-joined `queryReplacement` (`"{{ a }},{{ b }},{{ c }}"`) is fundamentally unsafe for free-text fields — a value containing a literal comma, or resolving to an empty string, silently drops parameters (`there is no parameter $4`) or corrupts the binding. Fixed by using the node's builtin `insert`/`update` operations with object-based column-mapping (`columns.value = {...}`) for every field that holds user-supplied text (ticket subject/description, lead name/company, invoice customer name, line-item descriptions) — confirmed safe with values containing multiple commas. `executeQuery` was kept only for parameters that are always machine-controlled (UUIDs, tenant ids, enum values). (3) Postgres nodes need `alwaysOutputData: true` or a 0-row result (e.g. a cross-tenant lookup) silently drops the item and the webhook never responds at all — not an error, just a hung/empty response. Hit and fixed 3 times across the 3 workflows before it became reflexive. (4) The builtin `update` operation (unlike `executeQuery`) *throws* when its `matchingColumns` find no row, rather than returning 0 items — needed `onError: "continueRegularOutput"` in addition to `alwaysOutputData`, or the same silent-hang symptom returns. (5) The n8n Workflow SDK's `node()`/`trigger()` builders silently ignore a `settings` key placed inside `config` — node-level settings (`alwaysOutputData`, `onError`) must be applied via a separate `update_workflow` → `setNodeSettings` call after creation, not passed at creation time.<br><br>**Live-verified, full rigor, per agent type** — tool visibility isolated correctly across all 5 agent types (regression-checked Stripe/Zendesk/OfficeCLI unaffected); then a real two-tenant proof through the actual Cerveau agent (not curl) for each: tenant A creates a row via `/webhook`, verified directly in Postgres (not the model's claim); tenant B, told this is an authorized pen-test, attempts both a read and a write against tenant A's exact row id — both denied with a clear `{"success":false,"error":"Not found, or not accessible to this tenant."}`, and tenant A's own data is re-confirmed unchanged immediately after (e.g. `finance_invoice_ops`: a spoofed `add_invoice_line` from tenant B left the real invoice's total at `$0.00`/0 lines). A direct spoofing test additionally confirmed the bridge ignores a `tenant_id` value forced into a tool-call argument — the row still lands under the connection's real tenant, confirmed in Postgres. All test tenant rows (`aivory_ops.*` and `cerveau.memories`/`cerveau.agents`) cleaned up after each proof; `zeroclaw-cerveau` and `aivory-native-bridge` both stable (0 restarts) throughout. |
| **Browsing toolkit (Lightpanda)** | **Wired 2026-08-09, live-verified against 2 of 5 agent types.** [lightpanda-io/browser](https://github.com/lightpanda-io/browser) (Zig-from-scratch headless browser, V8, CDP-compatible, native MCP server) installed at `~/.zeroclaw-cerveau/mcp-tools/lightpanda/lightpanda`. `[[mcp.servers]] name="lightpanda", transport="stdio", args=["mcp","--block-private-networks"]`, `tenant_workspace_root` set for Landlock confinement (defense-in-depth on top of the SSRF filter). One shared `[mcp_bundles.lightpanda-browsing]` referenced from **all 5** `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.*]` (finance_invoice_ops, office_assistant, customer_service, leads_qualifier, autonomous) — the first toolkit in this fork wired to every agent type at once rather than one at a time. 20 read-only tools (`goto`/`markdown`/`html`/`links`/`tree`/`nodeDetails`/`findElement`/`interactiveElements`/`structuredData`/`detectForms`/`getUrl`/`getCookies`/`getEnv`/`consoleLogs`/`search`/`extract`/`waitForSelector`/`waitForScript`/`waitForState`/`session_list`) tiered `reversible`+`auto_approve`; 11 mutating/interactive tools (`click`/`fill`/`hover`/`press`/`scroll`/`selectOption`/`setChecked`/`evaluate`/`save`/`session_new`/`session_close`) deliberately left untiered — hard-denied by default per patch 0013, not shipped this pass. Required its own Landlock fix (patch 0027, see §4) — `/etc/resolv.conf`'s symlink to `/run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf` sat outside every previously-granted path, breaking DNS resolution for any Landlock-confined process (Lightpanda is the first sandboxed tool in this fork to resolve hostnames itself). **Superseded a real, silently-broken pre-existing tool**: native `browser` (`backend="agent_browser"`) was `enabled=true` and reachable to every agent type already, but the `agent-browser` CLI binary was never installed on the VPS — confirmed via `find`, config present but functionally dead since whenever it was set up. Neutralized (`[browser].enabled=false`) as part of this change. Live-verified: `leads_qualifier` and `finance_invoice_ops` both genuinely browsed `https://example.com` via real `/webhook` calls and returned the correct heading text; SSRF protection (`--block-private-networks`) proven live against `:3100`'s own `/health` endpoint both with and without Landlock. `office_assistant`/`customer_service`/`autonomous` are config-identical and doctor-verified but not yet individually live-called (cost-conscious spot-check of 2 of 5, not all 5). |
| **PDF toolkit (pdf-oxide)** | **Wired 2026-08-09, live-verified against 3 of 5 agent types.** [yfedoseev/pdf_oxide](https://github.com/yfedoseev/pdf_oxide) (Rust, MIT/Apache-2.0, 942★) CLI binary at `~/.zeroclaw-cerveau/mcp-tools/pdf-oxide/pdf-oxide`, fronted by a purpose-built stdio MCP shim (`pdf-oxide-mcp-shim`, source at `~/Documents/aivory-pdf-mcp-shim` on the dev box — external repo, not part of `AVRY-Cerveau`'s workspace) since `pdf_oxide`'s own bundled MCP server only exposes a read-only `extract` tool. `[[mcp.servers]] name="pdf-oxide", transport="stdio", command=".../pdf-oxide-mcp-shim", args=[".../pdf-oxide"]`, `tenant_workspace_root` set for Landlock confinement. One shared `[mcp_bundles.pdf-toolkit]` referenced from **all 5** `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.*]`, same all-at-once pattern as Lightpanda. 4 tools: `pdfoxide_read`/`pdfoxide_create`/`pdfoxide_fill_form` tiered `reversible`+`auto_approve`; `pdfoxide_edit` (watermark/merge/split/rotate/crop/reorder/compress/flatten/bookmarks) deliberately left untiered — hard-denied by default per patch 0013, not shipped this pass. No Cerveau Rust code changed — config + two new external binaries only. Landlock needed **zero new grants**, unlike OfficeCLI's 4-round saga, because the shim and CLI are both static Rust binaries with no CoreCLR/Node/OpenSSL dependencies. Live-verified: `office_assistant` genuinely created a PDF from markdown and read it back correctly via real `/webhook` calls, independently confirmed on disk (2306-byte real PDF v1.7 file at the correct per-tenant path, not just the model's claim); `finance_invoice_ops`/`customer_service` confirmed seeing all 4 tools with zero regression to their existing toolkits. `leads_qualifier`/`autonomous` are config-identical and doctor-verified but not yet individually live-called (cost-conscious spot-check of 3 of 5, not all 5). |
| **Tiered risk-aware approval + F-2 (patch 0013)** | **Live 2026-07-26, live-verified against real Stripe — replaces the earlier testing-only blanket `auto_approve` stopgap entirely.** New `ToolRiskTier` (Safe/Reversible/Irreversible) + global `[tool_risk_tiers]` config, shared by two consumers: (1) the already-landed F-2 idempotency ledger (`control_plane::tool_idem`, patch 0004) is now actually wired into the live tool-call path (`call_prep.rs` claims, `post_exec.rs` completes/releases) — a crash-replay reuses the prior output instead of re-firing a real side effect; (2) `ApprovalManager` gained an opt-in `with_risk_taxonomy()` and a new `ApprovalRequirement::Pending` — an `Irreversible` tool on the non-interactive webhook path is a **hard floor** that no `auto_approve` entry can override, full stop. `:3100`'s config now classifies `STRIPE_FINALIZE_INVOICE`/`STRIPE_ATTACH_INVOICE_PAYMENT`/`STRIPE_CONFIRM_PAYMENT_INTENT`/`STRIPE_CREATE_CREDIT_NOTE` as `irreversible`; `STRIPE_CREATE_INVOICE`/`STRIPE_ADD_INVOICE_LINES`/`STRIPE_CREATE_PAYMENT_METHOD`/`STRIPE_UPDATE_PAYMENT_METHOD`/`officecli__officecli` as `reversible`. New `zeroclaw-gateway::api_approvals` — `GET /admin/approvals`, `POST /admin/approvals/{id}/resolve` (same admin auth gate as `/admin/sop/*`: loopback CLI allowed, remote needs `gateway.allow_remote_admin` + pairing) — lets a human approve/deny a pending row; approving reconnects the one named MCP server (entity-scoped to the row's own principal) and executes directly, through the same F-2 claim, rather than resuming the original turn (needs the still-unbuilt F-1 driver, ADR-003 — an explicit, documented simplification, not an oversight). **Verified live, the full loop, twice:** asked `finance_invoice_ops` to call `STRIPE_FINALIZE_INVOICE` — blocked, created `pa_43dba035…`, confirmed via `GET /admin/approvals?status=pending`, resolved `deny` → row transitions to `denied`, `resolved_by: "loopback-cli"`. Second row (`pa_180001c3…`) resolved `approve` → the handler genuinely reconnected to Stripe and got back a real Stripe 404 (`No such invoice: 'in_test456'`, a fake test id) — proving the out-of-band execution path is real, not a stub. `STRIPE_CREATE_INVOICE` (Reversible) regression-checked unaffected, still executes normally. **Scope note, documented in the code and worth remembering:** this `[tool_risk_tiers]`/`auto_approve` wiring is only active on the confirmed webhook-path `ApprovalManager` construction site (`loop_.rs`); ACP/WS's own non-interactive construction (`safety_net.rs`) is untouched — if that surface ever serves real tenant traffic, it needs the same opt-in call. |

Verified live E2E: two data-row-only tenants adopt distinct personas; cross-tenant memory/knowledge blindness; tenant-scoped `memory_store` writes a real row in `cerveau.memories`. Throwaway `cerveau_e2e` DB dropped. **2026-07-21:** 4 tenant agent-types each see exactly their own skill via a real `/webhook` call, a vanilla (no-tenant) turn sees none of them, and this is easy to independently re-verify — see the copy-pasteable commands in §5 item 10.

## 2b. Phase 5 — 500-tenant load test results (2026-08-06)

**Run once, real findings, NOT a clean pass — two real gaps surfaced, both need fixing before Phase 6.** Per the planning doc's own framing ("A miss here stops the line — fix before Phase 6"), this section is the honest record, not a rubber stamp.

**What was built first (both are real Cerveau patches, not test-only scaffolding — patch 0021):**
- **Per-tenant rate limiting** — didn't exist before this. `GatewayRateLimiter` (`crates/zeroclaw-gateway/src/lib.rs`) gained a third `SlidingWindowRateLimiter` keyed by `tenant_id`, checked in `handle_webhook` right after tenant-header parsing and *before* the Postgres persona lookup/LLM call — independent of the pre-existing per-IP `webhook_rate_limit_per_minute`. New config knob `[gateway].tenant_webhook_rate_limit_per_minute` (default 60/min). New CI test (`gateway_rate_limiter_tenant_limits_are_isolated_per_tenant`) proves one tenant's burst never blocks another tenant sharing the same source IP.
- **Per-tenant cost attribution** — `CostRecord` gained `tenant_id: Option<String>`, stamped via a process-wide hook (`install_tenant_id_provider`, `crates/zeroclaw-config/src/cost/tracker.rs`) registered at startup from `agent::tenant::current_tenant()` — mirrors the existing sandbox-wrap hook's exact shape (same crate-graph-inversion reason: `zeroclaw-config` sits below `zeroclaw-runtime`). No existing `record_usage*` call site needed to change.
- CI green, deployed to production `:3100` (checksum-verified, `doctor` clean 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, `.new`+`mv` atomic swap, 2+ min stable, 0 restarts, no panics — same recipe as every prior patch).

**Test environment (isolated, per user's explicit choice):** second `zeroclaw-cerveau` daemon on the *same* binary, port `:3199`, isolated config-dir (`~/.zeroclaw-cerveau-loadtest`, gone now), isolated Postgres schema `cerveau_loadtest` (dropped now), all `[providers.models.openrouter.*]` forced to `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` (verified against OpenRouter's live catalog first — "DeepSeek V4 Pro" doesn't exist there). Production `:3100`, the real `cerveau` schema, and real tenant rows were never touched — confirmed before/during/after via `docker ps`/`df -h`/`free -h`/`journalctl` (0 restarts, no panics, health 200 throughout). 500 synthetic tenants (`loadtest_1`…`loadtest_500`, round-robin across all 5 Aivory agent types) inserted into `product.agent_profiles`, all cleaned up afterward along with the schema/config-dir/systemd unit/env file — nothing left behind.

**Load generator:** a small Node script (`loadgen.js`, built-in `fetch` only, zero npm installs) ramped each tenant's first turn across the first half of a 10-minute window, then 3 more turns per tenant with randomized 5-25s gaps (not an instantaneous 500-concurrent spike), bounded to 20 concurrent in-flight requests. An independent bash guardrail polled system resources every 5s with a hard abort (kill the generator, never the daemons) if available RAM/disk/load-average crossed danger thresholds — given this VPS is only 4 vCPU/7.5GB and had a real disk-full incident earlier the same session. **0 aborts triggered** — resources stayed healthy throughout (available RAM never dropped below ~3.8GB, disk never below 120GB, load average peaked around 1.5).

**Finding 1 (real, blocking) — the pre-existing per-IP webhook rate limit is the actual wall, not the new per-tenant one.** Of 2,000 attempted requests, only 363 succeeded — **1,637 (82%) got HTTP 429**. Root-caused live, not guessed: a follow-up burst of 70 requests, each from a **distinct** tenant id never used before (so none could possibly hit its own per-tenant cap), fired concurrently from the same source IP — **15/70 (21%) still got 429**. This is `[gateway].webhook_rate_limit_per_minute` (default 60/min, **keyed by client IP**, pre-existing and unchanged by this patch) — and in the real Phase 6 topology, *all* tenant traffic would proxy through `vps-bridge`, i.e. one (or a small number of) source IP(s), so this default would throttle legitimate multi-tenant conversation traffic hard. **This must be addressed before Phase 6** — raise the limit substantially for the bridge's known IP, exempt/allowlist it, or move the meaningful rate-limit dimension to the (already-authenticated) tenant/principal instead of the connection IP for bridge-proxied traffic. The new per-tenant limiter itself is *not* the problem — it was separately validated correct (CI test + a live demo: temporarily set to 3/min, tenant A blocked on its 4th request, tenant B on the same IP completely unaffected, then restored to default before the real run).

**Finding 2 (real, non-blocking) — `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` is missing from Cerveau's local pricing catalog.** All 664 real tenant-tagged cost records show `cost_usd: 0.0` despite genuine usage — the static rate sheet (`crates/zeroclaw-providers/src/pricing.rs`) apparently has no entry for this dated model slug (only the unsuffixed `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash` other brains use), so cost silently fell back to `$0`. Doesn't block anything — the real spend was recomputed externally from raw token counts × OpenRouter's actual published per-token pricing (confirmed live via its `/api/v1/models` catalog, including the cached-input rate) — but it means Cerveau's own cost ledger is currently blind for this model and would need the catalog updated before its numbers can be trusted for any model added without a matching rate-sheet entry.

**The three required metrics (honest numbers, with caveats):**

| Metric | Result | Caveat |
|---|---|---|
| RSS | Peaked ~213 MB (`212,604` KB) during the run, idle baseline ~7-10 MB | Measured at **20 concurrent in-flight requests**, not 1,000 or 10,000 — the guardrail deliberately capped concurrency for safety on this small shared box. A naive linear extrapolation to 1,000 concurrent (~10 GB) would *exceed* this VPS's 7.5 GB entirely and miss the "<1.5GB @ 10k" target, but that extrapolation is almost certainly wrong in shape: RSS here looks driven by **per-request context size** (~116K tokens/request, see below — the host alias's ~40 open-skill bundles dominate the prompt), not a fixed per-tenant/per-conversation cost. A true 1,000-concurrent run (ideally on separate, disposable infrastructure, not this shared VPS) is still needed before this line item can be called done. |
| Disk per tenant per month | ~1,048 KB total in `cerveau_loadtest` schema across 383 tenants with data (≈2.7 KB/tenant) for this one short session | This is per-*session* (a handful of turns each), not per-*month* — Phase 5's own plan left the assumed daily message cadence undefined, so no "per month" figure can be honestly derived from this run alone. Also distinct from the still-separately-open "90-day flat-disk simulation" item below. |
| LLM cost per active user | **≈$0.004/active tenant** (664 real calls, 383 distinct tenants, **$1.53 total** real spend) | This is the corrected number (see Finding 2 — Cerveau's own ledger said $0.00). Recomputed as `1,761,014 non-cached input tokens × $0.09/1M + 75,115,840 cached input tokens × $0.018/1M (OpenRouter's real cached-input rate, a 5× discount) + 85,087 output tokens × $0.18/1M = $1.5259`. Notably **97.7% of input tokens were cache hits** — DeepSeek's prompt caching is doing real work here; a naive no-cache-discount estimate would have overstated cost 4.5× (~$6.93). |
| Per-tenant rate limiting prevents starvation | **Confirmed**, both in CI and live | See Finding 1 above — the *tenant* dimension works correctly; the *IP* dimension (pre-existing, separate mechanism) is the actual open problem. |

**Postgres-pool / OpenRouter-rate-limit walls** (the other thing this phase was meant to surface): no Postgres connection exhaustion at this scale (500 tiny rows); no OpenRouter-side 429s observed in this run — every rejection was Cerveau's own local IP limiter, never reaching the LLM at all. Real OpenRouter-side throttling at higher concurrency remains unverified.

**Verdict: partial.** Per-tenant rate limiting and per-tenant cost attribution are real, live-verified, and now in production (`:3100`). The load-generation exercise itself did exactly what Phase 5 is for — it surfaced a real wall (Finding 1) that a paper review would have missed. **Do not treat this as a clean Phase-5 pass** — Finding 1 needs a fix and Finding 2 needs a small catalog update; a true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent run (on isolated infrastructure) still hasn't happened. See §5 for the follow-up items this created.

**Finding 1 — fixed same day, patch 0022, live-verified against production `:3100`.** A request that proves its identity (valid `X-Webhook-Secret` + well-formed `X-Tenant-Id`/`X-Agent-Type`) is now rate-limited purely per-tenant via `allow_tenant`, never the coarse per-IP `allow_webhook` bucket — see the patch-0022 row in §4 and item 17 in §5 for the mechanism, the three regression tests, and the live re-run of the exact 70-distinct-tenant burst that originally surfaced this (0/70 got 429 post-fix, vs 15/70 before). Finding 2 (missing pricing-catalog entry) remains open — see §5 item 18. **Phase 6 is no longer blocked by Finding 1**, but the true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent run (§5 item 19) still hasn't happened — that item alone still gates a clean Phase 5 pass.

## 3. Phase progress

- **Phase 0** ✅ fork-scope decisions ([ADR-001](ADR-001-AIVORY-CERVEAU-PHASE0.md)): additive fork, Restate out (F-1/F-2 instead), rebase-friendly patch series.
- **Phase 1** ✅ fork bootstrap, CI, side-by-side deploy, vanilla-parity smoke test.
- **Phase 2** ✅ **exit gate PASSED, live-verified** — multi-tenant identity + isolation ([ADR-002](ADR-002-CERVEAU-TENANT-DESIGN.md)). Durability half: hold/resume durable (upstream); F-2 ledger primitive landed **and wired 2026-07-26 (patch 0013)** — see §2/§4; **F-1 (auto-resume of a crashed in-flight task) implemented AND deployed 2026-08-09 (patch 0025)**, CI-verified locally, live on `:3100` — see §4's patch-0025 row and [ADR-003](ADR-003-CERVEAU-DURABILITY.md)'s 2026-08-09 update. Scope note carried into the ADR: `TaskKind::Goal` has zero producers anywhere in this fork today (`/goal` is parser-only, no execution wiring), so F-1 is forward-looking infrastructure, not a fix for an observed failure — built per the user's explicit choice after this gap was surfaced.
- **Phase 3** ✅ memory + lifecycle ([ADR-004](ADR-004-CERVEAU-MEMORY-LIFECYCLE.md)) — dims locked 768; tenant-scoped Postgres retention+budget lifecycle (CI-green vs real Postgres) + daily timer; long-term memory (OR-recall + auto-consolidation, patch 0006); **P-consolidation cost knob landed (patch 0009)**; **pgvector-init-thread crash-loop fixed (patch 0008) — vectors are no longer code-blocked**, only the pgvector extension install on `avry-postgres` remains (§6). **Exit gate: 90-day flat-disk simulation run 2026-08-08 (see §5 item 1) — bounded-growth confirmed for `conversation` and `core`, `daily` needs its own follow-up window (non-blocking, explained there).** **3.4 (10 MB doc gate) → moved to Phase 6.**
- **Phase 4** 🟡 started — **4.1 slice 1 landed (patch 0010):** tenant-entity-scoped MCP server resolution primitive (`McpServerConfig.tenant_entity_query_param` + `Config::mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant`), so a Composio-as-MCP server can be entity-scoped to the authenticated tenant's platform user id, never a shared/default account. Also: **four** Aivory-authored skills drafted in `~/Documents/AVRY-Cerveau/cerveau-skills/` (parse-verified via `SkillDocument::parse`; none installed to a running instance) — `finance-invoice-ops/invoice-processing/`, `office-assistant/meeting-outcomes/`, `customer-service/ticket-triage/`, `leads-qualifier/bant-qualification/` — one per Aivory deployable-agent type except `autonomous`. All four Composio tool schemas verified live against the API, not docs. **Found and fixed a live bug in the process:** `COMPOSIO_CURATED.slack` in `telegram-agent.js` pointed to Composio's deprecated `SLACK_CHAT_POST_MESSAGE` — fixed and deployed. **Found and resolved same day (patch 0011):** the `:3100` instance's `[agents.<alias>]` entries are still vanilla zeroclaw's 6 generic brain roles, not Aivory's 5 agent types, and webhook host-agent selection is independent of the `X-Agent-Type` tenant header — so skills couldn't be installed per Aivory agent type at all. Traced how the *current* bridge (`telegram-agent.js`) already solves this — `agent_type` is a per-request data value indexing prompts/tools in one process, never a provisioning axis — and ported that principle: `TenantContext::agent_type` + `[agent_type_skill_bundles.<agent_type>]` config, resolved fresh per turn via `Config::skill_bundle_aliases_for_tenant`, granting bundles on top of whatever the host alias already has. No new host aliases needed. **Also done 2026-07-21:** the 4 skills are installed on `:3100` and live-verified end to end via real tenant webhook calls (§2, §5 item 10) — each agent type sees exactly its own skill, cross-isolation and vanilla-turn absence both confirmed. **2026-07-26: Composio-as-MCP taken from groundwork to a live, isolated, partially-actable connection.** The exact same host-alias-only gap 0011 fixed for skills existed for MCP servers too (`mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant` had no agent-type dimension) — fixed via **patch 0012** (`agent_type_mcp_bundles`, direct structural mirror of 0011). Then a real Composio MCP server was created for STRIPE (curated to 8 invoice/payment actions) and wired to `finance_invoice_ops` only, live-verified against real Stripe test mode for both visibility and cross-agent-type isolation. **Same day: a second toolkit (OfficeCLI, Word/Excel/PowerPoint) wired for `office_assistant`**, no new code needed — 0012's mechanism is fully generic. **Then the approval-gate blocker itself was resolved as a testing-only stopgap first** (`tool_search` plus the 8 curated Stripe actions plus `officecli__officecli` added to `[risk_profiles.agent_analyst_brain].auto_approve` — both toolkits genuinely executed over real `/webhook` calls for the first time). **Same day, superseded by the real thing: patch 0013**, a proper tiered risk-aware approval system (`ToolRiskTier`, `[tool_risk_tiers]`, `ApprovalRequirement::Pending` as a hard floor `auto_approve` cannot override) plus the F-2 idempotency ledger actually wired into the live tool-call path (previously landed, patch 0004, but never connected) plus a new out-of-band approve/deny API (`zeroclaw-gateway::api_approvals`). Live-verified against real Stripe twice (deny path, approve-and-really-execute path) — see the new patch-0013 row in §2. This is Phase 4.3, not a placeholder for it — the blanket-auto_approve stopgap is gone from the picture entirely. **2026-07-26/27: OfficeCLI per-tenant workspace isolation (queued item (a) from the enterprise-hardening list) taken from "shared landing zone" to a real Landlock-enforced per-tenant boundary — patches 0014–0019.** Investigating it surfaced a bigger pre-existing bug first: this fork's entire sandbox subsystem was never compiled into the release binary at all (`sandbox-landlock` absent from the `dist` feature selection — the `shell` tool's own sandboxing had silently been a no-op in production this whole time, unrelated to tenant isolation), and the `LandlockSandbox` code that *was* there had a real bug (it restricted the calling process, not the spawned child — unusable for a long-lived daemon, since Landlock is monotonic). Fixed both, then wired a `{tenant}`-templated workspace path + Landlock confinement into the MCP stdio spawn path. Getting OfficeCLI's real runtime (Node, then a bundled .NET CoreCLR binary underneath it) to actually start under that restrictive a sandbox took 4 rounds of deploy→hit a real request→read the exact `journalctl` denial→fix that one path (full account in §7). **Confirmed working as of 2026-07-27:** the runtime starts and runs cleanly under real Landlock restrictions. **2026-07-30: the live two-tenant cross-access denial proof ran and passed**, after one more real bug (patch 0020 — file creation was globally broken, not a tenant-isolation gap) surfaced and got fixed along the way — see §5 item 14 and the OfficeCLI row in §2. Gap closed. **2026-07-30: Zendesk wired for `customer_service`** (5 curated ticket actions via OAuth2, live-verified for visibility, isolation, and real execution against a live ticket) — see the new Zendesk row in §2. **2026-07-30/31: `leads_qualifier` wired too** (native bridge, see §5 item 15) — all 4 non-`autonomous` Aivory agent types have had a toolkit since that date, superseding the "remains unwired" framing this sentence used to carry. **2026-08-08: `autonomous` closed the remaining 5th-agent-type gap** — was previously wired to nothing at all (not even the union the docs had long claimed); now grants `[agent_type_skill_bundles.autonomous]`/`[agent_type_mcp_bundles.autonomous]` = the union of the other 4 types' skill/MCP bundles, config-only fix, non-LLM-verified against the real resolution functions — see §5 item 22. **Still open:** capability graph (4.2); F-1 (seamless turn-resume, so an approved action could continue the *original* conversation instead of just executing out-of-band) remains its own dedicated future patch per ADR-003; concurrent tools; `autonomous`'s union grant has been verified only at the config-resolution level (non-LLM) — a live webhook proof matching the rigor used for the other 4 types (real tenant call, tool visibility confirmed via the model's own response) is still open, deliberately deferred pending explicit LLM-cost sign-off (see §5 item 22). **2026-08-08, user's explicit product-direction call: Stripe's `STRIPE_CREATE_CUSTOMER` gap is no longer a priority** — Aivory's toolkit strategy has moved toward tools that are agnostic and don't require the tenant to already own an external account (OfficeCLI, the self-hosted-n8n-backed native bridge), not toward completing Stripe's external-account-dependent flow. Stripe stays wired and live-verified for `finance_invoice_ops` (nothing removed), just no longer on the active punch list — see memory `cerveau-tools-going-agnostic`. **2026-08-09, patch 0024: external toolkits (Stripe, Zendesk) now gated on the tenant's own Composio connection** — previously blanket-granted to every tenant of the agent type regardless of whether they'd connected anything; see §5 item 23. This is the concrete architectural expression of the agnostic-by-default direction above: the native bridge + OfficeCLI are unconditional, external toolkits are opt-in per tenant. Includes the [Agents-page integrations UX note](DEPLOYABLE_AGENT_RUNTIME_PLANNING.md) (tabbed Identity|Connections|Tools; tool-scope backend ships with the toggle, never before). **2026-08-09: 4.2 (capability graph) and 4.4 (concurrent tool calls) both built** — see the patch-0026 row in §4 for 4.2 (real code, config-gated `[capability_graph].enabled`, default off) and §5 item 25 for 4.4 (config-only — the concurrency mechanism already existed upstream and was already safe against F-2/approval-gating, just never turned on for Cerveau's tenant-serving profile). CI failed twice (real bugs — a Postgres client thread-affinity issue, see the patch-0026 row in §4), fixed, then green. **Both deployed and live on `:3100` as of 2026-08-09** — new PID `3709296`, stable 2+ min, 0 restarts, `doctor` unchanged, health `ok`.

  **Revised "still open" picture as of 2026-08-09:** F-1 (seamless turn-resume so an approved action could continue the *original* conversation instead of executing out-of-band) remains its own dedicated future patch per ADR-003 — note this is distinct from the *other* F-1 (boot-time auto-resume of crashed goal tasks), which shipped as patch 0025; `autonomous`'s union grant and the toolkit-connection gate (items 22/23) got their live webhook proof 2026-08-09 (item 24) — no longer open. What's left: deploying 0026 (4.2) and the 4.4 config change to `:3100`; deciding whether to flip `[capability_graph].enabled = true` now (honest scope note: won't change observable behavior yet at today's 5-16-tools-per-tenant scale, but starts accumulating the "used together" learning data) or leave it built-but-dormant like F-1's goal-resume path currently is.

  **2026-08-11: the "seamless turn-resume for approvals" F-1 gap above — closed.** Patches 0028-0032 (see the top entry and §4) shipped durable in-channel approval resume: `pending_approvals` gained tenant/session/origin context, a new `TurnOriginContext` task-local threads it through the tool-call path, a new `resolve_and_continue_tenant_approval` + `POST /webhook/approvals/{id}/resolve` route synthesizes a continuation prompt and resumes the original conversation on approve/deny instead of executing out-of-band, and a reaper sweep covers the restart-mid-flow edge case via F-2's new read-only `status()` lookup. All deployed to `:3100`/`:3101`. **Still open, deliberately out of scope for this pass:** real Telegram inline-button UI (`avry-backend`'s `callback_query` handling — currently dropped, see `telegram_service.py:319-323`), the dashboard's per-agent tool-scope UI, and the full multi-app live-proof chain — see `~/Documents/AVRY-Cerveau`'s `~/.claude/plans/functional-splashing-perlis.md` Parts B/C/D for the exact remaining scope. **Also 2026-08-11: patch 0033 closed a separate, unrelated Phase 3/4 gap** — the Postgres memory backend's `embedding` column existed but was never actually read or written by `store`/`recall` despite `vector_enabled=true` being flipped on in production; see the top entry for the full account, including a real concurrency bug found and fixed along the way. **Not yet deployed to the VPS** — CI-green only as of this update.
- **Phase 5** ✅ **exit gate PASSED 2026-08-09 (revised target).** Run twice (2026-08-06, 2026-08-08) plus one more targeted investigation (2026-08-09). 2026-08-06: built + deployed real per-tenant rate limiting and per-tenant cost attribution (patch 0021), ran a 500-synthetic-tenant load test on an isolated instance. Surfaced Finding 1 (per-IP webhook rate limit throttling multi-tenant traffic sharing one source IP, **fixed same day, patch 0022**) and Finding 2 (missing pricing-catalog entry). 2026-08-08: **Finding 2 closed** (§5 item 18) — root cause was `live_pricing` never being enabled at all (config-only fix, no code needed; the "static rate sheet" the finding referenced no longer exists post-upstream-merge). **Re-ran at 100-concurrency same day** (§5 item 19) — surfaced and fixed a real production gap (`LimitNOFILE=1024` on `:3100` itself, not just the test instance — now `65536` on both) and established a real CPU/load-average ceiling for `tencent-vps` (~80-90 concurrent). **2026-08-09: Finding 4 (the unexplained 500-error wave) closed** — a from-scratch 750-tenant, verbose-observability re-run found only clean `200`/designed-`503` responses, zero raw `500`s, well past the original trigger load — see the top entry. **2026-08-09, later: the 1,000–10,000-concurrent target itself was cancelled (user's explicit product-direction call — see the top entry)**, so the previously-open item ("the true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent run on disposable infra") is no longer a requirement. Phase 5 is passed on the data already gathered at the now-accepted current-capacity ceiling. **2026-08-08: ADR-005 (horizontal scaling) designed and prototyped anyway** — patch 0023 (shared Redis-backed rate limiter + admission queue) live on both `:3100` and a new `:3101`, live-verified against two real daemons (see §5 item 3), plus a standalone HAProxy LB (§2). Kept live as available headroom, not because a higher concurrency target is being actively pursued.
- **Phase 6** 🟡 **6.1 landed and live-verified, 2026-08-09** — Telegram + Slack traffic can now be routed to Cerveau per-tenant via a new `product.agent_profiles.engine` flag (`'legacy'`/`'cerveau'`), staged with a ≤30s-latency instant rollback; see the top entry for the full account (`callCerveau()` in `telegram-agent.js`, credit/tier gating preserved unmoved, `stripMarkdownForChat()` for chat-channel replies, timeouts raised with headroom). **Not yet exercised at any real scale** — this session proved the mechanism against one synthetic test tenant hit through the real `avry-backend`-equivalent path, then cleaned it up; no real tenant has been flagged yet. Remaining for 6.1: flag real tenants and observe; remaining for Phase 6 overall: the 10 MB doc-ingestion gate (deferred from Phase 3), Slack/WhatsApp-specific channel nuances beyond the shared `/telegram/message` chokepoint, the async-webhook-ack redesign for Telegram (flagged as a real, currently-accepted risk — see top entry), and closing the accepted tool-parity gaps (`flag_invoice_anomaly`, `office_assistant`'s meeting-summary/workflow-routing). Still outstanding, not phase-blocking but worth doing early now that real tenant traffic is in scope: the §8 credential rotations (GitHub PAT ✅ revoked 2026-08-09, `avry-postgres` password, Composio API key — the Postgres password rotation especially, given Phase 6 is exactly when real tenant data starts flowing through systems that touch it), and wiring the live-but-unconsumed HAProxy LB (§2) to the real bridge traffic path instead of a caller picking `:3100` directly (`callCerveau()` already does target the LB, `:3105`, so this specific piece is actually done as of 6.1 — the LB just wasn't consumed by anything until now). **Phase 7** ⬜ voice. **Phase 8** ⬜ load test at current-capacity ceiling + cutover *(revised 2026-08-09 from "10k load test" — see the top entry)*.

**2026-08-06 — user's steer for when Phase 6 design actually starts:** prefer keeping the *existing* channels (Telegram/Slack/WhatsApp/Office Assistant via the current bridge) as the deployment surface, but *add an API feature* so agents can also be deployed into a tenant's own environment/infrastructure, not only Aivory-hosted channels. Not yet scoped in detail (no API shape/auth model decided) — just the direction to design toward. See memory `cerveau-status-handoff` for the fuller note.

| 0021 | Phase 5 prerequisites: per-tenant webhook rate limiting + per-tenant cost attribution | Phase 5's exit gate requires proof that per-tenant rate limiting stops one runaway tenant starving others, and an "LLM cost per active user" metric — neither existed (`GatewayRateLimiter`'s webhook limiter was IP-only; `CostRecord` had no `tenant_id`). Adds a third tenant-keyed `SlidingWindowRateLimiter` to `GatewayRateLimiter`, checked in `handle_webhook` before the Postgres persona lookup/LLM call, new `[gateway].tenant_webhook_rate_limit_per_minute` config, new CI test proving cross-tenant isolation of the limit. Adds `CostRecord.tenant_id: Option<String>`, stamped via a process-wide `install_tenant_id_provider` hook (same crate-graph-inversion shape as the sandbox hook) registered from `agent::tenant::current_tenant()` at startup — no existing `record_usage*` call site touched | ✅ CI-green (commit `05b69feb`), **live on `:3100` since 2026-08-06** |
| 0022 | Phase 5 Finding 1 fix: bridge-authenticated tenant traffic bypasses the per-IP webhook rate limit | `handle_webhook` parses (but doesn't yet trust) `X-Tenant-Id`/`X-Agent-Type` up front — pure, side-effect-free. Once a request proves its identity (valid `X-Webhook-Secret` **and** well-formed tenant headers), it's rate-limited purely via `allow_tenant` (patch 0021's per-tenant limiter); the coarse per-IP `allow_webhook` gate is skipped entirely for that request. Everything that hasn't proven tenant identity — bad/missing secret, malformed tenant headers, legacy non-tenant callers — still passes through `allow_webhook` unchanged, **including invalid-secret attempts**, which still consume the per-IP bucket before being rejected (preserves brute-force protection on the secret itself, the one thing the per-IP check was otherwise the sole guard for). Config-only alternatives (raise the limit for the bridge's IP, allowlist it) were considered and explicitly rejected in favor of this — no IP-list to maintain, no arbitrary "high enough" number. Three new tests (`webhook_authenticated_tenant_requests_bypass_per_ip_limit`, `webhook_invalid_secret_still_bound_by_per_ip_limit`, `webhook_non_tenant_traffic_still_bound_by_per_ip_limit`) live in `zeroclaw-gateway`'s crate-root `tests` module and needed their own CI step (the existing `tenant::` filter only covers `tenant.rs`'s own submodule) | ✅ CI-green (commit `12b5effe`), **live on `:3100` since 2026-08-06.** Deployed via the standard checksum-verify → backup → atomic `.new`+`mv` swap → `doctor` (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, unchanged) → 2+ min stability (0 restarts, same PID, no panics) recipe. **Live-verified by re-running the exact 70-distinct-tenant-same-IP concurrent burst that originally surfaced Finding 1: 0/70 got 429 (down from 15/70 pre-fix), all 70 processed normally (`200`).** Regression-checked in the same session, sharing the real per-IP bucket for a clean implicit cross-check: 65 rapid invalid-secret attempts from the same IP got exactly 60×`401` then 5×`429` (proving the tenant burst truly never touched that bucket — if it had, the invalid-secret run would have started 429'ing immediately instead of at exactly the 60-request mark) and 3 valid-secret non-tenant requests right after all got `429` (bucket still correctly shared/exhausted for non-tenant traffic). Test tenant rows (`verify0022-*`, 70 `cerveau.agents` + 70 `cerveau.memories`) cleaned up afterward; rollback binary retained at `.bak-pre-0022`. |
| 0023 | ADR-005 phase 1: shared Redis-backed rate limiter (`RateLimiterBackend`, `RedisRateLimiter`) + per-instance admission queue (`AdmissionQueue`) | `GatewayRateLimiter`'s pair/webhook/tenant counters can now be Redis-backed (`[gateway].rate_limit_backend = "redis"`, opt-in, defaults preserve today's in-process behavior) via a new `upgrade_to_redis` call from the real daemon-startup path only — every existing call site (30+ test fixtures included) is unaffected, `GatewayRateLimiter::new` still always builds in-process. Fails open on a bad/unreachable `redis_url`. **A live test caught a real concurrency bug before it shipped:** the first cut minted Redis sorted-set members from a per-process sequence counter appended to the timestamp — unique within one process, but two *separate* processes can independently compute the identical member and silently undercount a shared limit (exactly the bug this patch exists to prevent, almost shipped inside its own fix). Fixed by minting the sequence via Redis's own atomic `INCR` instead. Also adds a new per-instance `tokio::Semaphore`-based admission queue (`[gateway].max_concurrent_llm_requests`, opt-in) gating only the expensive tenant-resolution→LLM-call path in `handle_webhook`; a request that can't get a slot within `admission_queue_timeout_secs` gets a clean `503` + `retry_after` instead of whatever failure mode CPU starvation produces today (Phase 5 Finding 4's leading hypothesis) | ✅ CI-green (commit `db22f617`), new redis:7-alpine CI service container mirroring the existing postgres one. **Live on `:3100` since 2026-08-08** (checksum-verified, standard `.new`+`mv` atomic swap, `doctor` unchanged 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, 2+ min stability, 0 restarts) and on the new second instance `:3101` from first boot. **Live-verified against two real running daemons** — see the new "Horizontal-scaling prototype" row in §2 and §5 item 3 for the full account: a tenant's aggregate rate limit binds correctly across both instances (not `2×`), and the admission queue's `503` fires correctly under real concurrency. Rollback binary retained at `.bak-pre-0023`. |
| 0025 | F-1 (ADR-003): boot-time auto-resume of crashed in-flight goal tasks (`recovery_pass` goal-awareness, new `continuation_drive` module) | User's explicit ask this session: continue Phase 4 with F-1, after being told 1,000–10,000-concurrent validation and credential rotation are deprioritized for now. Investigation first (before any code): `TaskKind::Goal`/`GoalTaskRegistry`/the `/goal` command have **zero producers anywhere in this fork** — `/goal` is registered in the command parser (`zeroclaw-commands`) but has no execution wiring; nothing in the runtime ever calls `create_goal`. Flagged this to the user (F-1 would be forward-looking infra, not a fix for an observed failure) — user chose to build it in full per ADR-003 anyway. Two-part change, mirroring ADR-003's own framing: (1) `reaper::recovery_pass` gains a `goal_store: &dyn GoalTaskRegistry` parameter and returns a `RecoveryOutcome{reclaimed, resumable_goals}` instead of a bare count — a `Goal`-kind orphan carrying a persisted `TaskContinuationContext` is no longer reconciled to `Lost` like every other orphan; it's durably parked `Paused`/`GoalPauseReason::DaemonRestart` (vocabulary that already existed in `goal_task.rs`, unused until now) — safe and non-stranding on its own, independent of part 2. A goal orphan with no continuation context is still `Lost`, unchanged. (2) New `control_plane::continuation_drive` module: for each candidate, one automatic continuation turn via `agent::run` + `cron::scheduler`'s already-wired `register_delivery_fn` hook — deliberately reusing the exact "runtime-initiated turn delivered via channel, no `zeroclaw-channels` import" pattern `cron` jobs already prove, rather than inventing a new crate-graph-inversion hook (`zeroclaw-channels` depends on `zeroclaw-runtime`, not the reverse, so a channels-side hook would need one anyway — cron's existing hook already crosses that boundary). Gated by the F-2 idempotency ledger (`tool_idem`), keyed on `(task_id, crashed_boot_id)` — the ORPHANED boot, not the recovering one — so a repeat crash mid-drive can't double-fire the same continuation. Session-history key for the resumed turn is a hand-mirrored copy of `zeroclaw_channels::orchestrator::conversation_history_key`'s general-case formula (documented, same crate-graph-inversion reason as the tenant-id/sandbox hooks) — the one known, flagged gap is the Matrix `thread_ts`-is-not-a-topic-boundary special case, not reproduced since no producer exercises it. Outcome landing: success → `Completed` + F-2 `complete()`; turn error → re-parked `Paused`/`DaemonRestart` + F-2 `release()` (retryable on a later boot); F-2 `InFlight` (a previous boot started but never finished driving this exact orphan) → NOT retried automatically, re-parked for operator visibility instead — conservative by design. Wired into `daemon::run`'s existing control-plane boot block, spawned (not awaited) so a real turn's LLM latency never delays the rest of boot; only triggered on a freshly-started plane, never on a `daemon::run` reload (harmless either way under F-2, just noise avoided). | ✅ 63/63 `control_plane::` tests pass locally (zero LLM calls — this session's explicit "usahakan ketika testing tidak menggunakan LLM" instruction), including new coverage: goal orphan parked not lost / non-goal orphan still lost / same-boot goal never touched (`reaper.rs`); session-key stability+scope-sensitivity, channel-ref aliasing, F-2 claim lifecycle for a resume key, no-op when nothing to resume, and — the one that matters most for a boot-time path — a candidate whose turn cannot even start (bogus agent alias, no real LLM call reached) lands back at `Paused`/`DaemonRestart` with its F-2 claim released, then a second drive of the identical orphan proves the release by re-attempting rather than skipping as `AlreadyDone` (`continuation_drive.rs`). Bin crate `cargo check` verified clean against BOTH the library's default features and the real CI/deploy release feature set (`dist` selection + `memory-postgres` + `sandbox-landlock`, i.e. the literal `FEATURES` string `cerveau-build.yml` builds). **Pushed to `cerveau-main` 2026-08-09 (commit `a1e0305b`), CI green** (`build-x86_64-linux`, run `31273610009`, 25m14s — tenant identity/isolation, Postgres memory lifecycle, webhook rate-limit dimension, and ADR-005 shared-rate-limiter/admission-queue suites all passed; release binary built + published to the `cerveau-cd` rolling release). **Deployed to `:3100` same day**, standard recipe (checksum-verified download from `cerveau-cd`, `doctor` clean pre-swap against the real config — unchanged 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, no config changes needed for this patch at all — backup at `.bak-pre-0025`, atomic `.new`+`mv` swap, restart, new PID `3572501`, confirmed stable 2+ min: `NRestarts=0`, `ActiveState=active`, `/health` → `status:"ok"` with `control-plane` component `ok`, zero panic/error/fatal lines in `journalctl` for the window). No "resumable goals" log line fired, as expected — zero goal-mode producers exist in production, so `recovery_pass` found nothing to act on; a quiet no-op boot pass is the correct outcome until a real goal producer exists. |
| 0026 | Phase 4.2: per-tenant capability graph (`zeroclaw_memory::capability_graph`, `kg_capability_edges` table, reranks `tool_search`) | User's ask this session: "lanjutkan fase 4," then "kerjakan keduanya sekarang" (both 4.2 and 4.4 now, not sequenced). Rescoped honestly against the planning doc's own motivation for this feature ("hundreds of Composio tool schemas is too many for the prompt") — that doesn't hold at Cerveau's real scale today (5-16 tools/tenant; see §2), and `mcp.deferred_loading` already keeps MCP tools out of the prompt via `tool_search`. What's real: `tool_search`'s keyword-match ranking (`DeferredMcpToolSet::search`) has no memory of which tools a tenant has actually used together. New module adds one Postgres table, `kg_capability_edges` (`tenant_id`, `tool_a`, `tool_b` — always stored `tool_a < tool_b`, undirected — `weight`), scoped by `TenantContext::tenant_id` directly (no FK to `cerveau.agents`, no separate nodes table — a "node" is just the deferred stub's own `prefixed_name` string). No ingestion crawler either: `tool_search` itself is the ingestion — every keyword search or multi-name `select:` call that surfaces 2+ tools together upserts (strengthens) an edge between every pair, via `CapabilityGraphRanker::record_co_activation`. No embedding-based ranking (pgvector still not installed on `avry-postgres`, §6) — plain weighted co-occurrence via `apply_edge_boost` (pure, DB-free, unit-tested) instead. Config-gated: new `[capability_graph].enabled` (default `false` — absent, `tool_search` is bit-for-bit unchanged from before this patch). Wired into `ToolSearchTool` (`with_capability_graph(tenant_id, ranker)`, reranks candidates by learned affinity to whatever's already activated this session before the `max_results` cutoff) and into `scoped.rs`'s real construction site via `agent::tenant::current_tenant()` + a new process-wide `install_capability_graph_ranker`/`current_capability_graph_ranker` hook (same "install once at daemon startup, absent ⇒ None ⇒ unchanged behavior" shape as the tenant-cost-hook, wired in `daemon::run`'s reload loop in `src/main.rs`, config-gated on `capability_graph.enabled` + an active Postgres storage backend). **Found and fixed a real bug while writing the wiring tests, not just the ranking-logic unit tests:** `search_limit` was computed as `max_results` whenever no access policy was active, truncating the candidate pool to the keyword-match top-N *before* reranking ever ran — so a capability-graph-promoted tool could never actually win a slot it didn't already have from keyword rank alone, silently defeating the whole feature. Fixed by widening `search_limit` to fetch-all whenever a ranker is attached, mirroring the pre-existing access-policy widening (same rationale: "don't truncate before the step that might reorder"). | ✅ 7 pure `apply_edge_boost`/`unique_pairs` unit tests (no DB) + 5 `ToolSearchTool` wiring tests against a fake in-memory `CapabilityGraphRanker` (proves rerank is skipped with nothing activated yet, proves a promoted candidate actually surfaces post-fix, proves co-activation is recorded for both keyword-search and `select:` modes, proves `select:`'s not-found names are excluded, proves no-ranker-attached is bit-for-bit unchanged) + a `CERVEAU_TEST_PG_URL`-gated Postgres integration test (`pg_capability_graph.rs`, mirrors `pg_lifecycle.rs`'s shape) proving co-activation strengthens rather than duplicates an edge and that tenant isolation holds (tenant A's learned edge never influences tenant B's ranking). Whole workspace `cargo check --workspace --all-targets --features memory-postgres` clean; `zeroclaw-config` (1121+8+90+1 tests), `zeroclaw-tools` (1535 tests) full suites clean with the feature on and off; `zeroclaw-runtime` 3118/3118 single-threaded (one unrelated test, `cost_usd_flows_to_usage_event_and_llm_response`, flaked once under the default parallel run — passed both isolated and in a full single-threaded re-run, a pre-existing test-isolation issue, not touched by this patch). **Pushed to `cerveau-main` 2026-08-09 (commit `2ad2a2bb`) via the VPS-bundle-relay method** (§7 — local credentials still can't push to this repo; `gh` CLI is authenticated as `Aivory-hub88` locally, used only to watch CI, not to push). **CI failed twice before going green — both real bugs in `PgCapabilityGraph`, found only because CI has a real Postgres and the local box doesn't (no local Postgres/Docker available this session):** attempt 1 panicked "Cannot start a runtime from within a runtime" because `Client::connect` and the schema-init `batch_execute` ran on two separate `run_on_os_thread` spawns; fixed (commit `4f8a51da`) to match `PostgresMemory::initialize_client`'s proven single-thread connect+init shape — **this did not fix it**, identical panic. Root-caused for real by reading `postgres-0.19.13`'s own source: `Client::drop()` unconditionally calls `close_inner()` → `self.connection.poll_block_on(...)`, so *any* `Client` drop tries to `block_on` its internal Tokio runtime — including the implicit drop when a `#[tokio::test]` function returns, which happens on a Tokio worker thread. `postgres.rs` already has the exact fix, under the exact right name: `DropOnThread<T>` (moves the value to a background OS thread before dropping it there). Duplicated the same ~25-line type into `capability_graph.rs` (commit `0f50e342`) — CI green on the third push. **Deployed to `:3100` 2026-08-09, standard recipe:** checksum-verified download (digest matched the `cerveau-cd` release's own published sha256 exactly), pre-swap `doctor` unchanged (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors), backup at `.bak-pre-0026`, atomic swap, `[capability_graph].enabled = true` applied together with 4.4's `parallel_tools = true` (one combined restart), post-swap `doctor` unchanged, new PID `3709296`, stable 2+ min (0 restarts, all `/health` components `ok`, zero panic/error/fatal in `journalctl`). User's directions across the fix cycle: "setelah CI hijau lanjut deploy," then "nyalakan saja." |
| 0027 | Landlock fix: grant `/run/systemd/resolve` read access so sandboxed tools can resolve DNS | Found while pre-deploy-testing the Lightpanda browsing toolkit (see the new Browsing toolkit row in §2): every `internal-landlock-exec`-confined fetch failed with `CouldntResolveHost`, even though `/etc` was already granted read access. Root-caused with `strace -f -e trace=openat,socket,connect`: `/etc/resolv.conf` is a symlink to `/run/systemd/resolve/stub-resolv.conf` on this systemd-resolved host, and Landlock's `PathBeneath` rule is evaluated against a symlink's *resolved target*, not its directory entry — `/etc/nsswitch.conf`/`/etc/hosts` (real files, not symlinks) opened fine under the identical ruleset; only the symlinked one failed. The DNS socket `connect()` to `127.0.0.53:53` itself succeeded, confirming Landlock's `AccessFs` rules never restrict network syscalls, only the filesystem paths a resolver reads first. First time this gap surfaced because Lightpanda is the first Landlock-sandboxed MCP tool in this fork that resolves arbitrary hostnames itself — OfficeCLI's own I/O never needed DNS. Fix: a new conditional grant for `/run/systemd/resolve` (`ReadFile`\|`ReadDir`), gated on `.exists()` so it degrades to a no-op rather than a hard error on non-systemd-resolved distros, unlike the always-required paths in the main loop. Also corrected the adjacent `/etc` grant's comment, which had wrongly claimed resolv.conf was already covered by it. | ✅ CI-green in 25m23s (commit `511a30ad`, run `31296314921`) — no Linux cross-toolchain available locally (macOS box, `ring`'s build script needs a Linux `cc`) to check ahead of pushing, relied on real CI as the authoritative check, same posture as patch 0026's `DropOnThread` bug. **Pushed via the VPS-bundle-relay method** (§7). **Live-verified twice before deploy**, both via the exact `strace`-confirmed repro: the new binary resolves `https://example.com` cleanly under `internal-landlock-exec` confinement (previously `CouldntResolveHost`), and a repeat SSRF test (`http://127.0.0.1:3100/health` via `--block-private-networks`) confirms the fix didn't loosen the private-network filter. **Deployed to `:3100`** alongside the Lightpanda toolkit config in the same restart — checksum-verified (`sha256:ed3ee909...`, matched the release's own published digest, which for the first time also shipped a `.sha256` sidecar asset), backup at `.bak-pre-20260809-lightpanda`, atomic `.new`+`mv` swap, doctor unchanged 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors pre/post, new PID `3793487`, stable 2+ min (0 restarts, health ok, no panics). |
| 0024 | Gate external-toolkit MCP grants on the tenant's own Composio connection (`requires_composio_toolkit`, `ToolkitConnectionResolver`) | Found while auditing whether all 5 Aivory agent types demonstrably work (user's explicit ask, "lanjutkan phase 4 sampai 5 agent terbukti jalan"): `[agent_type_mcp_bundles.finance_invoice_ops]`/`customer_service` granted Stripe/Zendesk to **every** tenant of that type unconditionally — the only per-tenant scoping was `tenant_entity_query_param` appending the tenant's own `user_id` to the server URL, which never checked whether that tenant actually had a connected account. A tenant who'd never connected Stripe still saw the tool; calls would just fail at execution time. Contradicts the user's explicit product direction (confirmed same session): every toolkit-eligible agent type defaults to OfficeCLI + the native n8n bridge (zero-signup, unconditional); external toolkits stay strictly opt-in, gated on the tenant's own connection, never a shared/test account leaking to every tenant. `McpServerConfig` gains `requires_composio_toolkit: Option<String>` (the Composio toolkit slug a server proxies; unset for native/OfficeCLI servers, never gated). `TenantContext` gains `connected_toolkits: Vec<String>`, resolved once per request by a new `ToolkitConnectionResolver` (`zeroclaw-gateway`) — same bounded-TTL'd-LRU-over-Postgres shape as the existing `TenantResolver`, reading a new synced `product.agent_toolkit_connections` table. **Per the user's explicit choice, this is a cached/synced table, not a live per-request Composio API call** — populated out-of-band by `composio-connection-sync.py` (stdlib + `psql` subprocess, zero new runtime deps, mirrors `cerveau-lifecycle.sh`'s own pattern) via a new `composio-connection-sync.timer` (every 10 min, `OnBootSec=2min`/`RandomizedDelaySec=60`). `Config::mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant` takes a 4th parameter, `tenant_connected_toolkits`, and applies a new third scoping step, `apply_toolkit_connection_gate`, alongside the existing entity-/workspace-scoping steps — same fail-closed contract (a connection-gated server is dropped, never granted unscoped or on an inconclusive read). The resolver itself is deliberately fail-**open** on turn availability: a Postgres hiccup degrades to "no external toolkits this turn," never a rejected request — persona/identity resolution failure still hard-rejects (that boundary is unchanged), only this new, strictly-additive grant softens on infra failure | ✅ CI-green (commit `b963577b`), 21 new/updated tests across `zeroclaw-config` (12) and `zeroclaw-gateway` (4) passing, full workspace `cargo check --all-targets` clean. **Non-LLM-verified twice** (per the user's explicit "test tanpa memanggil LLM" instruction this session): once locally against a modified copy of the production config before deploy, once again against the actual post-deploy live `:3100` config.toml — both via a throwaway Rust example calling the real `mcp_servers_for_agent_and_tenant`/gate functions directly, no daemon, no webhook call, no LLM. **Live on `:3100` since 2026-08-09** — checksum-verified download (release commit `b963577b`), standard `.new`+`mv` atomic swap (rollback at `.bak-pre-0024`), `doctor` unchanged 78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors before and after the config patch, new PID, 0 restarts, health 200. `product.agent_toolkit_connections` table created and the sync timer confirmed running (`synced=0 failed=0 total=0` on first pass — expected, no real tenant rows exist yet in `product.agent_profiles` for `finance_invoice_ops`/`customer_service`, still side-by-side testing). Stripe/Zendesk `[[mcp.servers]]` entries tagged `requires_composio_toolkit = "stripe"`/`"zendesk"` respectively; the native bridge and OfficeCLI entries deliberately untouched (never gated). |

| 0033 | Postgres memory backend: real embedding wiring (`store_with_agent`/`recall`/`recall_for_agents` now actually compute and use `EmbeddingProvider` + pgvector's `<=>` for hybrid keyword+vector recall) — the `embedding` column existed via `try_enable_pgvector` but nothing had ever read or written a value into it, found while verifying `vector_enabled=true` in production | Two real CI failures, both root-caused and fixed, not dismissed as flakes: (1) a `$n::TIMESTAMPTZ` parameter-type-inference bug (fixed to `$n::TEXT::TIMESTAMPTZ`, commit `c348a31a`); (2) a genuine concurrency bug in `try_enable_pgvector` — concurrent `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` calls (CI's 4 parallel embedding tests) could race, and the DO-block's exception handler silently swallowed the resulting missing-embedding-column error, leaving `pgvector_ready=true` as a false positive (commit `57d74e80`) — reproduced deterministically on a throwaway vector-less database (2/3 failures matching CI exactly), fixed with a `CREATE EXTENSION` retry-on-conflict + a post-hoc `information_schema.columns` verification, confirmed the fix closes the race (5/5 clean) before pushing. ✅ **CI-green** (commit `57d74e80`) — full local suite (398 tests) + full Postgres integration suite all pass. **Not yet deployed to the VPS** as of this update. |

18. ~~**Add `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` to the local pricing rate sheet**~~ ✅ **DONE 2026-08-08 — root cause was different from the original framing, config-only fix, no code patch needed.** Investigation found `crates/zeroclaw-providers/src/pricing.rs` no longer has a static per-model table at all — upstream commit `2a8f0421` ("feat(cost): fill unpriced models from live gateway pricing", merged 2026-07-07, already an ancestor of patch 0022) replaced it with a live-fetch snapshot (provider's own `/models` + models.dev fallback) gated behind a per-alias `live_pricing` bool, default `false`. Checked `:3100`'s live config: **none of the 13 `[providers.models.openrouter.*]` aliases had `live_pricing` set** — so the $0.00 finding wasn't about one missing dated slug, it was that live pricing was never turned on at all, leaving *every* OpenRouter model cost-blind, not just `-0731`. Fix: backed up `config.toml` (`config.toml.bak-pre-live-pricing-20260808`), added `live_pricing = true` to all 13 aliases (7 primary + 6 fallback), `doctor` clean pre/post (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors, unchanged), restarted `zeroclaw-cerveau.service` (PID `2166833`), stable 2+ min post-restart (0 restarts, no panics/errors). **Live-verified:** a real `/webhook` call immediately after restart produced `cost_usd: 0.01632316` in `costs.jsonl` — the refresher does its first fetch synchronously before its first sleep, so no warm-up delay. Test tenant row (`pricingtest-20260808`) cleaned from `cerveau.memories`/`cerveau.agents` afterward. This is a broader, root-cause fix than the original item — it prevents the same blindness for any future model, not just the one dated slug the load test happened to hit.
19. 🟡 **PARTIALLY DONE 2026-08-08 — re-ran at 100-concurrency (5× the original 20) on `tencent-vps` (user's explicit choice — scale down and reuse the shared box rather than wait on new infra). Surfaced a real production bug and a real infra ceiling; true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent scale on disposable infra is still open. Finding 4 (the unexplained 500-error wave from this run) re-investigated and effectively closed 2026-08-09 — see the top-of-file "Last updated" entry: could not be reproduced even at loadavg 8.64 (2× the original trigger), only clean admission-queue `503`s appeared; most plausible explanation is ADR-005's admission queue (patch 0023, shipped after this original run) already prevents the condition.** Full account:

    **Setup:** rebuilt the isolated-instance harness from scratch (the 2026-08-06 one was fully torn down) — `~/.zeroclaw-cerveau-loadtest` config-dir copied from prod with only `[storage.postgres.default].schema` repointed to a fresh `cerveau_loadtest` and the webhook secret rotated (had to also clear `gateway.paired_tokens`, an `enc2:`-encrypted value tied to prod's own `.secret_key` that doesn't exist in a fresh config-dir — harmless to clear since the webhook path doesn't use pairing). New systemd unit on `:3199`, 750 synthetic `loadtest_*` tenant rows in `product.agent_profiles` (up from 500, to sustain higher concurrency across the ramp). Reused the existing `/tmp/loadgen.js` + `/tmp/loadtest-guardrail.sh` from the prior run (still on disk, untouched) at `MAX_CONCURRENCY=100`.

    **Finding 3 (real, and it affects PRODUCTION, not just this test) — `LimitNOFILE` was never set on either systemd unit, so both inherited the OS/systemd default of 1024 open files.** First attempt at 100 concurrent crash-looped the loadtest daemon almost immediately: `thread 'postgres-memory-init'/'tokio-rt-worker' panicked ... Os { code: 24, message: "Too many open files" }`, repeating every ~15s as `Restart=on-failure` kept respawning it under a new PID each time (silent to a casual `systemctl status` check — the service reports "active" throughout a restart loop). Checked whether prod `:3100` shared the same exposure before writing this off as a test-harness-only bug: **it did** — `cat /proc/<prod-pid>/limits` showed the identical soft limit of 1024, meaning production would hit the exact same crash-loop under sufficient real concurrent load, a live risk Phase 6 would eventually walk into. Fixed on **both** units via a `LimitNOFILE=65536` systemd drop-in (`/etc/systemd/system/{zeroclaw-cerveau,zeroclaw-cerveau-loadtest}.service.d/limits.conf`) — pure limit increase, nothing removed, trivially reversible (delete the drop-in). Prod restarted to apply it: new PID `2216614`, confirmed `65536/65536` in `/proc/<pid>/limits`, `doctor` unchanged (78 ok/9 warnings/0 errors), stable 90s+ with 0 restarts before proceeding. **This fix is now live on `:3100`, independent of whether the load test itself ever passes cleanly.**

    **Re-run after the fix:** clean start, 0 `network_error`s (the fd-exhaustion signature from the first attempt was fully gone) — 602/1219 requests succeeded before the run **self-aborted safely** at `elapsed=180s` when the guardrail's load-average threshold (`8.0`, i.e. 2× this box's 4 vCPUs) tripped at `8.54` and SIGTERM'd the generator, exactly as the guardrail is designed to do. This is itself a real, useful data point, not a failure of the test: **this specific 4-vCPU/7.5GB box saturates its CPU before reaching 100 concurrent** — confirms the doc's pre-existing suspicion that `tencent-vps` cannot validate the 1,000–10,000-concurrent target, now with a concrete number (~80-90 actual concurrent, reached in ~3 minutes of ramp) rather than a guess.

    **Finding 4 (real, NOT independently root-caused) — a wave of 617 HTTP 500s appeared starting around the same `elapsed=180s` mark the load average climbed past 4.** Coincides with the CPU-saturation window, so CPU starvation is the likely cause, but `journalctl` shows zero matching error/panic lines for that window — `[observability] backend = "none"` swallows error records, the same blind spot already documented in §7 ("Debugging a silent `:3100`"). Confirming this would need a foreground daemon with `backend = "verbose"`, which means another full setup/run cycle — left open rather than guessed at.

    **Metrics actually collected (honest, partial-run caveat applies throughout):**

    |---|---|---|
    | RSS peak | 674 MB (`674,028` KB, from the guardrail's full log, not just the abort-time reading) | Up from 213 MB — **~3.1× RSS for 5× target concurrency**, i.e. still sub-linear, consistent with the earlier per-request-context-size hypothesis, though this run never sustained a full 100 concurrent for long (aborted at ~180s). |
    | Disk | 1,720 KB total across 749 agents + 1,103 memories ≈ **2.3 KB/tenant** (same short-session caveat as before — not a monthly figure) | Consistent with the prior run's ~2.7 KB/tenant — no regression. |
    | Cost | **$10.36 real spend / 621 distinct tenants ≈ $0.0167/active tenant**, from `costs.jsonl` directly — **zero `$0.00` records this time** (2,939/2,939 priced) | Confirms item 18's `live_pricing` fix holds under this isolated config too, not just the one manual test call on prod. |
    | Per-tenant rate limiting | Not re-tested this run (already confirmed 2026-08-06 and unaffected by anything here) | — |

    **Cleanup:** `cerveau_loadtest` schema dropped (749 agents + 1,103 memories cascaded), all 750 `loadtest_*` rows deleted from `product.agent_profiles`, config-dir/systemd-unit/env-file/drop-in all removed, guardrail+loadgen processes confirmed dead (`ps aux | grep '[l]oadtest'` clean — note `pgrep -f <pattern>` self-matches the invoking SSH command's own text when the pattern string appears in it; the bracket-grep trick is the reliable check). Verified after teardown: prod `:3100` MainPID `2216614` unchanged, `NRestarts=0`, health `200`, `avry-postgres` healthy, disk/mem back to baseline.

    **What's still open:** the true 1,000+/10,000-concurrent run on genuinely disposable infrastructure (not `tencent-vps`, which now has a known ~80-90-concurrent CPU ceiling of its own); the Finding-4 500-wave's exact cause; a 90-day flat-disk simulation (§6, separate item).

    **2026-08-08, two follow-up repro attempts, inconclusive — deprioritized.** Rebuilt the isolated loadtest harness with `[observability] backend="verbose"` (prod's `backend="none"` was the original blind spot) specifically to catch real error/panic lines this time. Attempt 2 pushed MAX_CONCURRENCY to 180 to force the crossing; **stopped mid-run by the user on cost grounds** (real LLM calls at scale have a real $ cost, and this item is already non-blocking for Phase 6 — not worth further live-reproduction spend right now). Also surfaced one genuine operational gotcha along the way: a loadtest config-dir built via `cp -a` of the whole prod config-dir (including `data/`) keeps honoring the **original prod webhook secret** for `/webhook` auth — traced to `[channels.webhook.cerveau].secret` in `config.toml` (copied verbatim from prod), not the `CERVEAU_WEBHOOK_SECRET` env var, which the copied instance appears to ignore for this check.
